Each site's exact `LLM_MODEL` semantics were reproduced by threading `envModel` in rather than re-deriving it, deliberately NOT "fixing" the asymmetry, because the entire safety claim is that nothing moves. A stored id whose provider was later Disconnected, or which is no longer offerable, falls back silently to the cheapest without throwing; a corrupt hand-edited config degrades to defaults; prototype keys are refused (M5: a naive object index returned a **function** for `getSelectedModel("constructor")`). **The agent corrected the brief on fallback semantics and was right:** an invalid per-call override falls back to the USER'S STORED SELECTION, not to `DEFAULTS` — dropping to `DEFAULTS` would silently demote a user who deliberately paid for a better model every time any caller sent a garbage id, indistinguishable from the picker being broken, and it stays money-safe because a stored id can only be one the user explicitly POSTed, still allow-listed, whose key is still saved. Precedence documented: **per-call > `LLM_MODEL` > stored > `DEFAULTS`**. `isUpdateInProgress` was deliberately NOT added, with the distinction stated: that token guards a domain write checking for an app update, whereas this is a config mutation being protected FROM an in-flight write, which is `hasActiveWrites` via `guardConcurrent` — and `/update` already refuses while writes are active, so the pair closes from both sides. **(8) A GUARD FOUND TO BE FUNCTION-SCOPED, not file-scoped.** `test-next-provider-rows.js` extracts `renderProviderRow`/`onSaveKey`, so the binary `p.id === 'gemini' ? A : B` shape reappearing in a NEW function leaves it **61/0 green** — proven by mutation, twice, by two different agents. The orchestrator's brief had claimed it enforced "anywhere in settings.js"; it does not. The class invariant is now carried in the new function too. **(9) MUTATIONS THAT STAYED GREEN AND WERE CHASED, NOT FILED.** Forcing every Settings section OPEN — the maintainer's exact complaint — passed **200/0**, because every openness assertion drove the render function DIRECTLY and supplied its own argument, never touching the real call site; closed by extracting and EXECUTING `renderProviders`, not by adding a grep. Two client key-scoping layers could BOTH be deleted with everything green, because the fixture fed the client a payload the server had already filtered — the suite was testing the fixture. A promotion assertion passed with the rise element deleted, because every promoted model's `note` happens to repeat its own prices. An XSS check gave a FALSE POSITIVE on correctly-escaped output (`[^>]*` walks straight through `&gt;`). And a suite section pinned the words *"not wired up yet"*, so it went red the moment the endpoint landed — the guard working, and the claim was updated rather than the assertion. **Verification: 77 -> 80 offline suites, all green**; `test-chat-model.js` 637 -> **858** assertions, the Settings picker **357**, the composer **297**, persistence **200**; the allow-list independently re-attacked by the orchestrator (`__proto__`/`constructor`/`toString`/a foreign `gpt-4o`/an `AWAITING_MEASUREMENT` Opus all refused); the no-stored-selection path verified against the REAL config to resolve byte-identically to before the feature existed; frozen shipping files byte-identical; real `.curator-config.json` `sha256`-unchanged throughout; secret scan 0 hits with a positive control. **NOT in this release:** OpenRouter and local models — their Settings rows sit inert as *"not available in this build"*, which is the honest state and the slot they will fill. |
| `v3.12.0` | **The data layer for multi-model choice — where a model cannot reach a user until it has been MEASURED — plus the composer's box-in-a-box, whose cause was not the one everybody assumed.** Until now the app pinned exactly two models, `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` and `claude-haiku-4-5`, both with extensive adaptation work behind them; community members asked for stronger models for deeper context on large wikis. **(1) `OFFERABLE_MODELS` — 14 models, every one measured.** Seven Gemini (all Flash-Lite and all Flash, 2.5 through 3.7 — no Pro, by maintainer decision) and seven Anthropic (Haiku, three Sonnet, three Opus), frozen table AND frozen entries, ordered cheapest-first, each carrying `id/label/maxOutput/thinks/jsonRaw/tokenizerFactor/suitability/note` plus price fields — every value probed live against the repo's REAL `buildOutlinePrompt` on real prose, with prices verified against the live provider pages rather than a cached table. **A model is structurally unable to be offered while incomplete:** a missing price or cap makes the module REFUSE TO LOAD with a named message. `claude-opus-4-7` and `claude-opus-4-6` sit in a new `AWAITING_MEASUREMENT` map and are asserted NON-offerable — the provider gives their price and ceiling, but neither has met a real ingest prompt, and this release's premise is that a model may not be offered for a job it has not been measured doing. **(2) THE ALLOW-LIST LIVES IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE** — inside `getProviderInfo()`, the single producer of the model string both SDKs receive. Validating at a route would leave six other `generateText` callers with their own entry point, and two hand-maintained copies of a guard is what produced the v3.2.0 CRITICAL. `isOfferableModel` scans with `===` rather than an object lookup, so `__proto__`, `constructor` and `toString` are refused — the v3.0.9 prototype-key bug, pre-empted. `getProviderInfo`'s return shape is deliberately UNCHANGED (`{provider, model}`); it is destructured at ~15 sites. **(3) THE PROMOTIONAL-PRICE TRAP, AND THE MUTATION PROVING THE OBVIOUS TEST CANNOT CATCH IT.** `gemini-3.6-flash` and `gemini-3.7-flash` are $0.75/$3.75 only until 2026-12-31, then DOUBLE to $1.50/$7.50 — the v3.6.0 trap inverted (that release nearly shipped an inverted cost ordering by trusting a cached table claiming a rise that had been cancelled; this is a discount that will genuinely end). Handled BOTH ways: `MODEL_PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` holds the STANDARD price, a separate `PROMOTIONAL_PRICES` holds the discount with an inclusive UTC expiry, and `input`/`output` are getters resolving what is billed NOW. **`M6c` is the finding: encoding BOTH promos as permanent left every cheapest-first assertion GREEN**, because the array order survives the doubling — an ordering test is structurally incapable of catching this. Only the strict-dearer assertion (standard greater than promo on both axes) catches it: green over a wrong number, this repo's named shape. Both boundary sides are asserted TODAY, because a guard that can only fire in January is a comment. Every failure direction — wrong clock, dropped promo record, typo'd id — resolves to the HIGHER price, matching v3.9.0's rule that the fail-safe direction on money is to warn. **(4) TWO LISTS WITH DIFFERENT RULES, made an invariant.** `FALLBACK_CHAINS` picks FOR the user, silently, on the day their default died — so a measured-bad model must never be in it. `OFFERABLE_MODELS` is the user choosing deliberately with the reason on screen. The intersection of DOMINATED and FALLBACK_CHAINS must be EMPTY while DOMINATED may be a subset of OFFERABLE, and v3.10.1's bespoke pair of assertions naming one id folds into a class invariant over `DOMINATED_MODELS`. `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` is therefore pickable with its warning but unreachable automatically. A SECOND dominated model fell out of the data unprompted: `claude-opus-4-5`, identical $5/$25 to `claude-opus-5` and behind it on all three measured axes. **(5) THREE REFUSALS TO OVER-CLAIM, the most valuable part.** `claude-opus-4-8` MEETS the domination definition but only on outline coverage from a small sample, so it is marked `caution` with the number rather than `dominated` — *"an over-claimed domination is worth less than an honest one"*. `claude-sonnet-4-5` genuinely IS dominated by same-priced `claude-sonnet-4-6` (64k vs 128k ceiling, fenced vs raw JSON, 15-16 vs 17 pages) and is NOT listed only because it is a live chain rung and the chains were frozen here — recorded in the doc as the rung to re-examine, not quietly dropped. And `tokenizerFactor` (measured **1.329x** on real Curator prose, so Opus 5 at $5/Mtok is effectively **~$6.65 against a Haiku baseline** — 6.6x, not the 5x the headline implies) is applied to INPUT ONLY, because that is where it was measured. **(6) THE API SURFACE, added without breaking `/old`.** `GET /api/config/api-keys` gains `offerable`, gated the same CONFIG-SCOPED way as `hasGeminiKey`/`hasAnthropicKey` (v3.0.13's rule — saved Settings keys, never `.env`), so a Disconnected provider is not pickable. Two traps guarded BY NAME: `models` must stay a provider-to-STRING map, because `/old` does `escHtml(models[p])` and `escHtml` begins `String(str)`, so an object renders the literal text `[object Object]`; and `hasGeminiKey`/`hasAnthropicKey` must survive, because their absence makes the first-run check believe no key is configured and re-fires the 4-step onboarding overlay — no Escape, no backdrop close, no X, no Skip on step 1 — on every load for an already-configured user. The route imports llm.js as a NAMESPACE, not a named import: a missing named export throws `SyntaxError` at module load and would take down the whole route file, whereas `llmModule.OFFERABLE_MODELS` degrades to `undefined`. **(7) THE COMPOSER'S BOX-IN-A-BOX — and the diagnosis was wrong.** Reported by the maintainer with screenshots. v3.0.12 fixed the same SYMPTOM in the old app via a `textarea:focus` rule, and that was assumed to be the cause here. Measured in a real browser, it was not: `tokens/base.css:24` applies `box-shadow: var(--ring-focus)` to **`:focus-visible` globally**, on every focusable element in the app. `chat.css` had `.chat-input:focus { outline: none }` but never addressed `box-shadow` and never touched `:focus-visible` — and **in Chromium `:focus-visible` matches on a CLICK into a text field**, not only on Tab, so merely clicking the composer painted a 3px ring nested inside `.chat-composer`'s own `:focus-within` border. Fixed with `.chat-input:focus, .chat-input:focus-visible { outline: none; box-shadow: none; }`; verified by real keyboard Tab in both themes with the dropdown and Send bounding rects byte-identical and auto-grow intact. **Recorded, not fixed, and deliberately not smuggled into a border fix:** the remaining focus affordance — `.chat-composer:focus-within` changing border colour — measures **~1.65:1 (dark) / ~1.58:1 (light)** against its own background, below WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 for UI-component indicators. It is PRE-EXISTING and SYSTEMIC: `--accent-border` at that alpha is the same token behind every highlighted-container state in `/next` (active rail item, selected states in Domains, Settings, Ingest), so strengthening it in chat alone would make the composer inconsistent with the whole app and strengthening it globally is a design-system decision. It affects only keyboard users, who cannot route around it. **No CSS test guard was added, and the reason is recorded rather than papered over:** the repo's CSS suites are pure Node text analysis with no headless-browser dependency, and a hand-rolled specificity calculator adjudicating a cross-file cascade would be the decorative-guard shape this project keeps hitting; it was verified behaviourally in a visible browser instead. **Two agents found defects in their OWN tests before reporting:** an unguarded `promo.price` dereference that threw a `TypeError` and aborted the file, hiding every later assertion, and the route suite's key-absence check proven non-vacuous by planting a leak. **Verification: 76 to 77 offline suites, all green**; `test-chat-model.js` **129 to 637 assertions**; the allow-list independently re-attacked by the orchestrator (`__proto__`/`constructor`/`toString`/a foreign `gpt-4o`/an awaiting-measurement Opus all refused, real ids allowed); frozen shipping files byte-identical; secret scan 0 hits with a positive control proving the scanner detects. **NOT shipped in this release, stated plainly: there is still NO user-facing picker.** This is the data layer. The Settings control and the chat composer's model dropdown come next, and OpenRouter and local models after that. |
| `v3.11.0` | **The app froze for 15 seconds behind one button, and the "nothing happens then suddenly something happens" complaint turned out to be the opposite of what everyone assumed.** **(1) THE P0 — one click froze the ENTIRE app.** `GET /api/health/:domain/semantic-dupes/estimate` runs `findSemanticCandidatePairs`, a synchronous O(N^2) sweep. Measured through the real HTTP layer on a 3,288-page copy of a real domain: while it ran, a `GET /api/version` that normally answers in **0.7 ms took 14,508 ms**, and the server served **13** requests in 14.5 s. Rail, badges, every view, every other user action — dead. v3.2.0 removed this scan from automatic view ENTRY; it was never made non-blocking, so the freeze stayed one deliberate click away ("Find duplicate pages"). Fixed by yielding the loop (`setImmediate`) on a **remaining-budget** counter rather than a per-outer-iteration one — a per-iteration budget never fires at all on any domain smaller than the chunk, because the tail of the triangle is thousands of short iterations. After: worst concurrent latency **52 ms**, **1,451** requests served, longest synchronous block **15,018.7 ms -> 21.1 ms**, zero slices over 50 ms. **Output identity was the acceptance criterion, not responsiveness** — this feeds a merge that DELETES a page and rewrites every link to it, so a chunking bug that silently changed which pairs are found would be far worse than the freeze: the pair list is `sha256`-identical and the suite pins it against a golden computed **UNCAPPED over all 6,493 pairs**, so the ranking's long tail is covered and not just the shipped top 500. Chunk size **2,000** was chosen by a measured sweep (32k/16k/8k/4k/2k/1k), and the agent's own a-priori estimate was **3x wrong** because it ignored GC on ~10.8 M allocations. Wall clock is unchanged in isolation; it only *appears* higher under load because the server is now actually serving 1,451 concurrent requests instead of stonewalling them. **Two corrections to the brief, both load-bearing:** the function was ALREADY `async` and every caller already awaited it (zero call sites needed changing), and the other health endpoints do **NOT** block — `/api/health/:domain` (646 ms), broken-links (639 ms) and orphans (646 ms) are I/O-bound over thousands of async reads and yield naturally, worst concurrent latency 31-38 ms under ~7,000 probes. There is no second workstream. **(2) THE LOADING COMPLAINT WAS BACKWARDS.** The maintainer reported "you load some data, nothing happens, and then all of a sudden something happens". Measured, every view-entry placeholder in the shell lives on screen for **1.3-12 ms** — they are not slow loads, they are **sub-frame flashes that collapse the column and re-expand it**. Adding transitions to them makes the app WORSE; deleting them makes it better. The real staircase is Domains: **929 px of movement across 4 jumps over 1.76 s**, because the health scan lands at 733 ms and the cost estimates at 1,757 ms with no transition at all. Fixed with a **delay-gated** loader — nothing shown under **200 ms**, and once shown held a minimum **400 ms** so it cannot strobe — implemented as a pure decision core (`shouldShowLoader` / `settleDelayMs`) with injected timers, so the truth table is driven exhaustively offline without sleeping. **The gate IS the device adaptivity, and that was a deliberate refusal:** the maintainer asked for adaptation to "the resources of the computer", and measurement said no — under 20x CPU throttling the 719 ms health scan moved only 773 -> 1,057 ms (it is I/O-bound) while pure render moved 17 -> 105 ms, so a CPU- or memory-derived signal would mispredict the one wait that matters. The gate measures the real wait instead of predicting it: one constant, no feature detection, nothing to branch-test. Placeholder lifetimes at throttle x1/x6/x20 were 16.8/26.6/105 ms — under 200 ms even at 20x, so one constant covers the whole device range. **Measured result: warm re-entry total movement 902 px -> 76 px (-92%).** Cold entry went 4 jumps -> 3, and that smaller-than-hoped win is reported honestly: deleting the placeholder removes the TEXT but not a jump, because the chrome-only frame is itself an intermediate height; cold only improved because a redundant double-render 2.3 ms apart was folded. **(3) TWO FALSE STATES.** Chat rendered *"Chat needs at least one domain to talk to"* on cold first load — a factually wrong claim, and the first frame a brand-new user ever sees; now gated behind a `booted` flag set in a `finally`. Domains threw away a cached health report on re-entry so it VANISHED and returned 750 ms later (-501 px then +501 px); now stale-while-revalidate, **KEEP on same-domain re-entry, CLEAR on a domain switch**, two independent layers, because showing domain A's health under domain B is a correctness bug strictly worse than the flicker. **THE SEMANTIC-MERGE GATE CAUGHT A REGRESSION IN FLIGHT, and the orchestrator's diagnosis of it was WRONG.** `test-next-semantic-gate.js` went red on *"after a DOMAIN SWITCH the whole scan object is gone"*. The orchestrator assumed the stale-while-revalidate was keeping state across the switch. It was not: that suite lifts a **hardcoded list** of functions out of `domains.js` and executes them, the new module-level helper was not in the list, and `loadHealth` therefore threw `ReferenceError` **mid-clear** — the scan object survived because the clearing function died partway. The clearing logic was never touched. Severity stands (the guard on a page-DELETING path was dead), but the mechanism is a **blind spot in the guard's own `FNS` list**, whose comment already records this happening once before. Fixed by the existing `keepSemanticScan` precedent — the caller evaluates the predicate and passes a boolean, so the callee references no new identifier — with the default being **CLEAR**, so any call site that has not thought about it takes the safe path. State lifetime was then verified **mechanically, not by reading**: a diff of every `state.X = null|{}|[]|false` site reports `REMOVED: []` and `ADDED: ['healthSlug','healthStale']`. Nothing else survives a transition it did not survive before. **(4) TWO DEFECTS THE AGENT FOUND IN ITS OWN WORK, both recorded rather than quietly fixed.** A mutation reinstating **the exact bug being fixed** passed **206/0** — the suite tested the predicate and the renderer but never `loadHealth`'s reset; closed by executing the real function. And **the suite's exit code was INVERTED** (`exit(failed === 0 ? 1*0 + (failed?1:0) : 0)` exits **0 on failure**) — a suite that cannot fail the build is invisible to the runner and to CI, the v3.3.0 shape in a new form. Both fixed; the orchestrator independently re-verified the exit code by planting a failure (exit 1) and restoring (`shasum`-identical) — and its FIRST attempt at that check appended the assertion AFTER `process.exit()`, where it never ran, which is why a mutation must be read back off disk before its result is trusted. **NOT ENFORCED, named rather than implied away:** the min-visible clamp is delay-only in three places (`settings.js`'s four section loaders, `mcp-wizard.js`'s `loadAll`, `shared-brain-wizard.js`'s `populateDomains`) because each commits state from several branches — all measured at 2-3 ms so the loader never appears there; the placeholder walk scans single-quoted, line-scoped literals (a split call reads as ungated, failing SAFE); timer hygiene is source-scanned, not executed; and no offline assertion measures real rendering, because a `scrollHeight` delta is not reproducible in Node and `PerformanceObserver` reports **ZERO** layout shifts for this defect — the movement happens inside a scroll container via `innerHTML` replacement, which CLS does not score, so **a CLS assertion was deliberately not written because it could never go red**. **Deliberately NOT done:** `domains.js`'s "Scanning…" is left ungated at a measured **654 ms** — it is an honest indicator on the one wait users actually notice, and delaying it would add 200 ms of nothing; the reader overlay's dead `--t-enter` is still deferred; and no skeletons were added anywhere, because exactly one wait in the app earns one and that is its own follow-up. **Verification: 74 -> 76 offline suites, all green** (`test-semantic-scan-yield.js` 37, `test-next-loading-gate.js` 218); measured in a VISIBLE Playwright tab across 7 views x 2 themes with 0 console errors, 0 horizontal overflow and 0 stuck loaders; frozen shipping files `shasum`-identical; secret scan 0 hits with a positive control proving the scanner detects. |
| `v3.10.1` | **Two hazards disarmed BEFORE the release that would arm them — and the discovery that nothing in the suite could tell a live, correctly-priced model from a measurably broken one.** Both fixes are latent today; both become live the moment a model/provider picker ships, which is the next release. **(1) THE FALLBACK CHAIN CARRIED A DOMINATED RUNG.** Measured live this session against the repo's REAL `buildOutlinePrompt` (exported via `__testing`) on real prose: `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` produced JSON that **NEITHER `JSON.parse` NOR `jsonrepair` can fix in 2 of 9 runs** — the model dropped an object key, emitting `{ "concepts/knowledge-graph.md", "summary": … }`, unrecoverable because repair would have to invent `"path":`. `finishReason` was **STOP**, so it is not truncation. In production that fires ingest's Phase-1 stricter-retry ladder **~22% of the time** — silent extra spend and latency on a rung the user never chose. It was `FALLBACK_CHAINS.gemini[1]`, and it is **strictly dominated** by `gemini-2.5-flash`: identical price ($0.30/$2.50), 17-19 planned pages against 12-16, and no JSON failures. Removed — a REMOVAL, not a reorder, so the cheapest-first invariant holds trivially ($0.25 -> $0.30) and its own assertion stays green. Its price entry went with it, because the table's stated contract is "ids we actually ship" and the length-equality assertion enforces exactly that. **THE REAL FINDING IS WHAT THE SUITE COULD NOT SEE.** Proved, not asserted: the ORIGINAL unmodified `test-chat-model.js` was run against an `llm.js` with the rung re-added and correctly priced — **131/131 green, zero failures**. Every existing invariant encodes **price and liveness, never quality**: price coverage, length equality, cheapest-first ordering, the `RETIRED` dead-id list and `compareModelCost` are all satisfied by a model that is alive, fairly priced and empirically worse than its same-priced neighbour. Closed with a named regression assertion that distinguishes **dominated** from **dead**, and the honest limit is recorded rather than implied away: this guards ONE measured case, and "worse JSON reliability than a same-priced sibling" is not a computable invariant — it needs a live probe — so the durable shape is a curated `DOMINATED` list beside `RETIRED`, carrying its reasoning. **(2) A CREDENTIAL SCREEN THAT WOULD HAVE WRITTEN THE WRONG PROVIDER'S KEY.** `views/settings.js` selected credential state with binary ternaries — `p.id === 'gemini' ? A : B` — which have no third arm, so ANY third provider row fell through to the ANTHROPIC branch. The render half showed **Anthropic's masked key and "configured"/"active" state next to another provider's name**. The save half was worse, because it WRITES: `onSaveKey` built `provider === 'gemini' ? { geminiApiKey } : { anthropicApiKey }`, so a third provider's key would have been POSTed as `anthropicApiKey` and **silently overwritten the user's real Anthropic credential with a key for a different service** — undetectable until that other service began failing. Both were latent ONLY because `openai` and `local` carry `available: false` and `renderProviderRow` early-returns; the picker un-gates exactly that. Both now key off a lookup by provider id. The render half fails SAFE (an unknown id reads as "no key" rather than borrowing someone else's); the save half **REFUSES outright** and surfaces an error through the existing `catch`, because under-saving is recoverable while writing into the wrong slot may go unnoticed. **The save half was found only because the agent that fixed the render half reported it rather than stopping at its brief** — fixing the render alone would have been this repo's signature shape, the reported case closed while the purpose stayed broken. A whole-file sweep of every two-armed provider conditional (~50 ternaries read) found no third instance; `providerLabel()` echoes an unknown id back or returns null and never substitutes another provider's identity, and `onDisconnect`/`onSetActive` post the id verbatim with no client-side branching, so both are structurally immune. **Guarded by `scripts/test-next-provider-rows.js` (61 assertions)** — a CLASS invariant enumerated mechanically from the real `PROVIDER_ROWS`, never a hardcoded id list. It drives the REAL `renderProviderRow` and the REAL async `onSaveKey`, extracted whole by brace-matching and executed with a fetch spy, so the assertion observes what would actually go over the wire; a positive-control self-check proves the harness can see a real call before the unknown-id cases are trusted. Mutations: restoring either ternary goes RED naming the provider, and a synthetic third `available: true` row stays GREEN with the fix and RED without it — which is what proves it is a class invariant rather than two pinned lines. **The suite's own first draft was defective and the agent said so:** an unguarded `.includes()` on a null error crashed the run instead of failing, so the mutation was red for the wrong reason; fixed, re-run, 8 clean behavioural failures. **Also recorded, not fixed:** `claude-haiku-4-5` — the pinned Anthropic default — wraps its ingest outline in ```json fences **3/3**, so raw `JSON.parse` fails on **100%** of responses and only `jsonrepair` recovers them (same for sonnet-4-5 and opus-4-5, while everything 4.6-and-later returns bare JSON and parses raw 3/3). Benign because `parseJSON` is deliberately lenient, but every Anthropic ingest on the default depends on the repair path. The note lives at `DEFAULTS.anthropic`, not in a doc, because changing prompt content is a separate release with its own verification — the v3.0.16 precedent, where a prompt change was implemented, measured, and deliberately reverted. **Also corrected: a false claim v3.10.0 shipped one release earlier.** Its comments at `shell.css:208` and `app.js:461` both stated that "domains.js and chat.js each paint a 'Loading…' placeholder". A loading-state audit measured the tree and found `chat.js` contains **ZERO** occurrences of the string — it paints a different first frame. The substantive point those comments exist to defend (both views call `setMain` TWICE per entry, so the enter animation must live on the stable container) is unaffected and still true; only the supporting detail was wrong, and it would have sent the next reader hunting for a placeholder that does not exist. Both now name what was verified and when. **Verification: 73 -> 74 offline suites, all green**; both headline guards independently re-attacked by the orchestrator with `shasum`-identical restores; `docs/model-lifecycle.md` corrected in the same release (chain, price table, and a stale "~10 ids" count that was really 7). |
| `v3.10.0` | **The app stopped feeling flat when you click through it — and the fix is an ADOPTION gap, not a design gap.** The maintainer's report was that moving section to section "does not bring any satisfaction… now it's really flat". Measured rather than characterised: [tokens/motion.css](src/public/next/tokens/motion.css) already carries the complete design-system scale (80/120/180/240/400ms on one `--ease-out` curve, plus `--t-state`/`--t-enter`/`--t-layout`) and IS linked ([index.html](src/public/next/index.html) line 31) — but of **1,825** `var()` references in the shell only ~56 are motion: `--t-state` **37**, `--t-enter` **3**, `--t-layout` **2**, and `--dur-slower`/`--ease-in-out`/`--ease-linear` **ZERO**. Confirmed in a live browser: `.rail-btn` transitions at 0.12s while `#view-root`, `.main-inner` AND `.sidebar-inner` all computed `animation: none`. **So the 40px button animated and the entire content column hard-cut** — the motion was wired to the small thing and not the big one. That mismatch IS the flatness. Fixed with a short opacity + slide on the two STABLE containers, fired ONCE per navigation from `navigate()`. **Three decisions carry the whole change, and each had a real defect on the other side.** **(1) `#view-root`, NOT `#main`.** A CSS `transform` makes an element the containing block for every `position: fixed` DESCENDANT; `#main` contains `#reader-root`, whose `.reader-scrim` is deliberately viewport-anchored. Measured: applying the same transform to `#main` moved a fixed probe **left 0 -> 340px**. A prior author had already left the warning in `shell.css` (*"if `.main` ever gains one, this needs to move to a non-transformed ancestor"*); it was found and honoured. `#view-root` is a SIBLING of `#reader-root`, and every other fixed surface in /next is `document.body.appendChild`ed. **(2) The STABLE containers, not `.main-inner`.** `setMain` replaces `#view-root`'s child on every call and **the two most-used views call it TWICE per entry** — `domains.js:1222` and `chat.js` each paint a "Loading…" placeholder then replace it. Animating the inner element would fade in the placeholder, then fade in the content ~100ms later: the naive version of this change makes Domains and Chat feel WORSE. Proven with an `animationstart` counter against a `MutationObserver` control: domains **3** child writes, **1** animation start. **(3) translateX, not translateY — the acceptance criterion earned its place.** `.main` is `overflow-y: auto`, so a downward translate extends the scroll box: padded to the exact boundary (892/892), `translateY(8px)` drove `scrollHeight` **892 -> 900 sustained**, a real scrollbar flash; `translateX(8px)` measured **892 every frame** and is structurally immune because `.main` is `overflow-x: hidden`. No stagger: both start together and the sidebar is shorter (120ms) and shallower (5px) so it settles first; a delay only makes main feel slower to arrive. The JS is **purely additive at ONE call site** immediately after `renderRailActive()` — nothing reordered — and touches no mount token, teardown, view state or persisted key, so it cannot reopen the v3.2.0 stale-mount class. It is null-safe per element, because an unguarded dereference at module scope in that file once shipped a blank page to every user. **Verified in a VISIBLE browser tab, and the reason is a finding:** the agent's first cut measured as stuck at `opacity 0` with zero `animationstart` events — `document.timeline.currentTime` is frozen at 0 in a permanently-offscreen document, so an animation sits in its active phase forever. It then **corrected its own causal diagnosis** (removing `animation-fill-mode: backwards` does NOT fix that; an animation at `currentTime 0` applies the from-state regardless of fill mode) and rewrote the comment rather than leave a wrong claim standing. Anyone verifying shell motion here must use a visible tab. Measured across 9 navigations: correct keyframe and duration on both containers, 8/8 starts, **0 console errors**, both themes x 3 views with no horizontal overflow, reader scrim rect **byte-identical** with the animation applied, and `prefers-reduced-motion` emulation yielding `animationName: none` and **0** starts. Guarded by `scripts/test-next-view-enter-motion.js` (45 assertions) pinning the three decisions as behaviour: **13 mutations RED**, including targeting `#main`, targeting `.main-inner`, dropping the reflow, dropping the null guard, moving the call into `setMain()`, and a one-character class-name drift between the CSS and the JS. An independent partial regression (dropping only the sidebar target) was caught by **5** assertions with a `shasum`-identical restore. Coverage is non-overlapping with v3.9.2's guard: hardcoding the duration while keeping the reduce escape leaves `test-next-reduced-motion.js` **51/0 green** and turns this one red. **Verification: 72 -> 73 offline suites, all green.** **Known, recorded not hidden:** the reader overlay's three `--t-enter` declarations remain DEAD CSS — `renderReader()` re-paints the whole overlay and the markup carries `.open` from the first paint, so there is no prior computed style to interpolate from and the entry transition has never once fired. It is deliberately NOT fixed here: that function re-runs on every content update while the overlay is open (loading -> loaded, backlink navigation), so a naive enter animation would replay mid-read. It needs its own change with its own proof. |
| `v3.9.2` | **An accessibility defect on the one path where it is invisible to everyone who does not need it — and a guard for it that was decorative until it was attacked.** `tokens/motion.css:22` zeroes the five `--dur-*` custom properties under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. A user who has asked their operating system to stop motion still got both. `0.16s` was also **off-scale** — the system's ladder is 80/120/180/240/400ms, so 160ms was a fourth, undocumented duration; the rules broke the token system twice over. Both now read `var(--dur-mid) var(--ease-out)` (180ms is what `motion.css`'s own header prescribes for "things that enter or leave"), plus an explicit `animation: none` under reduce — two layers deliberately, because a 0ms animation still runs one cycle and the explicit form states the intent. The house already contained both correct precedents: [views/chat.css](src/public/next/views/chat.css)'s `.chat-spinner` DISABLES, while `shared/progress-ring.css` deliberately SUBSTITUTES a 2.6s opacity breath because liveness is doctrine there and must not be removed along with the motion. The failure shape, occurring inside the fix for an instance of the failure shape. Corrected so a selector is covered **only if the reduce rule ITSELF sets `animation` or `animation-duration`** — which keeps both real exemptions (disable AND substitute) while rejecting the probe. Longhand-only overrides (`animation-play-state`, `animation-name`, …) deliberately do NOT count: none alone guarantees the duration is neutralised, so they fail in the SAFE direction. Re-attacked independently afterwards on three shapes — unrelated-property reduce, a reduce block covering a DIFFERENT selector, and longhand-only — all three RED for a behavioural reason, clean tree still green. Enumerated mechanically, exactly THREE handlers delete a page — and **only one of the three repointed links**. `hyphenVariants` is in `AUTO_FIXABLE` *and* in `fixAllSafe`, so the button labelled *"structural repairs run locally and free"* reached it. **The orchestrator reasoned that `fixCrossFolderDupe` was safe because "the slug does not change" — measurement refuted it two ways:** folder-prefixed links dangle even when the slug is stable, AND the slug genuinely can change, because `crossFolderDupes` pairs on the **hyphen-normalised** key (`concepts/e-mail` → `entities/email`). Reading the handler supports the wrong answer; driving it does not. Fixed with ONE shared `repointInboundLinks` called by all three — `fixSemanticDuplicate`'s inline version **deleted, not copied** — plus a class guard that brace-matches the enclosing function of **every** delete call and requires a repoint at an earlier offset. Ordering is load-bearing and pinned: the merged body must be written BEFORE the repoint, or the duplicate's own self-references are reinstated as dangling. **(2) The audit found a defect in the safety dialog this release ADDED to protect that very operation.** The new confirm computed `count = issues.length`, but one `hyphenVariants` issue is a **GROUP**: it announced *"1 page will be deleted"* and deleted **2** (6 pages → 4, live). Under-report is `groupSize − 2`, **unbounded**. A destructive confirm that understates the damage is worse than none. **(3) THE CUTOVER TRAP THAT WOULD HAVE SILENTLY DEFEATED THE WHOLE RELEASE:** `express.static` defaults to `index: 'index.html'`, so **`/` was answered by the static mount and never reached the catch-all**. Flipping the catch-all alone would have left the OLD app at `/` with every guard reporting success. Measured at baseline, closed with `{ index: false }` — which also puts `/` behind the DNS-rebinding Host guard **for the first time** (it previously returned 200 to `Host: evil.com`). **(4) A 19th asset ref, built in JS, that no guard could see.** The orchestrator verified "all 18 `/next` asset refs are root-absolute" by grepping `index.html` — the same file, and therefore the same blind spot, as `test-next-asset-paths.js`. `app.js`'s rail brand mark was bare-relative: correct at `/next/` by accident, and at `/` resolving to `/assets/…`, which the SPA catch-all answers with **HTML at 200** — a broken image with no 404 to notice. **(5) The one-time notice fired for users who install AFTER cutover — forever.** `hasKey && hasDomain && hasPages` has no provenance component, so every new user's second page load told them an interface they had never seen had changed. **The orchestrator's proposed fix was WRONG and the agent refused it:** keying on a "shipping-only" localStorage key does not work, because **`/next` writes all three of them** (`chat.js`, `domains.js`). The orchestrator had verified those keys earlier — but verified that `/next` READS them, then reused that finding for a question about WRITES. Same keys, different property. Replaced with a provenance record decided **once**, on the reasoning that an existing user has key+domain+pages on their FIRST `/next` load while a new user can only acquire them BY USING `/next`. **(6) Cost honesty, the release's other theme.** A cancelled batch item's spend was never counted — the tally reported the figure from *before* that item started (~35–40% under) while `spendIsEstimated: false` asserted it was measured. Now the real partial spend is charged AND flagged: the in-flight call at abort is unmeasurable, so the number is a measured **lower bound**, and reporting it as exact "would repeat the defect in a smaller font". `spendIsEstimated` was then found to carry **two contradictory meanings** — an estimate share that can read ~50% HIGH, and a measured floor — each rendered as the other; split into two flags with estimated winning when both. A new `shared/format-usd.js` (imported, never copied) means no non-zero cost renders as `$0.0000`. **(7) A mutation that stayed GREEN and was chased rather than filed as coverage:** deleting `spendIsLowerBound` from `toWire`'s allow-list left **154/0 and 343/0** — flag computed, renderer reading, qualifier silently never shown. v3.6.1 finding 5's dead-data shape exactly. Closed with a **class** invariant enumerating every `job.spend*` flag and requiring each in `toWire`. **(8) SIX OF SEVEN guards in `splitWikiRef` were decorative** — replacing any with `if (false)` left the suite at 89/0, and the seventh was "covered" only by a **crash**, a red for the wrong reason. All seven now RED behaviourally; covering them exposed a real latent bug (a 3-segment ref splitting to the WRONG page). **(9) Honest labelling over false comfort:** `test-cutover.js` **never issues an HTTP request** — it validates the cutover by reading source text. It caught all three reverts thrown at it, so it is not decoration, but its guarantee is "these strings are present", not "the server serves the right shell"; the header now names that gap and the three regressions that would slip. **(10) The claim that the notice and the first-run panel are mutually exclusive is an IMPLICATION, not a partition** — Settings → "Show setup guide" bypasses the predicate; harmless as built (panel sits below the bar, 0 controls covered) and corrected rather than left absolute. **Verification: 59 → 61 offline suites, all green**; **two full live end-to-end passes** against copies of the maintainer's real documents on real Gemini AND Anthropic (multi-phase ingest, batch queue, cancel stopping at Phase-2 batch 9 of 11, chat with rendered markdown, Length and Model selectors, Compile, domain lifecycle, wiki browse, Health incl. the destructive merges, Settings, MCP wizard); **one adversarial audit that attacked the repoint twelve link shapes and could not defeat it** (prefix-slug, regex metacharacters, non-ASCII, fenced code, frontmatter all correct); every headline guard mutation-proven RED for a behavioural reason with `shasum`-verified restores. **Security housekeeping:** ten stale `.curator-config.json` files holding live API keys, and one `.sync-config.json` holding a real GitHub PAT, were found in `/tmp` from earlier sessions and deleted. **(13) THE PARITY SWEEP - cutover was NOT ready, and nothing that found the gaps was looking for them.** After the maintainer drove the build by hand, five regressions surfaced SIDEWAYS: a README pass found raw-source retrieval missing; a user-guide rewrite found the boot-recovery panel telling users to escape to `/`, which post-cutover IS the broken shell - a loop - and the rail's sync badge hardcoded to `0`. Five accidental finds means the true count is unknown, so a MECHANICAL sweep was run: every control and every `fetch` in both frontends, diffed, plus every backend route with a consumer in one and none in the other. That last axis is what a UI diff misses and is this project's named **dead-data** shape. Result: **3 BLOCKS CUTOVER, 12 SHOULD-FIX, 5 inert controls.** All three blockers were **capabilities users would LOSE** - exactly what the v3.7.0 parity release exists to forbid - and none was among the six it closed, because it audited the wizard workstream. **(a) Raw-source retrieval, the whole of v3.5.0, zero references.** The reader already FETCHED `page.frontmatter` and read only `.tags`; `frontmatter.source`, the field the feature hangs on, arrived and was discarded. Restored in the SHELL reader so both entry points inherit ONE implementation; the external-URL case stays **inert text**, proven behaviourally (exactly ONE request, to our own endpoint, declared host never contacted) - fetching an LLM-authored, sync-delivered string would be an SSRF primitive. **(b) The fallback-cost banner had no surface at all** - `.fallback` and `activeModel` both had **0 readers**, so a provider retirement silently billing 2.5x/3.75x more was invisible everywhere. Restored, and deliberately STRICTER than shipping: an unrecognised tier resolves to `unknown`, never `similar`, because `costlier:false` cannot distinguish parity from ignorance and the fail-safe direction on money is to warn. **(c) A Shared Brain admin could no longer add a member** - `generate-invite` existed only in the first-time wizard, so onboarding someone meant tearing down and re-creating the brain, re-introducing the exact defect v3.0.5 shipped to fix. The restored derivation is verified **BYTE-FOR-BYTE against the route's real `encodeInviteToken`**, extracted and executed, with negative controls proving the equality is not vacuous. **(14) A CLASS, not three bugs: decisions correct while `/next` was a PREVIEW, silently wrong once it became primary.** The title said "Next (preview)"; the Updates button refused, calling itself a preview shell; the recovery panel's escape hatch pointed at itself; `shared.js` told users "this preview shell doesn't handle...". Each had a comment explaining sound reasoning - the reasoning had simply expired. Swept the whole tree; a class invariant now fails on that vocabulary in any user-facing string. **(15) THE UPDATE BUTTON WOULD HAVE ROLLED THE CHECKOUT BACKWARDS.** The route computes `updateAvailable = latest !== current || commitsDiffer`, so a local build AHEAD of the remote reported *"Update available: v3.9.0 -> v3.8.0"* with an install button running `git reset --hard origin/main`, discarding the newer commit. Only its being inert prevented it. Now a `local-ahead` branch renders NO install button. **(16) In-design dialogs, built so the dangerous mistake is INEXPRESSIBLE.** `window.confirm` blocks; a custom dialog does not, so `const ok = confirm(...)` without `await` makes `ok` a truthy Promise and the DELETE fires on Cancel - demonstrated by mutation, `got 1, expected 0`. The module exports **nothing that resolves to a decision**: the destructive work is handed in and referenced only inside the confirm handler. **(17) The two-layer progress ring**, ported from the private design bundle (never copied into this public repo). Its rule - *never advance the outer ring to look busy* - is this project's honesty doctrine as a component, and it is the better answer to the v3.0.17 "the app is hung" report: Planning is ONE LLM call with no sub-progress, so its segment now **stays empty while it runs** while the orbit carries liveness. Verified live: outer ring byte-identical across 8 samples over 2.4s while the orbit moved. Adopting it found Health **already streaming `{processed,total}` that `/next` threw away** - the third dead-data instance of the release - and the ring's own suite caught an honesty bug in itself: `ringValueFromCounts(null, 10)` returned `0` because `Number(null)` is `0`, turning "the server has told us nothing" into "nothing has been done yet". **(18) Housekeeping with teeth:** the Settings icon rendered a SUN because its gear path carried `opacity="0"` and `icon()` fell back to a REAL glyph for unknown names (now an unmistakable placeholder, plus an invariant that every `VIEW_META` icon exists); the favicon used light ink on a light tab; ten stale configs holding live API keys and one holding a real GitHub PAT were deleted from `/tmp`. **Verification: 61 -> 68 offline suites, all green.** **Known, recorded not hidden:** the AI broken-link fixer retargets `[[claude-sonnet-4.5]]` → `[[claude-sonnet-3.5]]` (Jaccard 0.6 on `{claude,sonnet,4,5}` vs `{claude,sonnet,3,5}`) — factual corruption in an AI-model wiki, deferred because the lexical gate needs care, not a hasty edit; heading-anchored `[[x#h]]` links are invisible to BOTH the scanner and the repoint, so a merge leaves them dangling with no Health signal (pre-existing); non-ASCII hyphen variants are refused safely but reappear on every scan unexplained; any string is accepted as an API key; renaming into a collision produces two identically-named domains; reader wikilinks are inert; headings render as `div`, so there is no screen-reader outline; and the shipping onboarding wizard's trap survives at `/old` for the escape-hatch window. |
| `v3.8.0` | **First-run guidance for `/next`, the last parity gap closed, a CI flake diagnosed to its mechanism — and a FALSE RETRACTION of a real security finding, caused by the orchestrator breaking his own tree freeze.** Shipping frontend **byte-untouched** (empty diff on `app.js`/`index.html`/`styles.css`/`markdown.js`, verified every round). **(1) Onboarding is NOT a ported modal.** `HANDOFF §0d` scoped a port of the shipping 4-step wizard; **R7 (Tali, 2026-08-23) says the opposite** — ARCHITECTURE.md "explicitly rejects a first-run wizard", requiring a dismissible, re-findable layer, "coach-marks over the REAL UI, never a modal that hides the thing it describes", that "must not block the composer". Nothing amended R7, so R7 governs. The result is a **non-blocking panel that POINTS at the real surfaces** in R7's load-bearing order (**API key → domain → ingest**; pointing at domain creation first makes the user build an empty domain and hit a wall — an error before a success). It embeds no input and **POSTs nothing**. That choice deleted the build's biggest risk: `domains.js:726` is the ONLY `POST /api/domains` in `/next`, pinned by a whole-tree walk in `test-next-chat-compile.js`, so duplicating it would have gone RED and recreated the two-hand-maintained-copies shape v3.7.0 removed. It also structurally removes the shipping wizard's own defect rather than re-inventing safety around it: that overlay is the ONE modal with **no `role="dialog"`, no `aria-modal`, no Escape, no backdrop close, no X — and no Skip on step 1**, so a user who Disconnects their last key in Settings is blocked until they paste one. **(2) THE HIGHEST-RISK LINE IN THE BUILD** is the `boot()` hook: `markBooted()` runs immediately after `boot()` returns and the `<head>` guard treats a missing sentinel as a FATAL module failure — a blank recovery panel for EVERY user. Triple-guarded (not awaited + `boot()` not async; the callee is `async` so it cannot throw synchronously; plus `try/catch`), with the comment naming **which two the suite pins** rather than claiming all three. **(3) Dismissal FAILS SAFE BY SHOWING** — the opposite of v3.6.0's consent rule, and commented as such: guidance re-appearing is harmless, permanently hiding first-run setup has **no visible symptom**. `storage` is INJECTED so the throwing case is an executed assertion, not a source regex. **(4) The last parity gap: `/next`'s wiki reader rendered escaped markdown SOURCE.** `renderMarkdown` lifted from `views/chat.js` into `next/shared/markdown.js`, one renderer for both surfaces. Safety proof is **byte-identity**, mechanically: 98 inputs sha256-identical pre/post, independently reproduced by an auditor on a different 66-input corpus, then again on 6,207 real inputs — **0 differences**, with over-bound controls proving the corpus CAN differ. Class names deliberately kept: renaming would change output in the one change whose entire proof is that output is unchanged. **(5) A REAL ReDoS, and the widening is the finding.** The wikilink/citation regexes are **quadratic** on unclosed brackets — measured idle, isolated: 64 KB → **1,890 ms**, 256 KB → **3,300 ms**, 1 MB → **~70 s**, synchronous, straight to `innerHTML`. The algorithm is PRE-EXISTING and identical in the shipping renderer; what this release changed is **WHO CAN REACH IT** — chat answers the user paid for, versus wiki bodies arriving over Personal Sync and Shared Brain mirrors **from other people**. Bounded to `{1,512}`, **derived from the corpus, not guessed**: longest real wikilink target **241** (36,910 links), longest real `[source:]` path **205** — the orchestrator first measured that as **16** by walking only `.md` and missing `conversations/*.json`, and a 200-char bound would have **silently broken real content in the maintainer's own wiki**. **(6) THE FALSE RETRACTION — the orchestrator's own error, recorded because it is the most instructive thing here.** The auditor who found the ReDoS then RETRACTED it as a CPU-contention artifact. Its retraction numbers match the **bounded** module to within 0.2% (62 ms vs 61.9); its ORIGINAL numbers match the **unbounded** module to within 0.5% (1,882 vs 1,891.7). It had re-measured a file that was fixed underneath it, because the orchestrator declared the tree FROZEN to two auditors and then dispatched a fix agent while one still had a **backgrounded job** running. Failure shape #12 — an audit against a moving tree certifies a state that never existed — **caused by the reviewer, not the agent**: both of its measurements were competent. New rule: a freeze covers an auditor's BACKGROUNDED work, not merely its foreground turn; and record `uptime` beside every timing. **(7) THE FIXES INTRODUCED A CI FLAKE, caught by re-auditing the delta.** New timing **ratio** assertions failed **73%** at ~5× oversubscription (0% idle, 0% at 2×) on provably LINEAR code, ratios spanning **0.3–35.9** — below 1 meaning the small input measured slower. Their failure text read *"4× input → 25.2× time (quadratic measured 14–20×)"*, i.e. it told CI the ReDoS fix had REGRESSED. **Deleted, not loosened** — a metric spanning 0.3–35.9 has no threshold that both never flakes and still detects a regression, and a guard that cries wolf about a main-thread freeze teaches people to ignore the one assertion protecting it. Absolute ceilings carry the guarantee (worst 337 ms against 1500 at load 82; a reverted `+` still trips them in ~1.9 s). **Verified 20/20 under the load that produced 73%.** **(8) The single-copy guard was BLIND, then still overclaiming after its own fix.** §0 used a hardcoded 3-path list while its comment claimed "anywhere in /next"; a 4th declarer left the suite **129/0 green**. Rewritten as a whole-tree walk — and the re-audit then found it **still missed `export default`**, this codebase's own idiom (13× in `src/routes/`), under a docblock reading *"Every declaration form is matched now."* Fixed, mutation-proven RED naming the file, and given an explicit **NOT ENFORCED** block (name-scoped, not algorithm-scoped: a copy as `renderPageBody`, a class method, an object property, a `globalThis` assignment or an aliased re-export all evade) whose every claim was then verified in BOTH directions. **(9) The v3.7.0 tag-build CI failure, diagnosed to mechanism.** Same commit, branch build green and tag build red. NOT slowness — CI was **faster** than the dev Mac (17.6 s vs 20.4 s). The timeout landed in §3d's **SETUP**, not the H1 tripwire, so the failing run reported **335 passed against 343 — 8 tripwire assertions never executed**. §3d hand-forges `status:'running'`; `startOrResumeJob` refuses to start a worker when the claim is **already held AND** the on-disk status reads `running` (`ingest-queue.js:2008`, reachable only past the claim check at `:1988`), so in that window nothing runs the job and `waitTerminal` can never resolve. Distribution `{~17 ms | never}` — **no cap helps**, which is the v3.6.x mistake avoided. Fixed with one `await waitWorkerIdle()`, a helper §3d already used 30 lines later; **the sibling forge site already had it**, so this closes a symmetric gap (guard-applied-to-an-instance, again). Unreproducible naturally (0/90 idle, ~1–2 per 150 under load) but **12/12 deterministic** with the window amplified 60 ms — stated as mechanism-confirmation, not probability. The helper now carries a hazard note: called BEFORE an awaited start it observes an idle claim and is **decorative**; it has 14 call sites. **(10) Doc corrections.** CLAUDE.md carried BOTH known onboarding errors in one line — "3-step" (it is 4) and *"appears when no API keys are configured in either `.curator-config.json` or `.env`"*, which the code contradicts (config-only). `docs/architecture.md`'s provider table implied `.env` suppresses the wizard; `docs/user-guide.md` listed 3 steps. All corrected against the code, plus a stale `shell.css` attribution and three comments the lift left behind. **Verification: 56 → 58 offline suites** (`test-next-onboarding` 161, `test-next-markdown` 167), **all green**; **two independent adversarial audits with deliberately different lenses** (security; does-it-still-do-what-it-was-FOR) **plus a delta re-audit that found a defect the fixes themselves introduced**, plus an independent verification of the §3d comment that corrected three of the orchestrator's own overclaims. Every headline guard mutation-proven RED for a BEHAVIOURAL reason with the restore confirmed by `shasum`; secret scan clean. **Known, recorded not hidden:** step 2 reaches into Domains by element id (asserted to exist, degrades to "you land on Domains"); **the shipping onboarding trap is left for cutover to delete** (maintainer decision) rather than risking a focus/dismissal change on the first screen a new user ever sees, for code the next release removes — it stays reachable via `/old` during the escape-hatch window, so it is a live defect until then, not a theoretical one; and **the shipping renderer `src/public/markdown.js` is left UNBOUNDED, deliberately** (maintainer decision), because it only ever renders the user's own chat answers so no third party's bytes reach the quadratic — the two renderers have therefore DIVERGED on a security-relevant property, and the warning is recorded **at the regexes themselves**, not in a doc, because the person who widens that renderer's input is the person who needs it. **(11) THE PANEL WAS SWALLOWING CLICKS, and it was first reported to the orchestrator — and by him to the maintainer — as a COSMETIC overlap of a readout.** It was not. Measured with `elementFromPoint` over each control's CENTRE rather than by comparing text boxes, the fixed top-right panel was the topmost element over **Domains' `#dm-ask-btn` "Ask this domain"** (that view's primary action) and **Settings' API-key "Disconnect" and "Replace"** — and because `.obp-panel` is `pointer-events: auto`, those clicks were **swallowed, not merely obscured**. Text overlap hit **6 of 7 views**, not one. The sharpest part: the panel's own step 1 sends the user to Settings, so it told them to add an API key and then blocked the buttons for doing it. Fixed by RESERVING the strip rather than floating over it — `views/onboarding.js` puts `guide-docked` on `<body>`, `shell.css` gives `.main` a `padding-right: var(--guide-dock-w)`, and the card's width is **DERIVED** (`calc(var(--guide-dock-w) - 28px)`) rather than a second copy of `340px`, because a reservation that can drift from the thing it reserves for is the original defect re-armed. Swept **7 views × 2 themes × 2 viewports = 28 combinations: 0 text overlaps, 0 control overlaps, 0 horizontal overflow**; verified with a REAL trusted click and 35 REAL keystrokes landing in the composer with the panel open, and the reader overlay still above it. Mutation-proven in both directions, including a one-character drift of the dock class name (`guide-docked` → `guide-dockd`) which reproduced the blocked button live. **The lesson is the orchestrator's:** the first agent measured TEXT overlap and reported it as cosmetic; nobody asked `elementFromPoint` what was actually on top until a second agent was pointed at it. A characterisation relayed without measurement is not a measurement. **(12) An a11y defect only reachable on the accessible path.** `refresh()` re-renders ~100 ms after open, replacing `root.innerHTML` and dropping focus to `<body>` — so an explicit "Show setup guide" focused the heading and then silently stranded a keyboard user. Invisible because the AUTOMATIC path never takes focus at all (deliberately), so it could only ever fire for the users who depend on focus management. Focus is now captured before the re-render and restored **by id**, since the captured node cannot survive `innerHTML` replacement. **Fixed in this release, not deferred:** the shipping MCP tool count, which said "10 structured tools" in **two** places against a real table of 18 — wrong for the second time (v3.6.1 fixed "seventeen tools, ten read and seven write"), so it is now pinned by an assertion that reads the real `tools` array, living in the `/next` suite that already holds that reader rather than standing up a second copy of it. |
| `v3.7.0` | **PARITY RELEASE — the six capabilities `/next` was missing, so cutover cannot ship a version where users LOSE features. The shipping frontend is byte-untouched (empty diff on `app.js`/`index.html`/`styles.css`/`markdown.js`, verified every round).** Measured mechanically, six hard gaps sat OUTSIDE the wizard workstream: switching `/next` on would have shipped a build where a user cannot create/rename/delete a domain, cannot save a conversation to the wiki, cannot browse their own wiki, cannot serve a GDPR Article 17 erasure, and where **"merge every high-confidence duplicate sight unseen" was the ONLY action available on a semantic duplicate** — no Preview, no Flip, no Skip, on an operation that deletes a file and rewrites every `[[link]]` to it across the domain on an LLM judgement. **(1) Domain lifecycle** wired to the three existing routes. Two semantics that bite: **`newSlug` may EQUAL `oldSlug`** on a display-name-only rename (state is re-keyed off the RESPONSE, never an assumption the slug moved — otherwise every later call 404s), and a 409 refusal must render on a guaranteed-visible surface. **`api-reference.md` documented that identical-slug branch as a `400` error; the route returns 200 with `syncWarning:false`** — an agent that trusted the doc over the code would have built the wrong thing. **(2) The per-pair semantic-merge gate restored** alongside the batch bar: Preview / Merge / Flip / Skip, with the previewed-set reset on **every scan, every domain switch, and every Flip**. `domains.js` uses PERSISTENT state, so a set never cleared survives a navigate-away and can authorise a merge on **a different domain's pair** — the assertion is that the set is EMPTY, not merely that the gate blocks. Two independent audits ran that battery separately; force-enabling the disabled Merge button on an un-previewed pair produced **zero network calls** — the safety property holds, and independent live testing in v3.9.0 re-confirmed it with `fetch`, `XHR` and `sendBeacon` all instrumented and the file inventory byte-identical. **CORRECTION, recorded because this row originally overclaimed it:** that same testing found the refusal is NOT visible — the click is a silent no-op followed by a re-render. The gate is real; the feedback is not. Fixing the feedback is tracked, and the claim is corrected here rather than left standing, because a changelog asserting a user-visible behaviour the code does not have is what stops the next reviewer looking. **(3) `GET /api/wiki/:domain/list`** — readdir-only, **462 KB / 25 ms cold / 8 ms warm** for 3,363 pages against 14 MB for the full-content route. Built on `listMd` **imported** from `health.js`, never copied: two hand-maintained inventories is the v3.2.0 CRITICAL shape, and a naive `readdir` lists pages the reader then 400s on. Verified 3,363 listed / 3,363 openable / 0 drift. `title` is **slug-derived, not frontmatter** — documented AS a trade-off with its reason, so nobody "fixes" the mismatch by reinstating the 14 MB read. **(4) Compile to Wiki**, with the v3.0.14 invariant verbatim (inline card, **no `max-height`/`overflow`/`flex-shrink`** — any `overflow` makes its automatic `min-height` resolve to 0 and the thread squeezes it), and `refused` rendered as a NORMAL outcome, never an error: it is the most common first-use result on a short conversation. **(5) Shared Brain revoke + admin-token rotate** — `/next` shipped `/members`, which exists SOLELY so an admin can find a `fellow_id` to revoke, and not the action itself. **(6) THE HIGHEST-STAKES BUG, found by audit, live-reproduced: a remount re-enabled Compile mid-run, and the second click returned "manually delete the `.write-lock`" WHILE A LIVE PAID COMPILE HELD IT.** `compileBusy` was cleared unconditionally on every mount and `updateCompileButtonBusy` had no ownership check; `compile.js` emits `progress(20)` then nothing until `progress(85)`, so the re-enabled window was the entire 15–45 s LLM call. Fixed with an **owner token**, NOT by gating the reset — gating re-creates the stale-spinner bug the ungated reset exists to prevent. The asymmetry was established in code, not asserted: `state.sending` drives a visual artifact whose every repaint is already mount-gated, while `compileBusy` is a lock whose release is guaranteed by the run's own `finally`. **The comment three lines above the bug claimed the opposite of what the code did.** **(7) A second HIGH: the compile card scrolled UNDER an opaque sticky bar**, hiding the only signal that the `full → concise → summary-only` ladder had degraded the compile — and it bit precisely in the case the function exists for (a card taller than the viewport). Bar height is now MEASURED, never hardcoded. **(8) CROSS-AGENT COLLISIONS — four builders, none seeing the others' final state.** One built a first-run domain-creation panel in Chat; another deleted the handoff reaching it, leaving dead code, a comment asserting a reachability that no longer existed, and **a SECOND `POST /api/domains`** — the exact duplicate create-path the roadmap was reordered (parity BEFORE onboarding) to prevent. Removed; a filesystem walk now pins **exactly one** create call site. **(9) A TEST GUARDING THE MOST DESTRUCTIVE PATH WAS INVISIBLE TO CI.** `test-next-semantic-gate.js` was 95/95 green while the runner classified the whole suite as **⏭ skip** — not run — because an assertion label contained the word `SKIPPED`, naming the feature's own Skip button. v3.3.0 hit this class and answered it with a defensive assertion inside ONE suite, leaving every suite written since exposed. The first repair anchored the patterns, which fixed that instance, **missed two idioms entirely** (`⊘ No … key configured`, `Self-skipping (live-suite convention)` — both reporting ✓ pass while running NOTHING) and **introduced a new false positive** (a PARTIAL skip line marking 254 passing assertions not-run on a root runner). Now the discriminator is a property of what ran, not a phrase: **announces a skip AND reports no assertion tally.** **(10) FOUR docblocks contradicted by code in their own file**, including a confident "DISCREPANCY FOUND" note directing the next reviewer to re-fix a hole closed in v3.6.0, with six stale line-number citations — all replaced by route names, because line numbers rot and that is exactly what put a false claim there. **(11) CI: `test-beta15-production.js` moved to LIVE_LOCAL.** Its runtime is **bimodal, not variable** — ingest's Phase-2 recovery ladder turns one failed batch into page-by-page, one LLM call per page: fast path 248–600 s, fallback path >20 min. Measured 351/380/512/452/590/554 s green → **600 s TIMEOUT** → 248 s (fastest ever) → **1,200 s TIMEOUT against a cap that had just been RAISED**. Raising the cap was the WRONG FIX and is recorded as such: the fallback's cost is O(pages), so **no finite cap bounds it**. Recorded as a PRODUCT follow-up, not just CI hygiene — a run entering that ladder spends dozens of extra LLM calls, and **users pay for those too**. **Verification: 51 → 56 offline suites** (`test-next-domain-lifecycle` 112, `test-next-semantic-gate` 102, `test-next-chat-compile` 202, `test-next-sharedbrain-admin` 198, `test-wiki-list` 48); **one adversarial audit, one delta re-audit**, the second re-testing the first's three "could not break" claims rather than inheriting them (all three held) — plus **two findings the re-audit made against the ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN fixes**. Every guard mutation-proven RED for a behavioural reason with the restore confirmed by `shasum`; secret scan 0 unexpected across 250 files. **Known, recorded not hidden:** `/next`'s wiki-browse reader shows **escaped markdown SOURCE, not rendered markdown** — `renderMarkdown` lives inside the chat view, and copying a security-sensitive escape-first renderer across module boundaries is the duplication that produced the v3.2.0 CRITICAL; lifting it to a shared module is deferred to cutover, and `/next`'s renderer has no test coverage of its own. |
| `v3.6.2` | **Four recorded follow-ups, and what hunting them found: a GDPR erasure the app could certify without performing, a privacy flag that asked a question it never honoured, and a guard that was decorative inside the fix for a decorative guard.** **(1) THE WORST ONE: revoke could report a successful Article 17 erasure having scanned ZERO pages.** `sharedbrain-revoke.js` wrapped `adapter.listPages(...)` in `.catch(() => [])` — swallowing `SHARED_BRAIN_TREE_TRUNCATED`, the error whose OWN docblock says it exists because otherwise *"revoke would report a successful Article 17 erasure while missing submissions. Refuse loudly instead."* The guard was intact in the adapter and defeated by the one consumer it was written for. On a truncated tree: zero pages scanned, `pages_deleted: 0`, rebuild run, marker cleared, **"Revocation complete"** — while every page carrying that contributor's provenance survived untouched. Now a **scope** failure ABORTS before rebuild-and-clear (we cannot erase what we could not enumerate), while **per-item** failures record and continue — aborting at item 3 of 40 leaves 37 files we could have deleted, each one continuing unlawful processing. The safety property of abort is preserved by the marker: any recorded failure leaves `state/revocation-in-progress` ACTIVE, so synthesis keeps refusing and the admin is forced to resolve. **"Revocation complete" is now emitted at exactly ONE site, inside `if (problems.length === 0)` — there is no `else`, no `default:`, no fallthrough that can reach it**, because v3.6.1's finding 5 was a `default:` arm falling into the cheerful branch. **(2) THE FIX'S OWN GUARD WAS DECORATIVE — TWICE, and this is the third layer of that pattern.** `fullSuccess` decides whether the marker is cleared on an irreversible operation. Replacing `pagesRebuildFailed`'s entire derivation with `const pagesRebuildFailed = 0;` left the suite at **98 passed / 0 failed**; `rebuildOk` was equally uncovered. **Two of three terms gating an Article 17 erasure had no test that could fail** — inside the release fixing v3.6.1's decorative-guard finding. Both now covered by real failing-adapter fixtures (a genuine `EACCES` on a real `unlink`, with a precondition asserting the file actually survived, and a control proving the harness can go green); the constant-0 mutation now produces **9 RED**. **(3) A fix that introduced a cohort-wide outage, cut before shipping.** Adding `auditWritten` to `fullSuccess` meant a rate-limited audit-log write left the marker set — and the marker makes `runLocalSynthesis` return `ok:false` for **EVERY** contributor until an admin re-runs the whole revocation, which under a persisting rate limit fails identically. The marker exists to stop synthesis running over mid-erasure state; an audit-write failure has nothing to do with that. **Removed from the gate, kept loud** (`ok:false`, `audit_failed`, named in the summary), with a comment recording why so it is not "fixed" back. **(4) `attribute_by_name` obtained a privacy decision the system could not honour — and needed TWO gates, not one.** The flag was collected by both wizards, validated, persisted, and **read by nothing**, while `sharedbrain.js:620` wrote the contributor's real display name to shared storage unconditionally on every push. The consent review screen — the last thing read before agreeing — printed **"anonymous UUID (default)"**. Implemented per an explicit maintainer decision. The mechanical enumeration found a **second route the reported one did not name**: `fellowDisplayName` is copied into `delta.contributor_name` for **every page**, so gating only the payload would still have published the name on every page pushed — proven by a mutation where the object-level assertion stays GREEN and only a **byte-level scan of everything written** (utf-8 AND base64-decoded, since base64 is 3-byte aligned) goes red. Fails closed on an absent or non-boolean flag: `getSharedBrainWithToken` does NO defaulting on load, so a legacy connection reaches push with the flag absent entirely. **The checkbox also described a fictional exposure** — *"Show my name in Provenance sections"*, where `buildProvenanceSection` maps every id through `shortenId` and has never carried a name — while the real exposure went unmentioned, and the promised admin-side second gate (`allow_name_attribution`) has zero readers. Both frontends corrected, byte-identical. **NOT retroactive, stated plainly rather than implied away.** **(5) `/dismiss` and `/undismiss` lacked `assertWritableDomain` — and the class invariant written to catch that class did not catch it.** `test-route-write-guards.js` enumerates health.js mechanically, yet stayed green: the routes reached an **explicit exemption** whose stated reason (*"not wiki content"*) was factually wrong — the store is `<domain>/wiki/.health-dismissed.jsonl`, INSIDE the synced folder, which is exactly why the guard is required. Worse, `assertWritableDomain` **was not a checked token at all**; downgrading it on the bulk-destructive `/fix-all` left the suite silently green. A single flat token list can only express one axis. Now two guard classes with per-axis exemptions, single-sourced; **306 → 368 assertions**, and the file carries explicit **ENFORCED / NOT ENFORCED** lists naming the 12 routes whose concurrency guard is source-scan only — because a present-but-inert `registerWrite` is invisible to a text scan, and that is *not a fixable regex* but an unbounded family. **(6) Health cost estimates read ~25% low.** `health-ai.js` carried a drifted duplicate price table: the Gemini default was **exactly −25% on both axes** and **five model ids were absent entirely**, returning `null` — rendered by two preview panels as a **literal empty string** on a spend gate. Deleted; it now imports the single authoritative `getModelPrice()`. **(7) The dead-data shape, caught twice inside this release's own fixes.** Both backend fixes added result fields with **no consumer** — v3.6.1's finding 5 verbatim. Wired to both frontends; a route comment claiming a dead-data fix it did not deliver was corrected rather than left overclaiming. **(8) THE DOCS WERE THE BLOCKING ITEM: ten false claims, two GDPR-facing, all cross-agent collisions.** Seven agents edited in sequence, none seeing the others' final state — code moved, the docs describing it did not. The worst: *"the member directory shows a real name only for contributors who opted in"* — false for **every existing cohort**, because `groupMembers` takes the name from any historical payload that carries one and the gate is forward-only, so an upgraded cohort's directory is **byte-for-byte unchanged**; an admin could have relayed that to a data subject. Also: `admin.md` still documented the marker behaviour **this release deliberately removed**; `marker_cleared: false` has **four** causes, not the three enumerated at two sites; *"admins can review revocation history, including incomplete attempts"* is false — `appendAudit` runs after all seven abort paths, so an aborted erasure leaves the log looking as if it never happened; and a four-way contradiction across three docs about what the directory actually displays (it is the 8-char short ID, not the UUID). **Verification: 48 → 51 offline suites, all green** (`test-sharedbrain-attribution.js` 74, `test-health-ai-pricing.js` 80, `test-health-cost-readouts.js` 59; `test-sharedbrain-revoke.js` **39 → 254**, `test-route-write-guards.js` **213 → 368**); **two independent adversarial audits** — the second re-tested the first's three "could not break" claims rather than inheriting them (all three held) and found the ten doc defects the first missed, because the first had audited a **moving tree**. Every headline guard mutation-proven RED for a behavioural reason and the restore confirmed by `shasum`; MCP stdout re-verified pure JSON, 18 tools, **0 stderr bytes**; secret scan 0 unexpected across 245 files. **Recorded, not fixed:** aborted revocations write no audit record (documented, with the admin told to record it themselves — adding a write to an irreversible path after the re-audit was the wrong trade); `attribute_by_name` has no in-app withdrawal path (Art. 7(3) — changing it means Disconnect + re-join, now stated); `pages_rebuild_failed` blocking the marker converts a self-healing condition into a permanent one, kept deliberately to match `rebuildOk`'s eight-release precedent, with the recovery path now documented; `failures.markerClear` has no fixture; and bracket-notation route registration is invisible to the auditor — now an enforced assertion rather than a comment that would rot. |
| `v3.6.1` | **Two destructive defects users could reach today, a cutover landmine defused two releases early, and a documentation pass that found eight false claims — four of them in the file every new session reads as binding.** **(1) THE WORST ONE: `--domain=X` silently repaired the WRONG domain and deleted its files.** All three destructive wiki scripts parsed arguments as `args.find(a => !a.startsWith('--')) || 'articles'` — and because `--domain=business` *starts with* `--`, it was skipped and the script fell back to a hardcoded domain. Reproduced live in an isolated tempdir: `--domain=business` **deleted a file in `articles`** and left `business` untouched, and the wrong domain propagated into the child process too. `fix-wiki-structure.js` failed differently and worse — `--domain=X` became a literal directory name, `fixDomain()` hit `if (!existsSync(wikiDir)) return;`, and the run **exited 0 printing "Done. Review the changes above"** with nothing above it: a success report over a total no-op — while a *bare* invocation mutated **every** domain. **The fix closed the CLASS, not the instance:** all three now share one contract (positional or `--domain=`, interchangeable and required to agree; `--dry-run` proven by recursive checksum to write nothing; `--help`; **no default domain and no implicit all-domains mode** — a bare invocation is refused rather than guessed; an unrecognised `-`/`--` token is a hard error, never silently skipped; the domain validated as a single safe path segment; `main()` gated behind a direct-invocation check, proven by a mutation in which **a mere import deleted a wiki file**). `fix-wiki-structure.js` keeps its documented all-domains migration but now requires an explicit **`--all`** — the capability was never the danger, being the accidental default was. `bulk-reingest.js` shares the shape but has no `|| 'articles'`, so it fails loudly; CLAUDE.md's Scripts Reference nevertheless documented `--domain=` for it, which **cannot work**, plus bare invocations for two scripts that now refuse. Corrected. **(2) A corrupt `claude_desktop_config.json` was presented as an EMPTY one, beside a copy button that would erase the user's other MCP servers.** `readClaudeConfig()` returned `{__parseError:true}` and `/claude-full-config` returned an identical payload for absent and corrupt, with `merged` containing ONLY the Curator entry. `GET /config` already computed `claude_config_parse_error` — and **nothing in the tree read it.** Now `merged` is `null` when unparseable: structurally unpasteable rather than merely discouraged. **`was_empty` deliberately STAYS `true`** — an "accurate" `false` makes the shipping frontend dereference `null.mcpServers` and takes the whole MCP section down. The accurate-looking value was the dangerous one. **(3) The self-test did not test what the wizard tells you to paste.** `buildCuratorEntry` prescribes `--domains-path`; `/self-test` spawned without it, so the child fell back to config/env and a wrong folder still went green — after which the UI said *"the bridge is working — the issue is inside its config file."* Fixed to spawn from the same source, proven end-to-end against the real `mcp/server.js` in **both** directions. **(4) THE FIX'S OWN GUARD WAS DECORATIVE — found by an independent audit, re-proven by the orchestrator.** The regression test asserted the spawn with an *unanchored prefix* regex, so `selfTestEntry.args.slice(0, 1)` — D2 reproduced exactly — left the suite at **`Passed: 93  Failed: 0`**, `node --check` clean. The same suite's comment nine lines below records learning that precise lesson for a *sibling* guard. Anchored, and backed by a behavioural route-level proof rather than a regex. **(5) Six new API fields were DEAD DATA.** The honest `domains_status` classification reached no consumer — `runSelfTestInto` still read only `ok`/`tool_count`/`domains` and still said *"no domains yet"* for a missing folder. The mechanism was fixed and the purpose left broken: this release's named failure shape, inside its own fix.
