Now consumed, with the `default:` arm falling into the **honest** branch, because defaulting into the cheerful one is how the defect survived. **(6) A hostile invite token could inject ~6,000 characters into our own refusal message.** Shared Brain invite tokens are now **GitHub-only, refused at BOTH ends of the codec** — a `local` invite could never produce a saveable connection yet parsed fine, so the contributor created a real PAT on github.com before being refused, and `cloudflare-r2` passed *save* too, persisting a dead connection. But the new, friendlier refusal *echoed the attacker-controlled `storage_type` unbounded and FIRST*, so an injected *"email your Personal Access Token to …"* read as our instruction, at the exact moment the user is being asked about credentials — and invite tokens are designed to be forwarded over Slack and email. A usability improvement had quietly become an attack surface. Bounded to short, boring values; legitimate types are still named. **(7) The cutover landmine, defused early.** `next/index.html` carried **18 bare-relative references**. Served at `/` on cutover day, `src="app.js"` resolves to `/app.js` — **which exists** — so the shell would load the SHIPPING bundle; 16 CSS refs would hit the `app.get('*')` catch-all and receive **200 + text/html**, which a browser refuses as a stylesheet. And every guard would have certified success: `src/public/app.js` sets `window.__curatorBooted`, the exact sentinel `/next`'s boot guard treats as proof of a healthy boot, and `test-css-tokens.js` reads stylesheets from **disk, not HTTP**. Three layers reporting green over an unstyled app running the wrong code. All 18 are now root-absolute, verified by actually serving them (a control request for the old path returned **`200 text/html`**, reproducing the trap live), and pinned by `test-next-asset-paths.js` with a **negative-control fixture proving the detector can fail**. **(8) `/next` MCP setup wizard** — three steps plus a blocking state that, on a corrupt config, **never even issues** the whole-file request. Its tool counts had been wrong on all three numbers (*"seventeen tools, ten read and seven write"*); the truth is **18**, of which **4 mutate**. **(9) The docs pass found EIGHT false claims, four in CLAUDE.md itself.** `sync.md` described pulls as *"a rebase strategy"* where *"git will flag a conflict"* — it is `merge -X theirs`, which **silently resolves your local version away** while reporting success (the correction also documents the recovery path, which the auto-save commit does preserve). CLAUDE.md asserted *"the MCP is read-only — there is no write/mutate tool"* (four mutate, one deletes files) **and** *"exactly one wiki-write code path"* — `health.js` never imports `writePage` and writes directly, which is precisely why the v3.2.0 CRITICAL lived there and why a doc claiming one chokepoint stops the next auditor looking. `architecture.md` said the app *"refuses to auto-fix"* broken links; they are in `AUTO_FIXABLE` **and** in `fixAllSafe`. Two model-read surfaces were changing Claude's behaviour: the skill's `fix_wiki_issue` example passed a field that does not exist, so **every fix in the documented loop was rejected**, and a tool description promised writes are *"never overwritten"* when a prose heading you include **replaces** the existing one — the v3.0.1-beta.15 prose-wipe bug, re-armed in prose. **(10) Shared Brain privacy documentation promised things the code does not do.** The contributor's real display name is written to shared storage **unconditionally on every push** while the compliance doc listed real names under *"never in shared storage"*; the *"defensive double-gate"* for name attribution does not exist — `attribute_by_name` has writers and **zero readers**, and `allow_name_attribution` appears in the entire repository only in the sentence describing it; and EU deployments were told to buy GitHub Enterprise Cloud data residency, which the hardcoded `api.github.com` adapter **cannot reach**. Corrected to match the code, per an explicit maintainer decision. **Verification: 43 → 48 offline suites, all green; five independent adversarial audits**, two of which found HIGHs *inside fixes that had already been reviewed*; MCP stdout re-verified as pure JSON with **0 stderr bytes** after a tool description changed; secret scan clean. **Recorded, not fixed:** `health-ai.js` carries a second, drifted price table making Health estimates read ~25% low (paired with a doc change, so fixing one alone makes the other wrong); `/dismiss` and `/undismiss` lack `assertWritableDomain` while their MCP twins have it — a sixth instance of guard-applied-to-a-route-not-a-class; revoke swallows per-file delete failures and still reports success; and `attribute_by_name` obtains a privacy decision the system cannot honour — implement it or stop asking. |
| `v3.6.0` | **`/next` Ingest + Shared Brain + credential wizard, and four production bugs the work surfaced.** Nothing user-visible changes except 26 lines of `app.js`; the value is what building the new UI *revealed*. **(1) THE WORST BUG: renaming a domain mid-ingest wrote the rest of the document into an INVISIBLE folder.** `writePage` does `mkdir(dir, {recursive:true})`, so instead of failing it **recreated** the moved-away directory and wrote on. That ghost has no `CLAUDE.md`, so `listDomains()` filters it out under the v2.3.4 ghost-domain rule — the pages are unreachable from Domains, Wiki, Health, chat retrieval and the MCP alike. The ingest then died at `appendLog()` with a raw `ENOENT`, **after** the spend and **after** the pages were on disk. Paid for, written, invisible. Found by driving the real `files.js` functions rather than reasoning about them. `PUT /api/domains/:domain` now guards with `isDomainActive` — the **per-domain** predicate matching its `DELETE` sibling, not the global one — on **both** branches, since a display-name-only rename still rewrites `log.md` and races `appendLog()`. **(2) Config mutations mid-write.** `getDomainsDir()` and `getProviderInfo()` both resolve **fresh per call**, so changing the knowledge folder mid-ingest split one document across two locations, and a provider switch (or a key Disconnect — v3.0.2's last-saved-wins means even a *save* switches the active provider) could fail a run partway through or silently finish it on a different model. Five `config.js` routes guarded. `/pick-folder` turned out to mutate directly (it calls `setDomainsDir` itself, never delegating), and because `osascript` blocks for up to 60s it needed a **second** check immediately before the write — deliberately NOT folded into the `cancelled` branch, because `app.js:5150` tests `data.cancelled` **before** `res.ok`, so a refusal carrying that field would be silently swallowed as "user pressed Cancel". **(3) `POST /api/sync/setup` was the only one of five sync routes without `guardConcurrent`** — and it runs `git add -A` + commit + push across the domains work-tree, exactly the harm its own docblock describes. **(4) `POST /:domain/fix` is a SUPERSET of `/fix-all`, and only the subset was protected.** `fixIssue()` branches on its third argument: falsy runs the **full bulk path**. `/fix` passes `issue || null`, so `{type}` with no issue does identical bulk work — proven live at **1500/1500 files**, unregistered and unlocked. The comment on `/fix-all` called `/fix` *"sub-second and intentionally NOT registered"* — true of the shape the UI sends, false of the shape the route accepts. **(5) THE MODEL CHAIN WAS 80% DEAD — the v3.0.15 Gemini incident, repeating on Anthropic.** A live probe found **4 of 5 `FALLBACK_CHAINS.anthropic` rungs return 404**: the entire `claude-3-5-haiku` / `claude-3-*-sonnet` generation is gone from the direct API. It failed *safely* (`isModelNotFound` skips a 404) but silently landed on Sonnet 4.5 at **3× Haiku's price** — the exact inverse of the chain's documented promise to reach the cheapest still-working model. A safety net is only exercised the day a provider retires your default; that is the worst possible day to discover it rotted. Chain repaired to `['claude-sonnet-5','claude-sonnet-4-6','claude-sonnet-4-5']`, cheapest-first among verified-live models — **`claude-sonnet-5` is both newest AND cheapest** ($2/$10 vs $3/$15), a within-family price *drop* no family-name heuristic could see. **A trap caught mid-flight:** a cached pricing table said Sonnet 5's $2/$10 was *introductory, expiring in seven days*; the live provider page showed the rise had been cancelled. Trusting the cache would have **inverted the chain's cost ordering a week after shipping**, silently. New rule recorded: a price that determines chain ORDER is verified against the live page, never a cached table. **(6) Per-model output caps replace a flat clamp.** `ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` keeps its name and value but now means *the conservative default for an unrecognised id*; `anthropicMaxOutputTokens()` returns 64,000 for Haiku 4.5 / Sonnet 4.5 and **128,000** for Sonnet 4.6 / Sonnet 5 — caps are **not monotonic with recency**. An unknown id, `__proto__` or `constructor` all resolve to the **conservative** 64,000: guessing high is a hard API rejection, guessing low only truncates, and chat degrades gracefully on truncation (v3.0.7). **(7) A refused destructive write was INVISIBLE, and our own guard made it reachable.** Guarding `/fix` meant it could now 409 — and the semantic-merge handler called `closeMergePreview()` only on the success path while writing errors to `#health-status`, at the top of the Health tab, **underneath a 92%-opaque `position:fixed; inset:0` overlay that stayed up**. The `finally` reset the button. Net effect: the user saw the button flip back and nothing else, read it as "my click didn't register", and clicked Merge again — **retrying a refused DESTRUCTIVE write with zero feedback.** The refusal now renders inside the modal, reusing `openMergePreview`'s own existing catch pattern (the modal is the only guaranteed-visible surface; `#health-status` can be scrolled out of view, and keeping the pair on screen lets the user decide whether to retry a transient 409). Three siblings fixed with it: a button left permanently reading "Fixing…", an AI **"Apply"** silently relabelling to "Fix", and `(await r.json())` inside a `throw` at four sites turning a non-JSON 409 into `Unexpected token '<'` — the class fixed once before in v2.3.3. **(8) `/next` — Ingest view** ported with all three maintainer-found production defects preserved (selections **accumulate** across picker and drop events with batch mode sticky; cancel gives immediate feedback with **terminal status always beating a stale pending flag**; a terminal batch is dismissable without a reload), **Shared Brain view**, and a **5-step credential wizard** in its own module. **(9) A cross-view write gate, and the fix that broke it.** `beginDomainWrite()` returns a **release function that has already closed over the domain** — there is no second call site with its own copy of the key to drift from the first, which is the structural fix for the shipping app's H2 leak. Its first version coupled gate truth to view lifecycle, so a running batch read as not-busy once you navigated away; the fix added a shell-level watcher sourcing truth from `GET /api/ingest-queue/active` — **and that fix introduced a HIGH of its own**: its terminal test omitted `paused`/`pending`, and since `boot()` calls the watcher and a **crash-recovered job always returns as `paused`**, it would have shipped a permanently dead Sync button surviving reloads. Fixed by using the shared `queueBusyTransition` so "is this batch writing" has **one** definition rather than two that can drift. **(10) `/next` had NO AI privacy disclosure** while running the same LLM-backed Health actions the shipping app has gated for many releases. Added, reading the **same** key (`curator-ai-health-disclosure-seen-v1`) so an existing user is never re-asked at cutover, and failing **closed** when localStorage throws — for a consent, asking again is the safe direction. An auditor blocked and counted requests: **0** LLM calls reachable before consent, on mouse, keyboard and a triple-click race. **(11) Credential wizard, audited hard.** Hygiene held completely — the PAT appears in exactly **two POST bodies**, never a URL or GET; nothing in storage, cookies, history or DOM residue; every XSS sink escaped; the 8 KB invite cap holding. But a **HIGH**: a PAT verdict survived an invite-token change, so `read_only` — the **tier flag** — could be decided by a verdict about a **different repository**, with no validation call ever made for the repo actually saved. Wrong in both directions and silent. Fixed in two independent layers: `state.inviteMetadata` now has exactly **one** assignment site that clears the verdict on a repo change, **and** the only two readers refuse a verdict whose recorded repo does not match — the second layer does not depend on the first being remembered. Plus: admin step 1 minted invite tokens its own parser refuses (`branch` validated everywhere except where it is created, behind a comment claiming it mirrored the server *"verbatim"*), and Escape during save cancelled nothing while telling the user it had. **(12) Test guards that were quietly blind.** `test-css-tokens.js` covered **none** of `/next` (16 CSS files, 748 `var()` refs) — and when extended, an `href` regex matched `data-href`, so the suite scanned one file twice and the real target never, **reporting MORE files scanned while covering less**. Also fixed: `>` inside a quoted attribute truncating a tag (silent coverage loss), unfollowed `@import`, and an unguarded `readFileSync` that killed the whole suite with a raw stack. 51 → **124** assertions. New **`test-next-ingest-logic-drift.js`** pins 13 pure helpers **byte-identical** between the two frontends, so a bug fixed in one and not the other goes RED instead of silently re-shipping at cutover — it caught a realistic `cancelled`→`canceled` typo that would have broken the no-item-lost invariant. New **`test-route-write-guards.js`** (185 assertions) adds **class-level enumeration invariants** for `sync.js` and `health.js`: every mutating route must carry its guards, enumerated mechanically rather than spot-checked — because a spot-check is exactly what missed `/sync/setup`. **THE PATTERN, four instances in one release: a guard applied to a ROUTE rather than to a CLASS.** Each was added correctly for the case in front of its author, and each left a sibling doing identical work unprotected — invisible because the reasoning lived in a comment on the *guarded* route. `config.js` and `domains.js` remain per-route; that is the recorded soft spot. **Verification: 40 → 42 suites, all green; four independent adversarial audits** (each found something real, and **two rounds of fixes introduced new HIGHs that the next audit caught** — the recorded reason fixes get re-audited here); shipping `index.html`/`styles.css` **byte-untouched**, `app.js` confined to handler bodies with nothing at module scope; secret scan 0 new findings. Docs corrected in the same release, including `ingestion-pipeline.md` describing a Haiku-first fallback that no longer exists and `architecture.md` claiming a wizard that this release actually built. |
| `v3.5.1` | **Four fixes from the maintainer using v3.5.0 in production, plus repo hygiene.** **(1) The RAW source bar did not say what it was.** He opened `summaries/from-lab-to-life-growth-strategy` in `business` and asked whether the file was in the wrong folder. It was not — but the UI gave him no way to tell: `wiki/summaries/…md` (2,213 bytes, sha `04241e4d`) and `raw/…md` (22,179 bytes, sha `376adc8d`) are **two different files that share a name**, because the source was itself a `.md` and `computeSummarySlugFromSource` produced an identical slug. With a PDF the distinction is obvious; with a markdown source the bar looked like it pointed at the page being read. Labelled **RAW** — the maintainer's call, and the right one: `raw/` is the actual folder name used throughout the code and docs, and inventing a second name for the same concept is a small version of the confusion being fixed. **(2) YAML frontmatter rendered as body text on EVERY wiki page** — `type: summary source: … tags: […]` as a paragraph, which also made the word `source:` appear twice on screen meaning two different things. New `stripFrontmatter` removes only a **leading, properly-closed** block: a mid-document `---` horizontal rule survives, content inside fenced code blocks survives, and an **unterminated** opening `---` returns the document unchanged rather than swallowing it. Wired into `renderMarkdown` only — `markdown.js` (the chat renderer, a separate path) is untouched and asserted so. **(3) Sync hygiene — Obsidian noise.** The knowledge repo was tracking `.obsidian/workspace.json`, which Obsidian rewrites on every pane move, plus `*.base` / `Untitled*.md` leftovers — inflating the Sync badge for no reason, the same class as `.DS_Store` in v3.0.16. **Scope deliberately narrow: `workspace.json` ONLY, never `.obsidian/` wholesale** — whether appearance/graph settings sync across machines is a user preference, not ours to impose. **A real bug caught while writing the tests:** the first rule was `.obsidian/workspace.json` with no `**/` prefix, and per `gitignore(5)` a pattern containing a slash anywhere but a single trailing one is **anchored to the `.gitignore`'s own directory** — so it matched only `domains/.obsidian/workspace.json`, never the real vault-root depths. The untrack itself worked (git pathspec matching is not gitignore-based), but **the very next `git add -A` in the same cycle silently re-staged the file, undoing the untrack inside its own commit.** Reproduced live against real git, fixed with `**/`, and pinned by a test asserting the prefixed form is present and the un-prefixed form absent. Untrack ordering follows the v3.0.16 rule — **after** a successful merge in `pull()`, never before, or git aborts the next pull with "Please move or remove them before you merge"; mutation-proven by driving the real exported untrack functions out of order against real two-machine git fixtures. `git ls-files -z` is load-bearing here: `"Tali Režun.md"` is **non-ASCII**, exactly the C-quoting case that defeated ASCII-only parsing in that release. **Untrack is `git rm --cached`, never a delete** — asserted with `existsSync` after every untrack across root, one-level and two-level depths. **(4) `scripts/test-ingest-real-llm.js` de-personalised** — it is in the PUBLIC repo and hardcoded the maintainer's filesystem three times, including the title of an unpublished personal article, and asserted on "Dr Alice Smith" as its author. Paths now come from `CURATOR_LIVE_SCHEMA` / `CURATOR_LIVE_ARTICLE` with a clean self-skip when unset (the same contract every other LIVE suite uses); the author assertion is now **structural** — derived from whatever byline the supplied article carries via the already-exported `extractAuthorHints`/`slugifyName`, or skipped gracefully if it has none. Verified both paths: self-skip exits 0, and a live run with the vars set passed 18/18, deriving `tali-rezun` from the article's own frontmatter rather than a constant. **Verification: 40 offline suites, 4 consecutive green runs** (`test-raw-source-ui.js` 96 assertions, `test-sync-hygiene.js` **248**, +8 new sections); every fix mutation-proven RED with output recorded; browser-verified on an isolated instance reproducing the exact filename collision. **Repo audit (maintainer-prompted):** all six private working documents confirmed gitignored and **never committed in the entire history**; `domains/` tracks exactly one file (`.gitkeep`); no personal email anywhere; 229 tracked files. |
| `v3.5.0` | **Track 7 Part II — raw-source retrieval. "Get me the original document behind this summary", in the app and from Claude via MCP.** **(1) The link already existed.** Every summary's frontmatter carries `source: <verbatim original filename>` (`injectFrontmatter`, `files.js:720-791`), and `getWikiPage` already returns parsed frontmatter — so this release makes an existing link *usable and safe* rather than building a new one. **`computeSummarySlugFromSource` is NOT reversible** (lossy: strips extension, case and punctuation, truncates at 80 — `AI-as-a-Force-Multiplier_-Leaders-for-Industry-5.0 (1).pdf` → `ai-as-a-force-multiplier-leaders-for-industry-50-1`), so the filename must come from frontmatter and never from the slug. **(2) `frontmatter.source` is UNTRUSTED** — LLM-written, hand-editable, and delivered over Personal Sync and Shared Brain mirrors. This feature is "open the file this string names", so the string is hostile input. `resolveRawSource` in the new `src/brain/raw-store.js` is the single chokepoint, with an explicit **ENFORCED / NOT ENFORCED** docblock. **(3) It does NOT write a second containment check.** `resolveInsideWiki` turned out to be root-agnostic, so `raw-store.js` **imports** it with `rawPath(domain)` — two hand-maintained copies of a guard is precisely what produced the v3.2.0 CRITICAL, and a source guard asserts it cannot drift back. Layers: sanitiser (no separators, no control chars/NUL, ≤512 chars, basename equality, dot-only refused) → lexical containment → **physical `realpath` containment** (symlinked leaf OR ancestor, dangling → refused) → `lstat().isFile()` (refuses directories and **all** symlinks, even in-bounds ones — stricter than containment alone). **(4) THE TEST WAS GREEN WITH THE GUARD DISABLED — the v3.2.0 failure mode reproduced inside the test written to prevent it.** Disabling `isPhysicallyInside` left every symlink assertion passing (`Passed: 157 Failed: 0`), because the sanitiser refuses separators and `lstat` refuses symlink leaves, so **nothing ever reached layer 3**. The agent found this itself by breaking its own guard and being surprised. Rebuilt to exercise each layer independently: mutation A (physical check off) → 3 red, B (`lstat`→`stat`) → 3 red, F (all defences removed) → 12 red and a planted canary's contents leaked, proving the corpus can actually fail. **Layer 3 is redundant today and is documented as such** rather than claimed as active coverage — kept as the backstop if separator refusal ever regresses. **(5) A real corpus finding: a summary whose `source:` is a URL** (`medium.com/@talirezun`). Classified `external-source` and shown as **inert text — never linked, never fetched**; a test asserts **no HTTP client exists** in either module. Fetching an LLM-authored, sync-delivered string would make it an **SSRF primitive**. **(6) `get_raw_source` MCP tool** (17 → 18 tools) returns extracted, **byte**-capped text, or `text: null` + `text_unavailable: "binary"` — never binary bytes through the 400 KB budget. A byte-vs-character trap was caught pre-ship: a 120K *character* cap is up to **480 KB** of UTF-8, so the cap is in bytes with character-boundary-safe truncation (tested with CJK). **(7) Manifest** — append-only JSONL at `<domain>/wiki/.raw-manifest.jsonl`, deliberately **inside `wiki/` so it syncs** (`.health-dismissed.jsonl` precedent) while the blobs stay local and gitignored, so a second machine can say "this came from report.pdf, 2.4 MB — not on this machine" instead of having no idea it existed. Leaks nothing new: the filename is already in the synced `source:` field. A manifest failure can **never** fail an ingest. **(8) Real-domain measurement:** **44/44** raw files resolve with **0 false refusals** (combining diacritics, mojibake, parens, commas all survive). Of 85 summaries: **35 found · 45 missing · 4 no-source · 1 external-source**. **"Missing" is the NORMAL case** — `raw/` is gitignored, so a synced machine has the wiki and not the blobs; the UI says so plainly rather than implying corruption. Those 45 are pre-existing user data loss this feature *surfaces* rather than causes. **(9) `extractText` exported**; `reveal` uses the no-shell `execFile('open', ['-R', …])` pattern on a server-derived path only, refusing outright rather than best-effort opening a parent, and is macOS-only (501 elsewhere). **Verification: 40 offline suites** (39 → 40; `test-raw-store.js` **191 assertions**, `test-raw-source-ui.js` **59**), several consecutive green runs; **MCP stdout purity re-verified independently by the orchestrator** — every stdout line parses as JSON, **0 stderr bytes**, `get_raw_source` registered (a stray `console.log` in a module the MCP child imports corrupts JSON-RPC — the v2.5.3 bug, and `raw-store.js` is now on that path); browser-verified across found / missing / external / no-source, including an actual Finder reveal; frontend mutations red on anchoring the URL and on dropping `escHtml`. **Known, recorded not hidden:** `get_raw_source` has **never met a real model**, so whether Claude reaches for it appropriately versus `get_summary` is unmeasured; the manifest append has not run through a **live** ingest (call shape verified offline end-to-end); a symlinked `raw/` **directory itself** is permitted — a legitimate "raw library on an external drive" setup, a deliberate call; and `GET /api/wiki/:domain/page` was found to have **no `api-reference.md` entry at all**, predating this work. |
| `v3.4.0` | **Cancel now really cancels — 334 s → 63 ms, measured. An `AbortSignal` threaded queue → `ingestFile` → `generateText` → both provider SDKs.** **(1) The complaint was right and the original design was wrong.** v3.3.x honoured cancel only BETWEEN items, so cancelling during a 76 KB multi-phase ingest meant minutes of waiting **while still spending the user's API budget**, behind a button reading "Cancelling…". The maintainer reported it twice. Aborting mid-`ingestFile` does leave partial state — that was the reason for the original call — but it was weighed wrongly against a cancel that keeps billing. **(2) Measured, real Gemini, 150 KB source, multi-phase:** cancel POST acknowledged in 5 ms, **job terminal in 63 ms** (agent's own run on a 131 KB source: 74 ms), **zero LLM calls after the cancel**, versus a baseline of **334 s and 17 of 20 provider calls** for the same run continuing from that point. Verified independently by the orchestrator on a separate run. **(3) THE TRAP, and why this needed care: `ingest.js` has THREE recovery ladders** (Phase 1 outline retry, Phase 2 batch → page-by-page → **stub page**, single-pass → multi-phase). An abort caught by any of them would be "recovered" into stub pages **and keep spending** — the exact inverse of aborting, behind a green test. The catch sites were enumerated **mechanically** (brace-matching every `catch`/`.catch(` and testing whether its enclosing `try` contains an `await` on an LLM call), NOT by reading the flow — which is how the v3.2.0 symlink fix missed 2 of 7 handlers while its docblock claimed all of them. Result: **32 handler sites, 6 LLM-reachable, all 6 carry an abort re-throw placed FIRST**, before any recovery gate. The single-pass handler names the stakes in-code: *"A cancel must NEVER fall through to multi-phase: that ladder is the most expensive one in the file."* The explicit check is kept even though `isOutputTokenLimit` incidentally lets an abort through today — because a reworded message or a widened gate is one edit away, the same latent shape as the bare-`429` substring. **(4) Abort beats the OTHER escape hatches too:** checked before `is429`/`is503` in the retry catch, and before `isModelNotFound` in `callLLM` — a fallback-chain walk on cancel would have issued up to **5 more calls**. `sleep()` is now abortable, so a cancel during a 60 s backoff returns immediately instead of serving out the delay. **(5) A bug nobody was looking for:** the worker loop's "no pending items left → `finishJobDone`" branch runs BEFORE its cancel check, so **cancelling on the LAST file would have reported the batch as `done`**. The cancel now settles from inside the item. **(6) New item status `cancelled`** ("Stopped", amber, distinct from `failed`=error and `skipped`=never attempted), added to `ITEM_TERMINAL` — omit that and the v3.3.0 no-item-lost tripwire refuses to settle and the job hangs, which a mutation confirmed. Carries an honest message: *"Stopped partway through — some pages may already have been written. Re-ingest this file to complete it."* **Deliberately conservative wording** — in practice every page write happens AFTER Phase 2, so a mid-Phase-2 cancel writes nothing. **Nothing is deleted or rolled back**: partial pages may have merged into pages that already existed, so cleanup would be destructive; re-ingest is idempotent and completes the file. Caveat documented: the cancelled item leaves its source in `raw/`, so re-ingesting needs **Overwrite**. **(7) Pause deliberately does NOT abort** — it keeps its lossless semantics (finish the current file, then stop). Pause = stop cleanly, cancel = stop now. **(8) The post-LLM phase is deliberately NOT cancellable** (`writePage` loop → `syncSummaryEntities` → index → log): local, fast, free, and interrupting there would trade milliseconds for pages-on-disk with no index rows. **(9) TWO mutations stayed GREEN and were chased down rather than reported as coverage** — the Phase-2 gate alone, and either `llm.js` abort check alone, are each masked by a second layer; proven by pairing them (both pairs go RED), so those individual lines are defence-in-depth, not independently load-bearing. Probing the SDK directly also found `APIUserAbortError` carries `name: 'Error'`, so the `signal.aborted` disjunct — not the name — is what tags it. **(10) Every "no ladder fired" assertion is paired with a control run proving the ladder DOES fire for a token-limit error**, so a green cannot be a test that never reached the ladder. **(11) STALE COPY, caught by the docs pass:** the frontend still rendered *"Cancelling — finishing the current file first, then stopping"* and `toWire`'s docblock still described the between-items behaviour — both leftovers from v3.3.1 that now misinformed in the OPPOSITE direction. Corrected to *"Cancelling — stopping the current file now."*, and the docblock rewritten to carry the history explicitly rather than silently. **Verification: 38 offline suites** (37 → 38; new `test-ingest-abort.js`, **84 assertions**), 10 consecutive green runs; byte-identity of prompts, pages and warnings **with and without a signal** asserted directly, and `test-ingest-prompt-slimming.js` (488 assertions pinning prompt content) still green — so the single-file ingest path is provably unchanged when no signal is passed. **Known:** `test-ingest-abort.js` §7 binds an ephemeral loopback HTTP server to exercise the real 429 ladder without a paid call — the first OFFLINE suite to do so. |
| `v3.3.1` | **Batch ingest UX — four defects found by the maintainer in the first ninety seconds of real use, none of which three audit rounds or two live provider runs had caught.** They were all testing whether it *works*; he tested whether it is *usable*. **(1) "Cancel doesn't work" — it did, but nothing said so.** Cancel takes effect **between items** (aborting mid-`ingestFile` would leave partial wiki state, so that stays), and the backend already tracked `cancelRequested` in `_controlFlags` — but **`toWire` never exposed it**, so a polling tab saw only `status: running` while a 76 KB multi-phase file ground on for minutes. Verified before/after on a real batch: the same nine seconds of polls that used to say only `running` now carry `cancelRequested: true`, then settle `cancelled` with the in-flight item `done` and the rest never started. Flags are read **live at serialisation time** (an in-process Map against an on-disk job) so a second tab sees the same pending state, are **strict booleans never `undefined`**, and are **never persisted** — a cancel must not survive a restart as a booby trap on a job the user later resumes. A mutation stubbing the live read to a snapshot goes RED, pinning the ordering and not merely the field's presence. **(2) THE CROSS-AGENT SEAM, caught by reading the source rather than trusting either side.** `settleAsCancelled` emits its snapshot at the `break` while `_controlFlags.delete` runs later in the loop's `finally` (`:1848`), so a real wire frame carries **`status:'cancelled'` AND `cancelRequested:true`** — and if that is the last frame, a renderer keyed purely on the flag shows *"Cancelling — finishing the current file first…"* **permanently on a finished batch**: a worse bug than the one being fixed, with a Dismiss button beside a message claiming work is ongoing. Terminal status now always beats a stale pending flag, behind **two independent guards**, mutation-proven by defeating **both at once** (defeating one leaves the other catching it — real defense in depth, not redundant code). **(3) Selections REPLACED instead of accumulating** — the single worst assumption in the original design, and mine, not the implementation's: "select 2+ files" quietly assumed a batch is assembled in ONE action. Real users build one incrementally. Picking from a second folder discarded the first; dropping a second file replaced the first; **both reported symptoms were one root cause.** Selections now accumulate across any number of picker and drop events, with per-file remove, **Add more files**, **Clear all**, name+size dedup (re-picking a folder cannot double-queue), and a re-estimate on every change. Batch mode is **sticky** — removing down to one file stays a batch; only Clear all returns to the single-file flow, which remains byte-untouched. **(4) A terminal batch could not be dismissed** — reloading the browser was the only escape, on a screen used repeatedly. Dismiss is UI-only (no server call), gated purely on terminal status from a structurally separate render branch so it cannot appear while a batch is live. **(5)** `$0.0000 spent` mid-flight read as "this is free"; an in-progress zero now says *"spend so far: pending first file"* while a terminal zero stays a real final tally. **Two false signals caught inside the work itself, both worth recording:** a test asserted against `items[0].status === 'pending'` because `startOrResumeJob` writes `running` before the worker picks up an item — so it never reproduced the reported moment at all; and `String.replace(fn, STRING)` silently collapsed `$$` → `$` in a mutation's replacement text, producing a RED **for the wrong reason** (a syntax break, not the intended behavioural one), found only by inspecting the re-extracted mutated source rather than trusting the red. All 12 call sites moved to the `$`-safe function-replacer form. **Verification: 37 offline suites, 5 consecutive full runs green** with nothing being edited (`test-ingest-queue.js` **343 assertions**, `test-ingest-queue-frontend.js` **322**); every fix mutation-proven RED; all four reported flows reproduced and re-verified live in-browser — 2 files from folder A + 2 from folder B → **4 queued**; drop 1 then drop 2 → **3 queued, the first preserved**; same file picked twice → **queued once**; cancel mid-batch → immediate *"Cancelling…"*, then a terminal panel with **no lingering text**, then Dismiss with **no page reload**. Single-file ingest unaffected. Docs updated in the same release. |
| `v3.3.0` | **Track 3 — batch ingest queue. Select many files, walk away, resume across a crash or an app update. The single-file path is byte-untouched.** **(1) The feature.** Server-owned, disk-persisted queue at `getIngestQueueDir()` with an atomically-written manifest per job; **strictly sequential** worker; cost estimate as a confirm gate before anything uploads; pause/resume/cancel; optional budget cap; aggregate report plus the free local `scanWiki` at the end. **1 file still routes to the existing, unchanged `submitIngest()`; 2+ files route to the queue** — the most-used feature in the app was not rewritten for tidiness. **(2) The queue lives OUTSIDE `domains/`, and that is load-bearing.** The plan specified `<domainsDir>/.ingest-queue/`, but `getDomainsDir()` **is Personal Sync's git work-tree** (`--work-tree=getDomainsDir()`), so staged PDFs would have been committed and pushed to the user's GitHub repo. This project has shipped that exact class twice (`.DS_Store` v3.0.16, `.write-lock` v3.0.15); routing through `paths.js` removes the class rather than adding a third gitignore rule (both defensive rules added anyway). **(3) Duplicates are decided ONCE, at create time — a per-item check would have broken the feature's whole point.** `ingestFile` writes `raw/<name>` as its FIRST step, so an item interrupted mid-ingest already looks like a duplicate; a runtime check would mark the resumed item `skipped` and **silently drop the very file the crash interrupted, reporting success.** Caught in design review, before it was built. **(4) CRITICAL, found by BOTH auditors independently: concurrent `/start` ran multiple worker loops.** The claim was read, then three `await`s ran (`assertDomainUsable` → `listDomains` + a `CLAUDE.md` read per domain), then it was set. Four simultaneous POSTs all passed. Measured: **3 items ingesting at once, 6 `ingestFile` calls for 3 items, one document written to `log.md` three times**, and the job reporting `done` while two items were still `running`. `acquireFileLock` does not save it — `existsSync` → `writeFileAtomic`, no `O_EXCL`, and `isPidAlive(ownPid)` is true for a same-process caller. Reachable by double-clicking Resume or opening two tabs. Fixed by claiming the mutex **synchronously**, with a second independent in-flight counter around the one `ingestFileImpl` call — the invariant asserted where it means it, not where the caller arrived. `createJob` had the same TOCTOU (3 concurrent creates → **3 active jobs on disk**). **(5) A rate limit pauses the batch; a bad file fails alone.** `llm.js` already retries 429/503 four times, so a 429 reaching the queue means the provider said stop; continuing would fail items 10–30 identically in 90 seconds and report "done" having burned 20 files. Classification is a **structured tag** set at the throw site in `llm.js` (message text unchanged — tests assert on it), not a substring test: a bare `/\b429\b/` **also matches ingest's genuine `"yielded only 429 characters of text"`**, which is the v3.0.16 `friendlyError` CRITICAL shape exactly. Backed by a **consecutive-failure circuit breaker** (3 in a row → pause) that bounds the harm *regardless of whether classification was right* — and which then fired correctly on a real live failure. **(6) No item can be lost.** A job could reach `done` with an item still `running` — appearing in **none** of the UI's three buckets while its row showed a "Running" pill on a finished batch. Three sites could each have caught it and none did. Fixed as **one invariant in one place**: `reclaimStrandedItems` at the top of every loop iteration, inside the settle chokepoint, and on boot recovery for **every** job — plus a tripwire refusing `done` while any item is non-terminal. The UI independently buckets every item so `sum(known) + sum(other) === items.length` by construction. **(7) Never auto-start spend.** A crashed job recovers to `paused`, never running. Verified across **8 SIGKILL offsets** straddling item boundaries: manifest always parseable, zero stray `.tmp-*`, no item lost or re-run, staged files preserved. **(8) Wire leaks.** `toWire` was a `...rest` spread (a planted 48 MB manifest came back whole) and raw fs errors carried absolute paths through `item.error` and a create-route 500. Now an explicit **allow-list** with length caps, and a path scrubber — whose **first version leaked everything after the first space**, so `/Users/alice smith/Google Drive/…` survived almost intact: the one thing it existed to hide. The bridge width in the rewrite is **measured, not guessed** (a fixture table: `{0,4}` is the only value with 0 leaks AND 0 prose damage) and mutation-proven in **both** directions, so moving it either way goes red. **(9) The budget cap REFUSES rather than degrades.** `getModelPrice()` returns null for an unpriced model, and both charge branches then added 0 forever — a **$0.01 cap ran a full 6-file batch** with `spentUsd` frozen at 0 while `spendIsEstimated: true` claimed the estimate was in use. `createJob` now 400s a cap it cannot enforce. Its docblock carries an explicit **NOT ENFORCED** list: the fallback charge is approximate and can under-count, so what the cap guarantees is that spend is *tracked and bounded*, not that `budgetUsd` is never exceeded. **(10) Three times in one feature, ONE measurement became a general claim.** The `basis` string quoted "~416k input tokens" while its own arithmetic computed **1,051,296** (2.5× its own quote); then "roughly twice as much on a mature wiki" — true only at the 80 KB cap, where it was sampled. The real curve against a 3,336-page domain is **39.4× (2 KB) → 2.1× (80 KB)**, and there is a **step discontinuity at ~15,000 chars** where a source crosses from one AI call to multi-phase and input tokens roughly double. The generic multiple is gone and **not replaced by another constant**: the estimator runs the same files against an empty domain and quotes the ratio it computed **for that batch**. Tests pin the *shape* — the multiple must MOVE with document size — which no hardcoded constant survives. **(11) A dead Resume button was hiding as a "test flake."** The suite failed ~2 in 60, always `testRateLimitPauses`. The suspected cause in the brief was wrong; measuring found `settleAsPaused` publishes `paused` to disk **before** the loop releases its claim, so a Resume in that window hit `if (_runningJobId === jobId) return job` and **silently did nothing — HTTP 200, no work.** A real user-facing bug, not a test artifact. **(12) `npm test` reported the whole suite as `⏭ skip`** — passing but counted as NOT RUN, invisible to CI — because an assertion label contained the word SKIPPED. Introduced and caught within the same round. **Verification: 37 offline suites** (35 → 37; `test-ingest-queue.js` **317 assertions**, `test-ingest-queue-frontend.js` **225**), **10 consecutive full `npm test` runs green** with nothing being edited (baseline had been 2 failures in 60). **TWO independent adversarial audits** — one reading the code, one attacking a live server — plus a **re-audit of the fix delta**, which attacked every headline fix by mutation and could not defeat any. **~20 guards mutation-proven RED**; two that stayed green (`releaseClaim` in the failed-start catch — a single bad request would 409 every future batch for the process lifetime — and `reclaimStrandedItems` in `settleJob`) were covered afterwards. **Live on real Gemini AND real Anthropic** against an isolated copy of the 3,336-page `articles` wiki: **16/16 each**. Gemini 49 pages/84 s/**$0.017225**; Anthropic 26 pages/174 s/**$0.127088**; Health scan at scale 6 s and 3 s; a too-short file failed alone and the batch still reached `done`; a duplicate was skipped at create time **across a server restart and a provider switch**; zero path leaks in any body or SSE frame. Estimate accuracy is provider-dependent — **103.1% of `usdHigh` on Gemini, 66.8% on Anthropic** — so the range is documented as an estimate, not a ceiling, and the arithmetic was deliberately NOT tuned to fit either sample. **KNOWN, NOT FIXED (recorded, not hidden):** a filename containing a raw `"` is **silently dropped** (HTTP 200, `ok:true`, absent from `rejected`) because the multipart parse fails upstream of the module — a browser cannot trigger it (WHATWG escapes `"` → `%22`) but a scripted client can; a NUL byte is parse-fatal for the same reason and no file picker can produce one; `acquireFileLock` **double-grants** (5/5 for two in-process callers, pre-existing) so the same-domain guard against a parallel single-file ingest is **UI-level only**; and `appendLog` (`files.js:1163`) reads `log.md` unguarded, unlike `readIndex` beside it, so a domain missing that file completes a full ingest — real spend, pages on disk — then throws `ENOENT` at the last step (pre-existing, identical on the single-file route, unreachable via any documented path since `createDomain()` writes it and `docs/domains.md:75` tells manual users to). **Docs updated in the same release** — user-guide, api-reference, ingestion-pipeline §10g, README, CONTRIBUTING (whose suite count was **already stale** at 50/33 vs the real 54/37). |
| `v3.2.0` | **Redesign Phase 2 — four real views behind `/next`, plus a symlink escape that could DELETE files outside the wiki. The shipping app is byte-untouched: verified empty diff on `app.js`/`index.html`/`styles.css`/`markdown.js` at every round.** **(1) FOUR views wired to live data** — Chat (domain-scoped per R4), Domains with the Health panel inside it, Settings (5 sections), Sync — all presentation over backends that already existed. Health-in-domain shows **real cost estimates in the button label** (`Rescue 1 orphan $0.0003`) from the free estimate endpoints; the design's cost-before-spend rule works end-to-end, not mocked. **(2) SECURITY — a symlink escape reachable through the normal UI.** `resolveInsideWiki` was **purely lexical** (`path.resolve` + `path.relative`, no `realpath`, no `lstat`): it refused a path whose STRING escaped, never what the path pointed AT. Reproduced: a symlink at `wiki/entities/leak.md` served a file from outside; a symlinked DIRECTORY escaped wholesale. **Worse, it was not a read leak — `health.js`'s destructive handlers shared the flaw**: with the fix reverted, a test genuinely DELETED a real file outside the wiki. Reachable via git (which materialises mode-120000 entries) through a Personal Sync pull, a Shared Brain mirror, or a user symlinking folders into their Obsidian vault — a documented workflow. **The write side had been hardened (`atomic-write.js`, `sharedbrain.js` both `lstat`); the read side never was, and `atomic-write` only checks the FINAL component so a symlinked ANCESTOR walked past it.** Fixed with a physical `realpath` containment check, and `health.js` now IMPORTS the single chokepoint instead of keeping its own copy — two hand-maintained copies of a guard is what produced this. Cost: `scanWiki('articles')` 546 ms → 649 ms. **(3) A FOURTH escape found in audit, worse than the three known: `previewSemanticDuplicateMerge` had NO validation at all** — not the slug regex, not the folder allow-list — and `issue` is the verbatim POST body of `/semantic-dupes/preview`. `keepFolder: "../../.."` returned 4 KB of any file, **with no symlink required**, so it was reachable on a default install. It drifted because it carried its own COPY of its sibling's validation. **(4) Structural fix, not four patches:** `wikiFile()` is now the only way `health.js` builds an absolute path and `resolveInsideWiki` is called exactly once, inside it. The enumeration was derived mechanically — all 53 fs call sites extracted, every path identifier traced to its declaration — not by re-reading handlers, which is what failed the round before. **(5) `npm test` was BLIND to whether Wiki Health works.** All three suites with positive coverage of its destructive functions are in the **LIVE** manifest and need API keys, so they never run in `npm test` or the free CI job; offline, the only positive assertion on the file was one hyphen-variant scan. **A refactor could break every Fix button and CI would stay green — and that is exactly what happened**: a `removeSlug` dropped from a destructure left `previewSemanticDuplicateMerge` throwing `ReferenceError` on EVERY valid input, behind 35 green suites, because all five fixtures fed it invalid input and asserted a throw. New **§8d, 46 offline assertions** on every mutating export; two functions had zero coverage anywhere. It immediately caught a missing import in its own new code. **(6) A source guard that had to be rebuilt twice.** §8c pins that `health.js` cannot regain raw path construction. v1 was defeated by `const base = wikiDir` (an injected ungated `rm` handler kept the suite green); rebuilt on **declaration-site provenance**, then the rebuild leaked twice more to `wikiFile(…) || issue.sourceFile` and its ternary form because the producer test was a PREFIX match. Now classifies every binding form and **fails on any it cannot classify** — an allow-list silently permits what it forgot. 26 bypass shapes, all caught. **It is name-scoped, not scope-aware** (no JS parser in the dependency tree) so it is deliberately sound in the SAFE direction — false positives, never false negatives — and the docblock now carries explicit **ENFORCED** and **NOT ENFORCED** lists. **(7) FRONTEND: mount staleness, fixed structurally on the third attempt.** `setSidebar`/`setMain`/`openReader` now take a mount token and **fail CLOSED**; all seven views pass one, so omission is impossible rather than merely discouraged. v1 used per-view booleans (2 of 4 views forgot); v2 added a shell token that both consumers then **read live from a module variable the next mount overwrites** — defeating it in exactly the leave-and-return case the shell's own comment says the design exists for. Concrete harm: send a chat message, click Domains, click back — the answer landed in whichever conversation was open then. **(8) User-facing bugs found in audit:** Sync stuck on "Pushing…" **forever** after every push/pull/sync (the `finally` refreshed state without re-rendering; every button disabled, no way out but leaving the view); opening Domains **froze the entire server for ~15 s** (the semantic-dupe estimate fired on view entry — a concurrent request measured 12.5 s → 0.01 s after the fix); the reader **re-opened itself** after Esc if the fetch was still in flight; a new conversation's **first answer never appeared**; **a failed API-key save showed nothing at all** — the error was set and then erased by the reload in the same `finally`, and fixing it exposed a second dormant bug where that error branch replaced the entire provider list. **(9) Backend additions:** `getDomainStats` gains per-type `pageCounts` + `pageCounts.other`, and a bulk `GET /api/domains/stats`; new `GET /api/wiki/:domain/page` + `src/brain/wiki-read.js` returns one page plus **backlinks, which did not exist anywhere in the app** (only in the MCP process) — previously `GET /api/wiki/:domain` returned EVERY page with full content, 14 MB on `articles`. Implemented independently of `mcp/graph.js` rather than importing it, since MCP is a stdio child where a stray `console.log` corrupts JSON-RPC. Link resolution **replicates `health.js`'s existence check exactly, quirks included**, so the reader can never disagree with the Health tab. `pageCount` was narrowed and then **restored** to its recursive meaning — the narrowing made the shipping app's delete dialog under-report ("will delete 4 pages" while deleting 7). **(10) A production bug found adjacent to the work:** every Shared Brain mirror displayed as **`---`**, because `getDomainStats` trusted CLAUDE.md's first line and a mirror's first line is the `---` frontmatter delimiter, which is non-empty so the slug fallback never fired. **Verification: 35 offline suites green** (33 → 35; `test-wiki-page.js` 208 assertions incl. 7 traversal vectors, `test-domain-stats.js` 88 with a `find(1)` oracle); **four adversarial audit rounds**, each finding real issues and twice finding the previous round's FIX defective; browser-verified across all 7 views in both themes with zero console errors; secret scan 0 findings. **Known limitation, recorded honestly:** `settings.js`/`sync.js` were fully rewritten while untracked, so "did anything quietly disappear" is unanswerable from git — reviewed as new code, every reachable control works, and the one structural difference (settings' `onEnter` no longer returns a teardown, whose only job was a boolean that no longer exists) is named rather than hidden. |
| `v3.1.3` | **Redesign groundwork: a parallel `/next` shell, plus the two backend gaps the new UI needs. Invisible to users — the shipping app is byte-untouched.** Tali delivered a complete redesign (`Design/design_handoff_app_redesign`) and design system (`Design/the_curator_design_system`, 26 components + 7 token files + 15 specimen cards) — both PRIVATE, in Google Drive, never in this repo. It is an **information-architecture restructure, not a reskin**: seven horizontal tabs become a chat-centric rail (Chat · Domains · Shared Brain · Agent memory · Ingest, footer Sync · Settings), Wiki becomes an Esc-dismissible overlay, Health moves inside the domain, and Shared Brain stops being a Settings flag and becomes a tier. **(1) DECISION — chat stays DOMAIN-SCOPED.** The design specified one cross-domain conversation with domains as filter chips. Tali overrode it on scaling grounds, and that single decision **deleted the largest blocker in the redesign** along with all four of its sub-problems: page-identity collisions (two domains both holding `entities/openai.md`), domain-qualified citations, the unanswered multi-domain system-prompt question, and a conversations-storage migration carrying a real trap — sync's work-tree is `getDomainsDir()`, so a conversation root outside `domains/` would have silently dropped chat history out of GitHub sync. It also moved chat from "blocked" to a **free surface** (6 of 7 screens are now presentation over working backends). Recorded as revisitable: the scope bar can gain multi-select if a page cache makes retrieval viable at 5k+ candidates — with measurements, never on vibes. **(2) DECISION — vanilla, not React.** The 26 components ship as `.jsx`, but the coupling is thin (no hooks, no state, no context; props in → markup out, inline styles referencing CSS custom properties) and every real value lives in the token files, so a port is mechanical and the no-build-step architecture survives. **(3) DECISION — Agent memory ships its rail slot with an honest empty state**, preserving the *your brain → your team's brain → your agents' brain* thesis the IA is built on, rather than promising a date. **(4) The `/next` shell** (`src/public/next/**`, new): 7 token files byte-identical from the bundle, three-column layout, both themes at parity via `data-theme` (the tokens carry NO `prefers-color-scheme` — the shell sets the attribute), and a real **`navigate()`/`onEnter` registry**. The design's hard rules — *an overlay must never survive a view change*, *rail selection clears the reader and closes the model picker* — are enforced **inside the registry**, so a view cannot opt out; verified live in-browser (open the reader, click a rail item, it closes). **A correction to the gap analysis worth recording:** it framed the registry as a prerequisite REFACTOR of `app.js`. It is not — with a parallel shell the registry lives in the NEW frontend, so `app.js` (6,788 lines, 80 top-level `getElementById`) gets **zero changes** and the blank-page-to-every-user failure mode v3.1.0 hardened against cannot fire while building this. **Verified: `git diff` on `app.js`/`index.html`/`styles.css` is empty; `src/server.js` is +12 lines.** **(5) Fonts deliberately NOT linked.** `tokens/fonts.css` `@import`s Instrument Sans + IBM Plex Mono from Google Fonts; the bundle's own CONTENTS.md says self-host before shipping, and a local-first, privacy-positioned app must not phone Google on page load. Copied but unlinked; a `fonts-local.css` system stack stands in until webfonts are self-hosted. **(6) `getDomainStats` gains `pageCounts {entities, concepts, summaries}`** (strictly additive; `readdir` only, deliberately mirroring `health.js`'s `listMd()` so the counts mean the same thing as `scanWiki`'s without its full-content read) plus a bulk `GET /api/domains/stats` so a sidebar needs one call, not one per domain. Verified on the real `articles` domain: 600 + 2,651 + 83 = **3,334**, exactly matching `pageCount`. **Flagged, not fixed:** `pageCount` recurses the whole `wiki/` tree while `pageCounts.*` reads only the three canonical folders — identical on a well-formed wiki, divergent only if a file is hand-dropped outside them; pinned by a regression test. **(7) `GET /api/wiki/:domain/page?path=…` + `src/brain/wiki-read.js`** (new) — the ONLY new backend the redesign needs now. Returns one page (frontmatter, title, type, body) plus **backlinks, which did not exist anywhere in the app** (only in the MCP process). Previously `GET /api/wiki/:domain` returned EVERY page with full content — 14 MB on `articles`. **Implemented independently of `mcp/graph.js` rather than importing it**: MCP runs as a stdio child where a stray `console.log` corrupts the JSON-RPC stream (the v2.5.3 bug), so coupling the app to it in that direction invites exactly that class of failure. **Link resolution deliberately replicates `health.js`'s existence check EXACTLY** — including its quirks (a bare `[[slug]]` never resolves to a summary; a non-canonical `[[entities/foo]]` still counts as a backlink though Health flags it as a style violation; cross-folder duplicate slugs stay ambiguous because `health.js`'s `incomingLinks` map is keyed on bare slug) — so the reader can never disagree with the Health tab about whether a link works. Cache is signature-based (`readdir`+`stat` → `{count, maxMtimeMs}`), **stronger than MCP's file-count-only invalidation**, which would miss a re-ingest editing a page in place — a gap that matters because the app is long-running while MCP is fresh-process-per-session. Verified on real data: 231 backlinks resolved, cold 653 ms → warm 41 ms, and 3 traversal attempts refused with 400. **Verification: 35 offline suites green** (33 → 35; new `test-domain-stats.js` 54 assertions, `test-wiki-page.js` 55 including 7 path-traversal vectors); browser-verified on an isolated :3394 instance (layout, all 7 rail items, active state, both themes persisting across reload, overlay close-on-navigate) **and confirmed `/` still serves the shipping app unchanged**; secret scan 0 findings in new content. **Known deviations, recorded:** rail icons are hand-drawn stroke SVGs standing in for Lucide (no build step, and a CDN would contradict local-first) with a comment mapping each to its Lucide original; `--sidebar-w: 248px` in the token file contradicts the 272px every layout hardcodes — 272 used, token untouched. |
| `v3.1.2` | **Dependency security pass — 10 advisories → 0, plus the one the version bump would have hidden.** Prompted by an unsolicited automated PR (#7, OrbisAI Security) proposing `overrides: {"fast-uri": "4.1.2"}` for CVE-2026-13676. **Declined, and the reason is the finding:** `npm audit` reported **10 vulnerabilities (5 high, 5 moderate)**, not one; the PR addressed **one of the five then-visible** `fast-uri` advisories, pinned an already-superseded version, and forced a **semver-major** override outside `ajv`'s declared `^3.0.1` — when **every one of the 10 resolved inside already-declared ranges**. The lockfile was simply stale: `ajv@8.18.0` had permitted the safe `fast-uri` all along. `npm audit fix` (no `--force`) closed all 10 with **neither SDK touched**. **(1) `fast-uri` 3.1.0 → 3.1.6** (transitive via `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk → ajv`), plus `overrides: {"fast-uri": "^3.1.6"}` as a floor that survives lockfile regeneration while staying inside ajv's range. **— CORRECTED 2026-08-30, and the correction is the SAME TRAP this row already describes one item below.** This row originally recorded *"5 high advisories, ranges to `<3.1.5`"*. Re-measured against `fastify/fast-uri`'s own repository advisories there are **NINE**, every one of them **HIGH**, and the 3.x ranges reach **`< 3.1.6`** — `>= 3.0.0, < 3.1.6` on two of them, `>= 3.1.2, < 3.1.6` and `>= 3.1.3, < 3.1.6` on two more. **THE ORIGINAL FIGURE WAS NOT CARELESS — IT WAS WHAT THE TOOL REPORTED, AND THE TOOL IS STILL WRONG TODAY.** `npm audit` reads the GLOBAL GitHub Advisory DB, which held five `fast-uri` entries then and **still holds exactly five now**; the other four were published to the REPOSITORY on 2026-08-23 — the same day this release shipped — and a week later are **still not ingested**. So `npm audit` prints `found 0 vulnerabilities` while four High advisories affecting `< 3.1.6` stand open against anything below it. **THE SHIPPED PIN IS AND ALWAYS WAS CORRECT: `^3.1.6` clears all nine**, which is why nothing was ever exposed — the defect was entirely in the RECORDED RANGE. Anyone re-deriving the pin from `<3.1.5`, or "tidying" the override down to match the documented bound, would reopen **exactly four High advisories with a green `npm audit`** — precisely the "green `0 vulnerabilities` scorecard over an unchanged attack surface" this row describes for multer, turned back on the row's own numbers. Measured, not asserted: `gh api /repos/fastify/fast-uri/security-advisories` returns **9, all `high`**; `gh api "/advisories?ecosystem=npm&affects=fast-uri"` returns **5**. The multer finding below, and the reasoning about staying inside ajv's declared range, are unaffected and stand as written. **(2) `multer` 2.1.1 → ^2.2.0** — the only DIRECT dependency affected, and the one the PR missed entirely; it handles every file upload at [routes/ingest.js](src/routes/ingest.js). **(3) THE FINDING: the multer bump silences `npm audit` while leaving the DoS completely open.** GHSA-72gw-mp4g-v24j (unbounded allocation from deeply nested field names) ships only the MECHANISM: the check in `multer/lib/make-middleware.js` is gated behind `hasOwnProperty(limits, 'fieldNestingDepth')` and the documented default is **`Infinity`**, so the option is inert unless explicitly set. The advisory says upgrade **and** configure. Shipping the bump alone would have produced a green `0 vulnerabilities` scorecard over an unchanged attack surface — and the green tick is precisely what stops anyone looking again. Closed with `fieldNestingDepth: 1` on the ingest multer instance. **Value derived, not guessed:** every submitter was enumerated — `app.js`'s ingest handler (the only in-app caller) sends `domain`/`file`/`overwrite`, the documented contract in `api-reference.md` is `domain`+`file`, the MCP never POSTs to `/api/ingest` at all (it imports `src/brain` directly), and no bracketed field name exists anywhere in `src/`, `scripts/` or `mcp/`. `file` goes through busboy's file event and is never depth-checked, so the measured requirement is depth **0**; 1 is one level of headroom and allocates exactly one nested object. A load-bearing comment records why the option is not redundant tidiness, since a future maintainer would otherwise delete it. **Verification:** 33 offline suites green; `npm audit` **10 → 0**; MCP re-probed (17 tools, stdout pure JSON, 0 stderr bytes) because `ajv`/`fast-uri` sit under the MCP SDK — the exact path touched; upload path verified end-to-end on an isolated server — flat fields **200 + SSE**, `a[b]` (depth 1) **200**, `a[b][c]` (depth 2) **400 JSON** `"Field name nesting too deep"` via the existing `isMulterError` fallthrough, `a[b][c]…` ×200 **400**, `.docx` **400**, 51MB **413**, and multer's temp dir empty after 7 uploads including 3 mid-stream aborts (which also exercises the cleanup path of the *other* multer advisory, GHSA-3p4h-7m6x-2hcm, genuinely fixed by the bump). Independently re-verified by the maintainer on a throwaway port. **Known residual, stated rather than hidden:** `fieldNestingDepth` is a per-`multer()` option and this is the only instance in the codebase — a second upload route would not inherit it, and no lint or test would catch that omission. |
| `v3.1.1` | **Shared Brain: a dead delta path revived, the data-loss hole that revival opened closed, and the `sharedbrain*` relicense.** Track 2 residue — Phase 5's test programme (5.1–5.12) shipped in v3.0.6, so the only GA gate left is the human beta pilot (5.13). **(1) `loadPriorContent()` was 100% DEAD.** Its pathspec was `domains/<domain>/wiki/<path>`, but Personal Sync's work-tree IS the domains dir (`sync.js` passes `--work-tree=getDomainsDir()`), so tracked paths carry NO `domains/` prefix — verified against the real repo, **0 of 5,249** tracked files matched. It returned `null` on every call, so `isNew` was always true and **every push re-submitted a page's entire body as new facts**. Fixed (prefix dropped, `--literal-pathspecs` added). Verified live on **real Gemini AND Anthropic**: a page with 3 existing facts + 1 new one now yields `new_facts` containing **exactly the new fact and none of the three unchanged** — on both providers. **(2) The roadmap's cost claim was WRONG, and measuring it changed the framing.** The plan said the dead function cost "degraded quality AND wasted tokens". The token half does not hold: reviving it makes each delta prompt **larger** by exactly the prior version's byte length — **+69.5%** measured across 25 real `articles` pages (independently reproduced at +41% on a synthetic prior). This is a **quality win that COSTS input tokens at push time**; the payoff is downstream, because synthesis consumes `new_facts` alone and without a prior the collective must dedup and Jaccard-pair-scan a full page copy every cycle. **(3) Reviving it opened a NEW silent-data-loss path, caught before it shipped.** A page in `pending_retry`/`permanent_skip` failed its last push, so NONE of its body reached the collective; diffing it against the pre-failure git version would contribute only the change since, and the never-contributed body would be dropped permanently — making Decision 3's retry path lossy, the exact inverse of its purpose. New `neverContributedPages` set, frozen off `connection` BEFORE the un-skip loop mutates the working copies. The conservative branch's failure mode is a redundant full contribution, absorbed by exact-string dedup; a gap is unrecoverable. **(4) HIGH, caught in audit — the fix closed the reported case and left the purpose broken.** The docblock argued at length that `--literal-pathspecs` turns a stray empty result into "a clean null". It does — **only for the glob-superset case it tested**. A genuinely 0-byte page still returned `''`, because `git show` legitimately emits empty stdout and that path contains no glob for the flag to engage. Downstream, `isNew` is a `=== null` check while `buildDeltaPrompt` branches on truthiness: the model was told **"IS NEW PAGE: false"** while shown the new-page block with no prior, under an instruction defining `stable_facts` as "facts UNCHANGED from prior" — and **`stable_facts` is written and read by NOTHING** (synthesis takes `new_facts` only), so anything routed there is gone permanently. Reachable: **Obsidian creates 0-byte files on "new note" and the documented vault root IS `domains/<d>/wiki/`**; 2 zero-byte `.md` files already exist in the maintainer's own domains, 1 tracked in `.knowledge-git` HEAD. Closed with `if (!content) return null`. Whitespace-only content is deliberately NOT caught (truthy → both consumers already agree). **(5) A flag that has now been over-claimed twice.** With the falsy guard in place, removing `--literal-pathspecs` no longer fails anything — the guard converts the glob case too. Every literal-vs-glob shape was probed, including delete-and-recreate where the two shas genuinely differ (`663daf5` vs `c4a103e`): **the shas diverge, the content never does**, because a globbed `git log -1` can only select a commit at or after the one that last touched the page and `git show <sha>:<path>` is snapshot-based. The flag is KEPT (it removes a parsing mode from an externally-sourced value) but the assertion was relabelled to what it actually proves, and the docblock now carries an explicit "do not over-credit this flag" note. **(6) HIGH — live coverage that was fictitious.** A new live section proving a real model handles the diff prompt passed on both providers while **silently running Anthropic twice**: `__setUserDataDirOverride` redirects ALL user-data paths including `.curator-config.json`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` exists only there (not in `.env`), and `getProviderInfo('gemini')` does not fail without a key — **it falls through to whichever provider still has one**. The tell was byte-identical output from two different models. Fixed by seeding the isolated config, plus a resolved-provider assertion so a silent fallback now fails loudly. **(7) MCP/app `DOMAINS_PATH` precedence aligned** (`mcp/storage/local.js`): env ranked ABOVE config in the MCP and BELOW it in the app. History shows **no design reason** — the MCP resolver was written independently in v2.3.0 and never cross-checked; v3.1.0 spotted the mismatch and froze it rather than fixing it. Now `CLI arg → config → DOMAINS_PATH → default`, identical to `getDomainsDir()`. Blast radius is narrow (`routes/mcp.js:35` always passes `--domains-path`). **Documented, not fixed:** the CLI arg is a SNAPSHOT taken at wizard-generation time, so a stale `claude_desktop_config.json` can still diverge — the docs' new "can never disagree" claim was an overclaim and was corrected; and MCP **reads** resolve through this adapter while MCP **writes** go through `files.js → getDomainsDir()`, which never sees the CLI arg (pre-existing; now noted in the docblock). **(8) Test-suite credential exposure closed, twice.** `test-sharedbrain-routes.js` spawns a real server and isolated only `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR`, so it resolved the maintainer's REAL `.sync-config.json` — **a live GitHub PAT** — and one stray Sync call would have pushed to their real repo. Migrated to `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR`; the on-disk `.pre-test-backup` dance is REMOVED (the audit rated this a net improvement — the old code actively `unlink`ed real credential files; the new code never writes at all) and replaced by a read-only content fingerprint guard. **`mtime` is deliberately EXCLUDED** from that guard: the maintainer's live app on :3333 rewrites `.curator-config.json` during ordinary Settings use, and a multi-minute suite would then fail with a false "isolation is broken" — the same misattribution shape that cost two investigations in v3.0.16. Separately, **`npm test` itself was DELETING the real `.curator-config.json`** (`test-beta10`/`test-beta11`, to make `DOMAINS_PATH` win) and restoring it — a crash between the two cost the maintainer their API keys. Both migrated to `__setDomainsDirOverride()`, which outranks config with no need to touch the real file; verified by hashing all four credential files across a full `npm test`. In `test-beta11` the delete was entirely **vestigial** — all 12 tests exercise pure functions. **(9) Licensing (D7) — the `sharedbrain*` relicense, before GA rather than after.** Research finding: backlash comes from RETRACTING rights, not from restrictive licences (Grafana went more restrictive with no fork; Redis/HashiCorp/Cal.com retracted and were forked within weeks). Sole authorship re-verified today across all 9 brain modules, the route, and all 14 test suites. Ten files (9 × `src/brain/sharedbrain*.js` + `src/routes/sharedbrain.js`) move to the **Curator Enterprise License v1.0** in `LICENSES/`; everything else — including all test suites and the Shared Brain UI in `app.js` — stays MIT. `package.json` → `"MIT AND LicenseRef-Curator-Enterprise"` (`AND`, not `OR`; `OR` would let a recipient take the whole work under MIT). **Three decisions:** a **grace clause** (organizational production use is free, permanently for each Release, because no key mechanism exists and an org that WANTED to pay could not); an **FSL-style clause** converting each Release's covered files to MIT two years after publication; and scope limited to brain + route. **(10) The drafted licence text contradicted the drafted README, and four contradictions were found in audit.** §4.2's draft required a key while §4.3's draft README promised GitHub "free for everyone, forever" — resolved toward the strategy (`ENTERPRISE-TIER-PLAN.md` commits to it in three places) as a permanent, non-withdrawable §3.1 grant. Then: **§9 terminated "sections 2 and 3", silently revoking the §3.1 forever-promise on any unrelated breach** (carved out, together with so much of §2 as is needed to exercise it — carving §3.1 alone would have granted a right nobody could exercise); **§3.3 was self-terminating** ("UNTIL keys are available") fourteen lines above "nothing is ever withdrawn retroactively" (re-scoped per-Release); **§7 accepted contributions with no relicensing grant**, so one accepted community PR would contaminate the salable tier; and a **fourth instance** of the same permanence-vs-termination error inside the new §3.3 text. **(11) A leak the plan would have made permanent.** §3.1 as first drafted also granted the local-folder backend free forever — and `storage_type: 'local'` is fully production-reachable (`sharedbrain-config.js:138` validates it, the factory instantiates it), so an org pointing `local_storage_path` at a mounted NFS/SMB share would have obtained the Enterprise proposition (on-premise, no GitHub, org-controlled) irrevocably free. Narrowed to GitHub only; costs nothing today because §3.3 makes every backend free for this Release. **§1.6 now defines "the GitHub storage backend"** as the shipped adapter against public `api.github.com` — GitHub Enterprise Server and data-residency Enterprise Cloud are outside the permanent grant, which **describes the software rather than carving an exception**: `sharedbrain-github-adapter.js:65` hardcodes `GITHUB_API` with no configurable endpoint, so those deployments are unreachable today. §3.1's two operative paragraphs are byte-identical to before the narrowing — the promise reads absolute first, the boundary arrives after. **(12) Anti-open-washing.** README gains a prominent Licensing section (three touchpoints, leading with what is MIT and stating "nothing is being taken away from anyone"); the `License: MIT` badge linking to opensource.org was corrected to "MIT + source-available" (the composite work is not OSI-conformant — §4.2 is a field-of-use restriction, and a badge asserting otherwise is the open-washing signal the disclosure exists to prevent); new `TRADEMARK.md` (name/logo not licensed, forks need a different name, four permitted nominative uses, enforcement never entangled with code distribution — the WP Engine anti-pattern named explicitly). **Deferred deliberately:** removing `stable_facts` from the delta schema (generated, capped, stored in every payload, **read by nothing** — a real token saving, but it changes prompt content a second time in one release); a delta-prompt size cap; unifying the MCP read/write path split; and the Cloudflare R2 → S3-compatible rename, which was implemented, found to be **2 of ~12 references** across five files that no single agent owned, and **reverted byte-clean** — half-applied reads worse than either end state. **Verification: 33 offline suites green**; `test-sharedbrain-push.js` 83 → **110** assertions (real git fixtures, a **decoy page committed under a literal `domains/` directory** so a regression fails loudly with wrong content instead of quietly with null), `test-paths.js` +82, `test-sharedbrain-routes.js` 73 → **81**, `test-sharedbrain-llm-live.js` 33 → **49** live on both providers. Every new guard was proven by **reverting the fix and confirming the suite goes red**, then restoring. Secret scan: **0 findings in new content**. First git tag since `v3.0.0-beta.1`, so §5's two-year conversion clock has a verifiable anchor. |
| `v3.1.0` | **Track 1 Foundation — every user-data path now resolves through one module. Deliberately invisible to users; that is the point.** An installed macOS `.app` is **read-only** — writing inside it invalidates the code signature and macOS refuses to launch — but all six user-data locations currently live inside the repo folder. This routes them through a new [`src/brain/paths.js`](src/brain/paths.js) so they can later move to `~/Library/Application Support/` (NOT `~/Documents`/`~/Desktop`/`~/Downloads`, which are TCC-protected — and the MCP server is spawned by *Claude Desktop*, so a permission prompt would be attributed to Claude Desktop and may never render for a headless child). It ships **one full cycle before any packaging work**, specifically so it can be proven a no-op in the only mode that exists today. **(1) Detection is POSITIVE and fail-safe.** `isBundleInstall()` requires an explicit signal (a packager-written marker file, or an `<X>.app/Contents/` path shape); **everything else is repo mode — unknown layout ⇒ repo ⇒ data stays where it is.** The asymmetry is the whole design: wrongly deciding "bundle" is catastrophic AND silent (the user opens the app to an onboarding wizard, no domains, data intact but invisible — it presents as *"the update deleted my second brain"*), while wrongly deciding "repo" is a loud first-write failure on a read-only bundle with nothing lost. **(2) HIGH, caught in audit — the first two attempts at this were both wrong, in opposite directions.** v1 was `existsSync('.git')` alone: a ZIP download, a deleted `.git`, or a copy that dropped dotfiles silently relocated everything (reproduced). v2 added data markers `['.git','.curator-config.json','domains']` — but **`domains/.gitkeep` is tracked**, so every checkout AND every tree built by `git archive` contains `domains/`, which made bundle mode **unreachable by construction**: a packaged app would take the repo branch and write into itself. Its test encoded the false premise (a synthetic tree with no markers, which no real bundle can be) and passed. The inversion kills both. The suite now builds bundles the way we would actually build one (`git archive HEAD | tar -x`) and **pins the premise itself** — three assertions that a shipped tree DOES contain `domains/` and does NOT contain `.git`/`.curator-config.json` — so a stale assumption cannot hide again. **`scripts/build-app.sh` deliberately UNCHANGED**: today's `.app` is an AppleScript wrapper that runs the checkout, so it genuinely IS a repo install, and writing the marker now would flip every live user into bundle mode. **(3) Repo-mode equivalence is the claim this release stands on, and it was verified independently three times** — by reconstructing pre-change derivations from `git show HEAD:` / `git archive`, probing the ACTUAL call-path functions (not the new module's own exports, which would be tautological), and diffing resolved absolute strings: **all anchors byte-identical**, before and again after the detection core was rewritten. **(4) An anchor the plan missed:** `sharedbrain.js` derived the sync git dir as `<domainsDir>/..`, already wrong today for anyone with a custom `domainsPath`. **(5) `__setUserDataDirOverride` was import-order dependent** — `config.js`/`sync.js`/`sharedbrain-config.js` snapshotted paths at module load, so an override set *after* importing them silently no-opped while the suite (which imports nothing else) reported success. All three now resolve **per call**, with a source guard forbidding top-level `const X = <paths getter>()`. **(6) NEW SAFETY SEAM `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR`.** `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR` isolates only `domains/`, so **every test server run during development was holding the maintainer's REAL GitHub PAT** — one stray click on Sync would have pushed to their real repo (this nearly happened; an agent avoided those buttons on judgement, not by design). The new env is checked before repo/bundle detection and re-read per call so it crosses process boundaries, isolating the four credential locations unconditionally and `domains/` too **unless** `DOMAINS_PATH`/`--domains-path` is set.
