Destructive-merge safety gate: the Merge button stays disabled until the user opens the Preview diff modal (shows keep path, delete path, link-rewrite count, list of affected files, first 4 KB of merged content). Full guide: [docs/ai-health.md](docs/ai-health.md). |
| `v2.4.4`  | Phase 2 of AI Wiki Health — `✨ Ask AI` button on **orphan** rows. New export `suggestOrphanHomes(domain, issue)` in `src/brain/health-ai.js` returns up to 5 candidate pages that should link to the orphan, each with `{target, description, confidence, rationale}`. Summaries are intentionally excluded from the candidate inventory (wiki convention: summaries reference entities during ingest, not retroactively). New generic helper `injectRelatedLink(targetPath, linkSlug, description)` in `src/brain/files.js` — dedup-safe bullet injection, unlike `injectSingleBacklink` which hardcodes the `summaries/` prefix. New pseudo-fix-type `orphanLink` added to `AUTO_FIXABLE` — **never emitted by `scanWiki`**; exists only to route user-initiated AI Apply calls through the same `/api/health/:domain/fix` chokepoint. `fixOrphanLink(wikiDir, issue)` applies four defences before writing: slug-regex validation, orphan exists on disk, target exists in entities/ or concepts/ (never summaries/), and self-link rejection. `/api/health/:domain/ai-suggest` now type-dispatches: `brokenLinks` → flat `{target, rationale, confidence}`; `orphans` → `{candidates: [...]}`. UI reuses the Phase 1 disclosure modal (same data shape leaves the machine — no re-prompt). Full guide: [docs/ai-health.md](docs/ai-health.md). |
| `v2.4.3`  | Phase 1 of AI Wiki Health — `✨ Ask AI` button on review-only broken-link rows. New module `src/brain/health-ai.js` (export: `suggestBrokenLinkTarget(domain, issue)` — READ-ONLY; never writes). New endpoints: `GET /api/health/ai-available` (frontend probe for a configured API key) and `POST /api/health/:domain/ai-suggest {type, issue}` (Phase 1 supports only `type: 'brokenLinks'`). Prompt sends the full slug inventory (~15 KB on a 2000-page domain ≈ 3–4k tokens) plus a ~4 KB excerpt around the broken link; LLM returns `{target, rationale, confidence}`. Hallucinated slugs are coerced to `target: null` with confidence `low` — validated against the on-disk slug set before the UI sees it. Apply reuses the existing `POST /api/health/:domain/fix` endpoint with `issue.suggestedTarget` patched in — no new write code. One-time privacy disclosure modal (localStorage `curator-ai-health-disclosure-seen-v1`) describes what leaves the machine. Provider-agnostic via `generateText()`; automatically inherits the v2.4.0 fallback chain. Full guide: [docs/ai-health.md](docs/ai-health.md). |
| `v2.4.2`  | API-key UX: last-saved-wins + per-field Disconnect. Before: when both keys were stored, Gemini always won, and there was no way to clear a key from the UI (saving an empty value was silently ignored). Now: saving a non-empty key also marks that provider as active (`config.activeProvider = 'gemini'\|'anthropic'`), so pasting an Anthropic key and clicking Save instantly switches to Anthropic. A new **Disconnect** button next to each saved key clears just that one — backed by `POST /api/config/api-keys/disconnect {provider}` and `clearApiKey()` in `brain/config.js`; if the cleared key was active, active moves to the other provider (if it has a key) or becomes null. Legacy configs (no `activeProvider` field) fall back to Gemini-first priority, preserving behaviour for existing installs until the user explicitly saves a different key. `getProviderInfo()` in `llm.js` now honours `activeProvider` with a defensive fallback if the active provider's key is somehow missing. |

---

## Environment & Config

```
.curator-config.json    — UI-managed config (API keys, domains path) — never committed
  geminiApiKey          — Google Gemini key (set via Settings tab / onboarding wizard)
  anthropicApiKey       — Anthropic Claude key (set via Settings tab)
  domainsPath           — custom path for domains/ folder (set via UI)

.env                    — developer fallback for API keys (never committed)
  GEMINI_API_KEY        — Google Gemini (default, recommended)
  ANTHROPIC_API_KEY     — Anthropic Claude (alternative)
  LLM_MODEL            — optional model override
  DOMAINS_PATH         — optional custom path for domains/ folder

.sync-config.json       — GitHub sync credentials (never committed)
```

**Key priority:** `.curator-config.json` (Settings UI) takes precedence over `.env` for API keys.
**LLM selection:** dispatch is on `getActiveProvider()` — **last-saved-wins** since v2.4.2. Saving a key in Settings makes that provider active, so with both keys stored the active one is whichever was saved most recently (and the Settings provider toggle flips it without re-pasting). `getProviderInfo()` in `llm.js` honours it, falling back to whichever provider still has a usable key if the active one's key is missing. Gemini-first-if-both applies only to **legacy configs with no `activeProvider` field**.
**Default models:** pinned in `DEFAULTS` in [src/brain/llm.js](src/brain/llm.js) — currently `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` / `claude-haiku-4-5` (Haiku is the low-cost tier chosen to match Flash Lite's cost profile; opt into Sonnet with `LLM_MODEL` in `.env`).

---

## Scripts Reference

```bash
# Run the test suite (v3.0.1-beta.21+)
npm test            # OFFLINE suites only — fast, free, no network, no API key.
                    # The suite list is the OFFLINE array in scripts/run-tests.js
                    # — read it there; the count moves nearly every release.
                    # Safe to run anytime / in CI.
                    # Children are spawned with API/network credentials stripped,
                    # so a mis-classified suite can never make a paid call.
npm run test:live   # OFFLINE + LIVE suites. LIVE suites hit real
                    # Gemini/Anthropic/GitHub and need keys in .env; each one
                    # self-skips (exit 0) if its key is missing. Costs a few cents.
                    # Live suites get transient-error tolerance (v3.0.1-beta.26):
                    # a provider 503 / dropped stream is reported "inconclusive",
                    # not a failure. See ci-flake.js + CONTRIBUTING.md.
# Manifest of which suites are offline vs live lives in scripts/run-tests.js.

# Retroactive backlink injection (all domains)
node scripts/inject-summary-backlinks.js

# Single domain
node scripts/inject-summary-backlinks.js --domain=articles

# Dry run
node scripts/inject-summary-backlinks.js --dry-run

# ── The three DESTRUCTIVE wiki scripts share ONE argument contract (v3.6.1+) ──
# Name a domain, positionally or with --domain= (interchangeable; they must agree
# if both are given). --dry-run reports every change and writes nothing. --help
# exits 0. There is NO default domain and NO implicit all-domains mode: a bare
# invocation is REFUSED with usage text rather than guessing, because each of
# these rewrites and DELETES wiki files. An unrecognised -/-- token is a hard
# error, never silently skipped — silently skipping `--domain=X` is exactly how
# these scripts came to act on a domain the user never named (they fell back to
# a hardcoded `articles`, so `--domain=business` deleted files in `articles`).
# Domain names are validated as a single safe path segment.

# Deduplicate near-duplicate entity/concept files
node scripts/fix-wiki-duplicates.js articles
node scripts/fix-wiki-duplicates.js --domain=articles --dry-run

# Migrate non-canonical folders (people/, tools/) → entities/
node scripts/fix-wiki-structure.js articles
node scripts/fix-wiki-structure.js --all           # every domain — must be EXPLICIT
node scripts/fix-wiki-structure.js --all --dry-run

# Comprehensive wiki repair (cross-folder dedup, link normalization, backlinks)
node scripts/repair-wiki.js articles
node scripts/repair-wiki.js --domain=articles --dry-run

# Re-ingest all raw files in a domain (NOTE: positional only — this script does
# NOT accept --domain=, and costs real API spend)
node scripts/bulk-reingest.js articles
node scripts/bulk-reingest.js articles --delay=5000  # slower, for rate limits

# Rebuild The Curator.app (called automatically by update, or run manually)
bash scripts/build-app.sh
```

---

## Active Development Decisions

- **No vector DB / embeddings** — the decision stands, but **not** because the wiki fits in one context window; it stopped fitting long ago (the real `articles` wiki is ~4.4 MB against a 60 KB content budget). What replaced the "just send everything" assumption is **query-driven retrieval in `src/brain/chat.js`**: entity-pivot detection, keyword scoring, and a compact slug catalogue select the pages to load within `CONTENT_BUDGET_CHARS` (60 KB), `CATALOGUE_BUDGET_CHARS` (12 KB) and `MAX_PAGES_LOADED` (50). The reason to stay embedding-free is that the `[[wikilink]]` graph is already a hand-curated relevance signal, and markdown files stay human-readable and Obsidian-native — not that the corpus is small. (A blunt 90 KB `slice()` was the pre-beta.11 behaviour and was a genuine bug: on a mature domain the chat saw only `index.md` and `log.md`.)
- **No React/Vue** — seven-tab UI (Chat · Ingest · Wiki · Health · Domains · Sync · Settings) with vanilla JS. No build step.
- **JSON mode for ingest, text mode for chat** — ingest requires structured output; chat needs free prose.
- **Conversations gitignored from app repo but synced via knowledge repo** — personal to each user's machine, not committed to source control.
- **CLAUDE.md per domain** — each domain is a specialist, not a generalist. The schema shapes how the LLM categorises knowledge for that domain.
- **syncSummaryEntities is idempotent** — safe to run multiple times; injectBulletsIntoSection deduplicates by link target.
- **deduplicateBulletSections is always safe to run** — only removes bullets whose dedupKey already appeared earlier in the same section; never drops unique content.
- **API keys UI-first** — `.curator-config.json` (set via Settings tab / onboarding wizard) takes priority over `.env`. The `.env` file remains as a developer fallback.
- **install.sh auto-provisions** — detects and installs Node.js (via Homebrew or nodejs.org .pkg) and git (via Xcode CLI tools); no longer asks for API key during install (onboarding wizard handles it); auto-opens the app on completion.
- **No Stop button** — removed entirely because AppleScript's `on reopen` handler is broken on modern macOS and caused unrecoverable crashes. Closing the browser tab leaves the server running in the background (uses ~0 CPU). Clicking the Dock icon re-opens the browser if the server is running, or starts the server if it is not. To fully quit: right-click the Dock icon → Quit.
- **No /api/shutdown endpoint** — the server runs until the process is explicitly killed (Dock → Quit, or terminal Ctrl+C). No heartbeat or auto-shutdown.
- **Auto-update via Settings** — compares local `package.json` version with GitHub's `main` branch; runs `git fetch origin main` + `git reset --hard origin/main` + `npm install` + `bash scripts/build-app.sh` (rebuilds the .app); returns `{from, to}` short SHAs; frontend then calls `/api/restart` which spawns a new process and exits the old one. Browser auto-reloads. The hard-reset (instead of `git pull`) means the app directory is always forced to match `main` exactly — `package-lock.json` regenerated by local `npm install` runs no longer blocks the update.
- **Server auto-opens browser** — `exec('open http://localhost:3333')` runs on startup, unless `CURATOR_NO_OPEN=1` is set (used by the restart endpoint to prevent double browser tabs — the frontend reloads itself via polling).
- **Absolute node path in AppleScript** — `build-app.sh` and `install.sh` resolve the full path to `node` (via `which node`) at build time and embed it as `property nodeBin` in the AppleScript. This avoids the "node: No such file or directory" failure caused by AppleScript's `do shell script` running in a bare `/bin/sh` environment without the user's PATH. A `export PATH=...` with common node locations (`/usr/local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`) is also added as a fallback. If the user upgrades or moves Node.js, `bash scripts/build-app.sh` re-resolves the path.
- **Restart uses `process.execPath`** — the `/api/restart` endpoint uses the absolute path to the currently running Node binary (`process.execPath`) instead of bare `node`, ensuring the restarted server finds the same Node regardless of shell environment.
- **UI auto-refreshes after mutations** — after ingest, domain stats (page count, conversation count) update automatically; after sync down/both, domain dropdowns and stats also refresh; switching to the Domains or Wiki tab reloads their data. No manual browser reload needed.
- **Onboarding wizard** — **4**-step modal on first run (API keys → create domain → sync setup → connect Claude Desktop). **The trigger is CONFIG-ONLY:** `checkFirstRun()` ([app.js](src/public/app.js)) reads `hasGeminiKey || hasAnthropicKey` from `GET /api/config/api-keys`, which resolve through `getApiKeys()` — `.curator-config.json` **only, never `.env`**. So a developer running on `.env` keys alone gets the wizard on every load while the app can already make LLM calls, and because **step 1 has no Skip and the overlay has no Escape, no backdrop close and no X**, they are blocked until they paste a key into config. The only "don't show again" state is a key existing in config — which **any** Settings save sets, and a Settings **Disconnect** of the last key resurrects. Recorded as a known shipping defect; `/next`'s replacement is a non-blocking, dismissible panel (R7).
- **My Curator MCP (v2.3.0; read+write since v2.5.2)** — a local MCP server (`mcp/server.js`) that Claude Desktop spawns as a child process via stdio. Reads markdown directly from `getDomainsDir()`; does NOT require the Curator web server to be running. The authoritative tool list is the `tools` array in [`mcp/tools/index.js`](mcp/tools/index.js) — count it there rather than trusting a number in prose. As of v3.17.0 it holds **20** tools: **12 read** (list_domains, get_index, get_graph_overview, get_tags, search_wiki, search_cross_domain, get_node, get_connected_nodes, get_backlinks, get_summary, get_raw_source, get_working_state) and **8 grouped as write tools**, of which **5 actually mutate** (compile_to_wiki, fix_wiki_issue, dismiss_wiki_issue, undismiss_wiki_issue, save_working_state) and 3 are read-only inspections that happen to sit in that block (scan_wiki_health, scan_semantic_duplicates, get_health_dismissed). **Note the two valid splits, so they are never read as a contradiction:** this file counts by SOURCE GROUPING (12 read / 8 write-block), while user-facing copy — e.g. the `/next` MCP wizard — counts by CAPABILITY (**15 that read, 5 that write**), which is the more useful framing for someone deciding what to let Claude do. Same 20 tools, same 5 mutators. **Derive the mutator list from the `refuseIfReadonly` call sites across `mcp/tools/**`, never from this prose** — that call is what makes a tool a mutator, and v3.6.1 records a wrong count in a model-read doc actively changing Claude's behaviour. The graph tools are the reason MCP exists — they expose frontmatter, tags, [[wikilink]] edges (section-labeled), and bidirectional backlinks as structured data, so a frontier model can reason about topology, not just fetch pages. The generated `claude_desktop_config.json` entry uses absolute paths (`process.execPath` + `mcp/server.js` + `--domains-path <absolute>`); moving the domains folder makes it stale — the wizard detects staleness and shows a banner.
- **MCP wizard lives in Settings** — not a top-level tab. Section uses the sync-tab three-state pattern: **landing** (hero + what/privacy grid + "Set Up My Curator" CTA), **wizard** (3 numbered steps with progress pips: Copy snippet · Paste into config · Restart & verify), **connected** (status card + Self-test / View & Edit Config cards + runtime note). The wizard also joins the onboarding overlay as step 4 ("Connect to Claude Desktop", optional, skippable). Re-entering the Settings tab always refreshes the MCP status via `refreshMcpSection()` so stale UI can't persist after closing the wizard.
- **MCP response budget is in tokens, not bytes** — 1 MB of JSON ≈ 250 k tokens, which alone saturates Claude Opus's 200 k context window. `enforceSizeLimit()` in `mcp/tools/index.js` caps at **400 KB (~100 k tokens)** so a conversation can sustain multiple tool calls plus reasoning. The guard trims heavy arrays (edges → nodes → results → tags → backlinks → outgoing_from_start → backlinks_to_start) and appends `_truncated`. `get_graph_overview` default = compact summary (stats + top 20 hubs + orphan sample + top 10 tags, ~4 KB at any scale); `include_nodes: true` / `include_edges: true` are opt-in and size-guarded. `get_tags` default = top 50 tags with 50-page samples each. `get_connected_nodes` caps at `max_nodes: 60` (ranked by hop + degree), max depth 2, 120-char previews — enough to keep even hub-entity traversals under budget (e.g. `tali-rezun` at depth 2: 39 KB on the 2116-node articles domain).
- **MCP security** — defense in depth against LLM-driven path traversal. `storage/local.js` has a single `resolveInsideBase()` chokepoint that rejects absolute paths, `..` segments, and anything resolving outside the domains folder. Tools additionally validate their `domain`/`slug` args via `isValidDomain` / `isValidSlug` from `mcp/util.js` (strict alphanum+hyphen+underscore) for clean error messages. The `/api/mcp/reveal-config` endpoint uses `execFile` (not `exec`) so no shell interpretation. **The MCP is NOT read-only** (it was, up to v2.5.1; write tools landed in v2.5.2 — see the read+write-client bullet below). Five tools mutate user data — `compile_to_wiki`, `fix_wiki_issue`, `dismiss_wiki_issue`, `undismiss_wiki_issue`, `save_working_state` — and `fix_wiki_issue` can **delete** files (`crossFolderDupes` / `hyphenVariants` / `semanticDupe` all `rm()`). Each of those five, and only those five, calls `refuseIfReadonly()` before doing any work, so a Shared Brain `shared-*` mirror is refused. **Enumerate the call sites across `mcp/tools/**` rather than trusting this sentence** — it said "four ... and only those four" through the whole of v3.17.0, three bullets below its own correct count of five, and cited line numbers that had rotted by three. Any new mutating tool MUST add that call.
- **MCP graph cache** — `buildGraph()` caches per-domain with a 10-minute TTL and a file-count check for invalidation. An ingest that changes the file count forces a rebuild on the next tool call; otherwise graph re-use is safe for the life of one Claude Desktop conversation.
- **Model-lifecycle policy (v2.4.0)** — single chokepoint for all LLM calls is `generateText()` in `src/brain/llm.js`, which dispatches to whichever provider the user configured (Gemini or Anthropic). Default models are pinned in `DEFAULTS`; `FALLBACK_CHAINS` provides an ordered next-best list per provider. On model-not-found errors (and ONLY those — 429/503 still retry) the chain is walked in order; the successful model is recorded in a module-level `_activeFallback` and surfaced to the UI via `getFallbackStatus()`. When a provider retires a model: bump the `DEFAULTS` constant, optionally add the old primary to `FALLBACK_CHAINS`, push a release. Users on older versions keep working via the chain until they Check for Updates. Full user/developer guide: `docs/model-lifecycle.md`.
- **Self-test isolation** — `POST /api/mcp/self-test` spawns `mcp/server.js` locally, sends initialize + tools/list + list_domains over stdio, and reports round-trip results. If this passes but Claude Desktop still can't see the tool, the issue is in `claude_desktop_config.json`, not the bridge.
- **Semantic-duplicate scan is opt-in, cost-gated, and scale-capped (v2.4.5+)** — never runs as part of the regular Health scan. The flow is: estimate → confirm dialog → SSE-streamed real scan → per-pair Preview → Merge. Three hard caps in code: `SEMANTIC_DUPE_MAX_DOMAIN_PAGES = 20000` (refuses larger domains), user-configurable candidate-pair cap (default 500), user-configurable cost ceiling (default 50k tokens). **Batch merge is offered for HIGH-confidence pairs only** (`fixSemanticDuplicatesBatch`, `src/brain/health.js:1205`, added v3.0.1-beta.15): an explicit confirm step names how many pages will be deleted, merges run sequentially server-side (never in parallel — each merge rewrites links domain-wide), the route caps at 2,000 pairs, and — since every change lands in the git-tracked wiki — a user with Personal Sync configured can recover with a git client. **There is NO in-app revert: `src/routes/sync.js` has never exposed one**, and claiming otherwise on the panel that launches a page-DELETING operation was a false safety promise found at 14 sites and corrected in v3.9.1. **Medium- and low-confidence pairs are still one-at-a-time behind the mandatory Preview-diff gate** — they are the ones most likely to be genuinely distinct, and a wrong batch merge across thousands of pages is the most expensive mistake in The Curator.
- **Destructive-merge safety gate (v2.4.5+)** — Phase 3 is the first and only feature that DELETES files. The Merge button is disabled until the user opens the Preview-diff modal for that specific pair, which shows exactly which files will change, how many links will be rewritten, and what the merged content will look like. The frontend tracks previewed pairs per session; new sessions require re-preview.
- **AI Health is READ-ONLY (v2.4.3+)** — `src/brain/health-ai.js` proposes fixes, it never writes. All mutations still go through the existing `fixIssue()` in `src/brain/health.js` via `POST /api/health/:domain/fix`, so the AI layer cannot corrupt the wiki even if the LLM hallucinates. AI suggestions are additionally validated against the on-disk slug set before being returned to the UI; unknown slugs are coerced to `target: null`. This invariant must be preserved for Phase 2 (orphan rescue) and Phase 3 (semantic duplicates).
- **Conversation compile reuses the ingest write pipeline (v2.5.0+)** — `src/brain/compile.js` imports `writePage`, `syncSummaryEntities`, `appendLog` from `files.js` and runs the same Pass A/B/C link normalisation, cross-folder dedup, hyphen-variant resolution, and summary-backlink injection that ingest gets. Compile and ingest therefore share **one** page-composition write path, and the MCP `compile_to_wiki` tool (v2.5.2+) reuses the same module via direct import rather than re-implementing it. **Scope, stated precisely — this is NOT "one write path for the whole app":** `src/brain/health.js` does not import `writePage` at all. Every Health fix writes markdown directly via `writeFileAtomic` (`health.js:584, 595, 618, 652, 1167, 1171, 1178, 1300`) and deletes with `rm()` (`:619, 650, 1181`) — no frontmatter injection, no `mergeWikiPage`, no Pass A/B/C link normalisation. Health's own chokepoints are `fixIssue()` (dispatch) and `wikiFile()` → `resolveInsideWiki` (path construction, v3.2.0). **Treat `health.js` as a second, independently-guarded write surface when auditing:** v3.2.0's CRITICAL — a symlink escape that could DELETE a file outside the wiki — lived there precisely because it builds its own paths, and a doc claiming a single chokepoint is what stops an auditor looking.
- **Compile slug is deterministic and idempotent (v2.5.0+)** — summary path = `summaries/<slugify(title)>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<4hex(corpus)>.md`. Same conversation, same date, same content → same slug → file existence check refuses re-compile with a clear message ("Already compiled to X. Send another message to extend it, or delete that file to start over"). Adding a turn changes the corpus hash → new slug → no collision → compile proceeds normally. This file-existence-as-state design avoids any side state in the conversation JSON and carries cleanly into the v2.5.1 MCP tool.
- **Compile does NOT regenerate index.md via the LLM (v2.5.0+)** — on a 2000-page domain the index is 20 KB of markdown table; asking the LLM to rewrite it on every compile saturated the JSON output budget and broke parsing on the second click against `articles`. `mergeIntoIndex()` in `compile.js` reads the existing index, appends rows for any pages this compile actually CREATED (not updates), and writes back. Sanitised against pipe/newline injection in cell content. Pages are paired with their summary by post-write canonical path so cross-folder dedup redirects don't drop the description column.
- **`writePage()` returns structured change records (v2.5.0+)** — `{canonPath, status, bytesBefore, bytesAfter, sectionsChanged, bulletsAdded}` (or `null` on invalid input). Status is `created` / `updated` / `unchanged`. The `sectionsChanged` heuristic flags section names whose bullet count grew (good enough — byte-level `bytesBefore !== bytesAfter` plus the status flag still accurately tells the user "something changed"; deep diff is overkill). The shared `renderChangeRecords()` helper in `app.js` is used by both ingest and compile result panels — splits new/updated/unchanged with unchanged collapsed by default. Future MCP write tools (v2.5.1+) will return the same shape so Claude renders the same panel in chat.
- **Conversation IDs are UUIDs and validated at the route boundary (v2.5.0+)** — `src/routes/chat.js` and `src/routes/compile.js` reject any `conversationId` that doesn't match `/^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i` before reaching the filesystem layer. Defense in depth — `readConversation` would otherwise build `path.join(conversationsPath, '<id>.json')` from arbitrary client input.
- **Health dismissals are persistent and synced (v2.5.1+)** — `domains/<d>/wiki/.health-dismissed.jsonl` lives inside the wiki folder (already git-tracked), so dismissals propagate across machines via the existing GitHub sync without any sync-config tweaks. Format is one JSON object per line so concurrent dismissals on different machines append cleanly through git's standard 3-way merge. Order-insensitive identities (semantic-dupe pairs, hyphen-variant groups) are canonicalised on write — `{a, b}` and `{b, a}` produce the same `keyForIssue()` and one stored record. `loadDismissed()` runs a silent stale-record prune on every load (drops records whose referenced files/slugs no longer exist) so renames and merges don't pollute the file forever.
- **Health dismissal scope (v2.5.1+)** — Dismiss buttons appear ONLY on review-only Health rows (orphans, broken links without a suggested target) and on semantic-dupe pair cards. Auto-fixable issues (`brokenLinks` with `suggestedTarget`, `folderPrefixLinks`, `crossFolderDupes`, `hyphenVariants`, `missingBacklinks`) intentionally don't get a Dismiss button — the right action is Apply, not skip. If a user really wants to suppress one of those, they can dismiss it after the fact from the Dismissed section. This keeps the UI focused on "fix or skip" and prevents the auto-fix surface from becoming cluttered with skip-style affordances.
- **MCP is a full read+write client (v2.5.2+)** — `mcp/server.js` and its tool modules import the brain's `writePage`, `syncSummaryEntities`, `appendLog`, `scanWiki`, `fixIssue`, `addDismissal`, `removeDismissal`, `listDismissed` directly from `src/brain/*`. The MCP and the in-app UI are clients of the **same** brain functions — no HTTP delegation, no re-implementation in `mcp/`. Note this means *shared implementations*, not a single write function: page composition goes through `writePage`, Health fixes go through `fixIssue` (which writes directly — see the compile bullet above), and dismissals go through `health-dismissed.js`. The Curator app is the install + wizard + manual fallback UI; the MCP is the live conversational surface — they are equally-capable clients to the same data.
- **MCP write-tool boundaries (v2.5.2+)** — every MCP write tool funnels through `resolveDomainArg(args, storage, getDefaultDomain)` in `mcp/util.js`: explicit `domain` argument → user's configured `defaultDomain` → error. Domain validated via `isValidDomain` AND `storage.listDomains().includes()`. Per-tool guards layered on top: `compile_to_wiki` enforces 50 KB/page + 10 pages/call hard caps + `validateAdditionalPath` (folder allowlist, slug regex, `.md` suffix, `REFUSED_FILES`); `fix_wiki_issue` requires `type ∈ AUTO_FIXABLE`; `semanticDupe` requires `preview: true` first via a per-domain in-memory token Set in `mcp/tools/health.js`.
- **Path-traversal hardening in fixIssue handlers (v2.5.2+)** — Adding `fix_wiki_issue` to the MCP exposed `fixIssue()` to LLM-crafted input for the first time. New `resolveInsideWiki(wikiDir, candidate)` helper at the top of `src/brain/health.js` resolves the path and refuses anything outside `wikiDir` (rejects absolute paths, parent-traversal, empty input). All five fix handlers (`fixBrokenLink`, `fixFolderPrefixLink`, `fixCrossFolderDupe`, `fixHyphenVariant`, `fixMissingBacklink`) now route their issue-derived paths through this gate before reaching `writeFile` / `rm`. Defense in depth alongside the existing slug-regex validators in `fixOrphanLink` and `fixSemanticDuplicate`.
- **MCP audit log is local-only (v2.5.2+)** — `domains/<d>/.mcp-write-log.jsonl` records every MCP write (timestamp, tool, paths, byte count). Sibling to `wiki/`, NOT inside it, gitignored via the `*/.mcp-write-log.jsonl` rule added by `ensureDomainsGitignore()`. Write history is intentionally machine-private — you don't want it spilling to GitHub. Best-effort: a failed audit-log append never blocks the actual write.
- **MCP idempotency = file-existence on disk (v2.5.2+)** — `compile_to_wiki` re-uses the v2.5.0 file-existence guard inherited via shared module: same conversation+title+date → same hash → same slug → second call is refused. The summary file IS the state; no separate tracking. Concurrent calls within milliseconds can race the `existsSync` check, but real Claude Desktop usage is sequential. Documented as a known limitation rather than engineered around — adding a lockfile would complicate the standalone-MCP property without solving a real-world problem.
- **MCP discoverability is description-driven (v2.5.2+)** — MCP has no separate "keywords" field; tool selection is driven by tool names + descriptions. Each new write tool's description is rich plain-English with the natural phrases users actually use ("save to my second brain", "compile our findings", "check my wiki", "find broken links", "clean up the knowledge base"). Tool ordering also matters slightly — read tools registered before write tools so Claude reaches for read first when the intent is exploration.
- **`normalizePath` special-cases index.md and log.md (v2.5.2+)** — pre-existing latent bug: `normalizePath('index.md')` returned `'entities/'` (no filename) because the second branch treated `'index.md'` as an unknown-folder name. The basename guard then refused the write silently. This had been masking ingest's index updates since v2.0; only surfaced when v2.5.2's MCP write path (which calls `writePage(domain, 'index.md', mergedIndex)` directly) made the symptom impossible to ignore. Fix: explicit early-return for `index.md` and `log.md` at the top of `normalizePath`.
- **MCP stdout discipline (v2.5.3+)** — the rule is about the *reachable import graph*, not a fixed list of files: **any `src/brain/` module reachable from `mcp/` (directly or transitively) must never write to stdout.** The direct import surface is currently `config.js`, `files.js`, `health.js`, `health-ai.js`, `health-dismissed.js`, `llm.js`, `paths.js`, `write-registry.js` (derive it with `grep -rho "src/brain/[a-z-]*\.js" mcp/ | sort -u`), and each of those pulls in more — `files.js` alone reaches `ingest.js`, `atomic-write.js` and others. The MCP protocol reserves **stdout** for JSON-RPC frames; any `console.log` on that graph poisons the stream and surfaces in Claude Desktop as `Unexpected token … is not valid JSON`. Rule: ALL diagnostics in shared brain modules use `console.error` (stderr). Defensive comments at the entry point of each module make this explicit so future contributors don't reintroduce the bug. Verified by a stdout-purity probe (`/tmp/mcp-stdout-purity.mjs`-style) that asserts every line on stdout parses as JSON.
- **MCP `compile_to_wiki` link grounding (v2.5.5+)** — `mcp/tools/compile.js` runs a pre-write resolution pass over every `[[wikilink]]` in the inputs and resolves against the union of (existing slugs on disk + slugs being created in this call). Mirrors `writePage` Pass A/B/C exactly so writePage's own pass runs as a no-op on already-resolved content. The `broken_link_policy` parameter (`keep`/`strip`/`refuse`) gives users + Claude a strict mode for fresh domains where a single broken link is worth aborting the whole compile to fix at the source. The `links` response field is the actionable signal — Claude sees broken links immediately and can decide whether to retry with corrections. Without this v2.5.5 grounding, fresh domains reliably accumulated 20+ broken links per compile.
- **Domains are siloed by default (v2.5.6+ documented; behaviour is implicit since v2.0)** — `compile_to_wiki` writes to one domain per call, ingest's slug inventory is per-domain, link grounding (v2.5.5+) only resolves against the current domain. Cross-domain reasoning happens at the MCP-read layer (`search_cross_domain`) — a Claude conversation can synthesise across domains without persistent cross-domain links being created on disk. Obsidian, when its vault root covers multiple domains, may surface accidental edges where slugs collide across domains; Health is per-domain so those show as broken in the source domain even though Obsidian shows them connected. Documented in full at [`docs/domains.md` § 4](docs/domains.md#4-how-domains-relate-to-each-other). Intentional cross-domain linking (e.g. `[[business:openai]]` syntax) is not currently supported — would require parser/scanner work in `health.js`, `compile.js`, and the MCP tools.
- **My Curator Claude skill is the canonical playbook (v2.5.7+)** — `claude-skills/my-curator/` ships the same prompt-engineering rules that, used inconsistently, produce broken links / duplicate pages / cross-domain confusion. Standard install (drop in `~/.claude/skills/`) makes every Claude conversation that uses the my-curator MCP automatically follow them. The skill is the SECOND canonical source-of-truth for MCP behaviour rules (the FIRST is the tool descriptions in `mcp/tools/*.js` — read by Claude during `tools/list`). When the rules change, both must be updated. The skill body is intentionally a duplicate of the discipline already encoded in tool descriptions — but the descriptions are 1-paragraph triggers; the skill is the full playbook with examples. Skill format follows the Agent Skills open standard so it works in Claude Code, Claude Desktop projects, and any other MCP-aware host that supports the standard.
- **Skill description must avoid XML-shaped placeholders (v2.5.8+)** — Claude Desktop's skill-upload validator rejects any `<word>` pattern in the YAML `description` field as a malformed XML tag. Claude Code is more permissive, but the conservative path is: never use `<placeholder>` syntax in skill descriptions; use `[placeholder]` or concrete examples instead. The body of the skill is fine — the validator only checks the description.
- **Server binds to 127.0.0.1 only (v3.0.1-beta.20+)** — `app.listen(PORT, '127.0.0.1', ...)`. The Curator is a single-user localhost app; binding to all interfaces (the pre-beta.20 default) exposed unauthenticated endpoints to the LAN. A cross-origin guard middleware additionally rejects mutating requests (POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH) carrying a non-loopback `Origin` header (CSRF / DNS-rebinding defense); no-Origin requests (curl, scripts, the revoke flow) and all GETs pass through. The 50mb `express.json` body limit remains (Health batch plans) — that's safe precisely because the server is loopback-only.
- **Credential files are 0600 + atomic (v3.0.1-beta.20+)** — `.curator-config.json`, `.sync-config.json`, `.sharedbrain-config.json` all write through `writeFileAtomic`/`writeFileAtomicSync` with the new `{mode: 0o600}` options form (explicit `chmod` of the tempfile before the atomic rename, so umask can't loosen it). A startup sweep in `src/server.js` also hardens those plus `.env` and `.knowledge-git/config` (PAT-bearing) for existing installs. When adding any new file that holds a secret, write it with `mode: 0o600` and add it to the startup sweep list.
- **Test suite is run via `npm test` (v3.0.1-beta.21+)** — one aggregator ([scripts/run-tests.js](scripts/run-tests.js)) runs all battle-test suites. `npm test` = offline only (free, no network, credentials stripped from children); `npm run test:live` = + live API suites. When adding a new test script, add it to the OFFLINE or LIVE array in the runner. Tests that need to redirect the domains dir to a tempdir MUST use `__setDomainsDirOverride()` from `config.js` (checked before config) — NOT `process.env.DOMAINS_PATH`, which loses to a configured `domainsPath` and silently no-ops on real installs.
- **CI runs on GitHub Actions** ([.github/workflows/test.yml](.github/workflows/test.yml)) — two jobs. `offline`: every push + every PR (incl. forks); `npm ci` + `npm test`; free, no secrets. `live`: gated to push-`main` + manual `workflow_dispatch` only, `needs: offline`, uses `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` repo Secrets (optional `GITHUB_TEST_REPO`/`GITHUB_TEST_PAT`). The live job never runs on PRs, and GitHub also withholds secrets from fork PRs — so fork contributors can't reach the keys. CI is dev infrastructure only: no app code, schema, or version change. Setup/secret instructions live in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).
- **Live test domains-dir isolation (v3.0.1-beta.23 follow-up).** The live suites had the SAME domains-dir isolation bug the offline suites had pre-beta.21 — they isolated via `process.env.DOMAINS_PATH` (defeated by a configured `domainsPath`) or wrote throwaway domains straight into `getDomainsDir()`, so `npm run test:live` polluted the real `domains/` folder on a configured dev machine (verified: it created `domains/zztest-beta15-anthropic/`). **Fix:** new `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR` env seam in `getDomainsDir()` ([config.js](src/brain/config.js)) — checked BEFORE config (unlike `DOMAINS_PATH`), so it works on a configured machine AND across a spawned child process (the routes test's server). Test-only; unset in production. All live suites that create domains now set it to a tempdir. **Manifest split** in [run-tests.js](scripts/run-tests.js): `LIVE_CI` (self-contained, deterministic — beta8-live-llm, beta14, beta15/16/17-production) runs on CI + locally; `LIVE_LOCAL` (beta13-chat-live = real `articles` data + LLM-quality asserts; ingest-real-llm = a SPECIFIC personal article on a hardcoded local path; ingest-deep = quality thresholds; sharedbrain-github-live = needs `GITHUB_TEST_*`; sharedbrain-routes = spawns a server) runs only locally and is auto-excluded when `CI=true`. Per-suite timeout is tiered: offline 2 min, live 10 min (dual-provider ingests legitimately take minutes). **A first CI live run (run #2) surfaced two suites reading files absent on a clean checkout:** `beta15-production` read `domains/projects/raw/ingestion-pipeline.md` purely as a large multi-phase source → repointed to the committed `docs/ingestion-pipeline.md` (~35k chars; assertions are behaviour-based, so content-agnostic) → stays in CI; `ingest-real-llm` is tied to a specific personal article (asserts author "Dr. Alice Smith") via a hardcoded `/Users/...` path → moved to `LIVE_LOCAL`. The Anthropic key was NOT the problem — `test-beta14-anthropic-fix` passed on CI, confirming the secret works. Verified: a `CI=true` live run isolates cleanly (zero domains/ pollution).
- **Live-CI gate tolerates transient provider errors (v3.0.1-beta.26+)** — the live-API job hits real Gemini/Anthropic, so a provider 503 / dropped stream / rate-limit / network blip would otherwise red the build for reasons that are not code defects. [scripts/run-tests.js](scripts/run-tests.js) retries any failed LIVE suite **once**; if it still fails showing **only** transient markers (`scripts/ci-flake.js` `hasTransientMarker`), it's reported **inconclusive** (⚠ flake) and does NOT fail the build; a second failure with no transient marker is a genuine FAIL. Offline suites are deterministic and never retried. The `RUN_TESTS_LIVE_ONLY` env is a **test-only** seam (drives the runner against `scripts/test-fixtures/` fakes in `test-runner-integration.js`) — unset in normal runs and CI. When adding a new transient failure string the providers emit, add it to `TRANSIENT_MARKERS` in `ci-flake.js`. Trade-off: a real failure coinciding with a transient error in the same run is reported inconclusive — accepted because the offline suite + local `test:live` still catch real regressions.
- **System Check is read-only w.r.t. user data (v3.0.1-beta.23+)** — `src/brain/diagnostics.js` powers Settings → System Check (UI label "System Check"; internal element IDs/classes keep the `diag-*` prefix). `runQuickDiagnostics()` is free + local (no network, no API); its only write is a self-deleting temp-file probe to test that the domains folder is writable — it never reads or writes wiki content. `runLiveApiCheck()` is opt-in, makes ONE tiny LLM call (~$0.0001), is gated behind a frontend cost-confirm + the POST cross-origin guard, and never throws. Both dispatch through `getProviderInfo()`/`generateText()`, so the feature is provider-agnostic (verified on Gemini + Anthropic).
- **Compile has a fallback ladder like ingest (v3.0.1-beta.27+)** — `compileConversation` ([src/brain/compile.js](src/brain/compile.js)) tries `full → concise → summary-only`, escalating ONLY on `isOutputTokenLimit(err)` (exported from ingest.js) or a JSON parse failure; ANY other LLM error (503/429/auth/network) surfaces immediately (never masked, no wasted retries). `buildCompilePrompt` takes a `mode` param (`full` is byte-identical to the beta.25 prompt). On total overflow it returns a **compile-specific** error ("conversation too large… compile a shorter thread or split by topic"), NOT ingest's "split the PDF by chapter". Degraded outcomes surface via a `warnings[]` on the result (shown as a `.compile-note` in the result panel). `compileConversation(domain, id, onProgress, opts)` accepts `opts.generateText` — a **test-only** seam to inject a fake LLM (used by `test-beta27-compile-fallback.js`); defaults to the real `generateText`. The summary-only page flows through the unchanged `writePage → syncSummaryEntities → mergeIntoIndex` pipeline.
- **Versioning policy (v3.0.2+, user decision 2026-07-02)** — plain semver `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, **no pre-release suffixes**. The old `3.0.1-beta.N` line shipped 27 consecutive "previews" straight to production via the auto-updater, so the `-beta` tag carried no meaning and confused users; it is retired. Each release bumps PATCH (Shared Brain hardening: Phase 2 → 3.0.3, Phase 3 → 3.0.4); a feature milestone like Shared Brain GA bumps MINOR (→ 3.1.0). Feature-level beta status is expressed in the UI (e.g. the Shared Brain "beta" pill), independent of the app version. New test suites are named by feature (`test-sharedbrain-hardening.js`), not by release codename.
- **Shared Brain hardening invariants (v3.0.2+)** — synthesis treats stored contribution payloads as a TRUST BOUNDARY (filter non-string facts; per-page try/catch — one bad payload must degrade, never abort); Shared Brain push/pull/synthesize/revoke MUST register with the write-registry (pull also takes the mirror's `.write-lock`); the `shared-*` domain namespace is reserved for read-only mirrors (refused as contributing domains, refused by all in-app write surfaces via `isDomainReadonly`); transient LLM errors (503/429/network, `isTransientLlmError` in sharedbrain.js) never count toward the permanent-skip strike counter; the Host-header guard in server.js applies to ALL requests (DNS-rebinding defense) — keep loopback forms in `ALLOWED_HOSTS` if the bind address ever changes. The Shared Brain enable toggle lives in Settings → "Shared Brain (beta)"; the Sync tab hosts only the operational section. v3.0.3 additions: synthesis consumes submissions ONLY after all their pages write successfully (watermark + processed_ids in state.last-synthesis — never compare wall clocks across machines); all fellow-supplied strings pass sanitizeFellowText/isSafeLinkSlug before composition and identity comes from the storage path; mirror pulls use writePage's `{replace:true}` + prune (never union-merge into a mirror); GitHub tree truncation must THROW (never silently miss files — Article 17 depends on it); an active `state/revocation-in-progress` marker blocks ordinary synthesis and only a fully-successful revoke clears it.
- **Shared Brain UI/UX invariants (v3.0.4+)** — the frontend Shared Brain card layer must go through the per-connection in-flight registry (`_sbInFlight`/`_sbLastResult` in app.js): status/busy writes resolve the CURRENT card in the DOM (never a captured node), one operation per connection, Disconnect blocked mid-op, final summaries restored on re-render. `read_only: true` connections are Pull-only end-to-end (wizard → card → push/synthesize 400s → `pending_pages` 0) — keep all four layers in sync when touching any of them. `GET /api/sharedbrain/list` must stay cheap (mtime scans only — never LLM/network) because the navbar badge polls it. Adapter operational warnings go through the `onWarn` channel (throwing callbacks must never break the operation). Wizard panel side effects (link population) belong in `sbWizardGoToStep` (on ENTRY), not in the buttons that leave a panel.
- **Shared Brain admin-credential invariants (v3.0.5+)** — the `sbat_…` admin token is shown ONCE (wizard step 2 or the rotate response) and never again: listings mask it, `patchSharedBrain` refuses it, and only `saveSharedBrain`/the rotate endpoint may write it. The revoke UI must keep the API contract intact (client expands the typed `REVOKE-<short-id>` to the full-UUID literal — never prefill the confirmation). Member identity in `listMembers`/the revoke picker is storage-path-derived, NEVER the payload's `fellow_id` (same rule as synthesis). The card's shown-once token display deliberately skips the post-op list refresh — don't "fix" that by adding one. `data_handling_terms` is persisted on connections so invite re-display reproduces the original deterministic token; treat its absence (pre-v3.0.5) as contributor_retains WITH a user-visible caution.
- **Shared Brain adapter-consistency invariants (v3.0.6+)** — GitHub's contents/tree APIs are only EVENTUALLY consistent (~1-2s): never read-after-write against them on a correctness path. The revoke rebuild takes its baseline via `runLocalSynthesis(…, {stateOverride})`, NOT by re-reading the meta it just wrote; the adapter's SHA-retry default must stay ≥3 with backoff (a single immediate retry loses the create-race — guarded in test-sharedbrain-github-offline). Live tests that read back a fresh commit must poll briefly. Concurrent same-file writes are last-writer-wins by design but must never throw or corrupt (verified live in github-live §6b).
- **Chat/query truncation is graceful; the MAX_TOKENS message is context-neutral (v3.0.7+)** — the output-token-limit guard in `callProvider` ([llm.js](src/brain/llm.js)) is a SHARED chokepoint for every LLM call, so its error must NOT carry feature-specific advice. All MAX_TOKENS handling routes through the exported `handleOutputTokenLimit(providerName, maxTokens, responseFormat, partialText)`: **JSON mode throws** (message must keep the literal phrase "output token limit" so `isOutputTokenLimit` — which ingest/compile fallback ladders key on — keeps matching; no ingest vocabulary like "split the source"/"Phase 2 batch size"), **text mode returns the partial answer + a truncation note** (never throw — a cut-off chat/query answer is still useful). Chat + query request 8192 output tokens (not 4096). Callers wanting feature-specific guidance (ingest, compile) add it at their own level, never in the shared guard.
- **Chat intent routing classifies the ASK, has three intents, and list/count COMMANDS beat decision words (v3.0.8+)** — `detectQueryIntent` in [chat.js](src/brain/chat.js) first calls `extractAsk(message)` to isolate the user's actual ask (interrogative sentences → else command-opener sentences → else the whole text) so list-like or decision-like words buried in PASTED CONTENT (a topic idea containing "everything", a pasted "List: A, B, C" line) can't hijack the routing. It then returns `decision | enumerate | synthesis` via an ORDERED router over that ask: (1) a NARROW analytical flag (disagreement words + whole-word `differ` only — never bare "most/least", which appear in benign list phrasing), (2) strong list/count command anchors (`list`/`how many`/`count`/`what|which <plural-noun>`/`all|every <noun>`) anchored to a clause boundary `(?:^|[.?!]\s+|\n)\s*` so they win over incidental decision words and work after a preamble, (3) decision cues, (4) weak list phrases. Order matters — do not move decision above the strong anchors (reopens the "content word hijacks intent" bug), and keep `extractAsk`'s questions-first precedence (a real trailing question must win over a pasted command line). The bare word "everything" must NOT be an enumerate trigger. A superlative/disagreement question shaped like a list request diverts to synthesis. Known accepted trade-off: a decision question that opens with a plural-noun enumerate anchor ("which papers should I read first?") routes to enumerate (a capped, deduped list — a reasonable answer, not a dump). All three `buildPrompt` blocks forbid reproducing the catalogue; `stripCatalogueEcho` is a last-resort net (strips runs of 5+ bare `folder/slug.md` paths separated only by spaces/tabs — never commas/newlines, so legit lists/code/multi-path citations survive; no global whitespace collapse). Tier 2 (a Concise/Balanced/Comprehensive response-style selector) is planned as a later release.
- **Chat response style is orthogonal to intent (v3.0.9+)** — `RESPONSE_STYLES` in [chat.js](src/brain/chat.js) (`concise`/`balanced`/`comprehensive`) controls answer DETAIL/LENGTH; `detectQueryIntent` controls answer SHAPE. The style directive is appended AFTER the intent block and MUST NOT relax the anti-catalogue-dump guardrails (comprehensive still forbids reproducing the catalogue; `stripCatalogueEcho` + the enumerate ~40-item cap still apply). `normalizeResponseStyle` MUST use an own-property check (`Object.hasOwn`) — a truthiness guard lets inherited keys (`__proto__`/`constructor`) through to an undefined cap/directive. `balanced` has an empty directive so the 5-arg `buildPrompt` stays byte-identical to the legacy 4-arg call. The UI selector (Chat tab, label "Detailed" = comprehensive) persists in localStorage and sends `responseStyle` per message; the server normalises any value so a bad/absent one is always safe. Every cap stays within both providers' output limits and chat is text mode (graceful truncation), so a larger cap never hard-fails.
- **Chat answers render Markdown via an XSS-safe renderer (v3.0.10+)** — [src/public/markdown.js](src/public/markdown.js) (`window.renderChatMarkdown`, loaded as a classic script before the `app.js` module; called from `appendMessage` for ASSISTANT bubbles only) turns Markdown into styled HTML. Cardinal rule: **escape the whole string FIRST, then insert only a fixed allow-list of tags** — never interpolate user text into an attribute or URL, and emit no `href`/`src`. Inline code is isolated by a STRUCTURAL split on `` `…` `` (not a text placeholder — a placeholder sentinel is forgeable). It's testable in Node via `new Function('window', src)` (see [test-chat-markdown.js](scripts/test-chat-markdown.js)); any change to it MUST keep the escape-first invariant and re-run that suite. The response-style directives must keep `concise < balanced < comprehensive` monotonic (balanced is soft-moderate/"not exhaustive"; comprehensive is "your longest answer" but still forbids catalogue dumps) — the live suite asserts this ordering on both providers.
- **The per-chat model selector is CONFIG-scoped, not `.env` (v3.0.13+)** — both the chat Model dropdown's availability (`hasGeminiKey`/`hasAnthropicKey`, config-only) and the backend override gate (`normalizeChatProvider` → `getApiKeys`, config-only) key off SAVED SETTINGS keys, NEVER `getEffectiveKey`/`.env`. A provider Disconnected in Settings must vanish from the chat selector AND be refused as an override (falling back to global), even if its key lingers in `.env`. Do NOT reintroduce `getEffectiveKey`/`geminiUsable`/`anthropicUsable` into the selector path — that was the v3.0.11 bug (a Disconnected-but-.env key stayed usable in chat). The `.env` fallback still legitimately drives the GLOBAL provider (`getProviderInfo`/`getEffectiveKey`) for the documented dev use; only the per-chat selector is config-scoped. The selector re-inits on Chat-tab entry AND at the end of `loadApiKeyStatus`.
- **Chat has a per-chat model (provider) selector (v3.0.11+)** — a user with BOTH keys can pick Gemini or Claude for one chat without changing the global Settings provider. It picks the PROVIDER only; the model id is always `DEFAULTS[provider]` (`getDefaultModel` in [llm.js](src/brain/llm.js)) surfaced via `/api/config/api-keys` `models`, so bumping `DEFAULTS` auto-updates the UI label with NO frontend change. Override plumbing: `getProviderInfo(preferProvider)` → `callLLM(…, providerOverride)` → `generateText(…, {provider})`; `normalizeChatProvider` in [chat.js](src/brain/chat.js) is the trust gate (only a keyed `gemini`/`anthropic` survives — a client value never reaches the SDK as a model, only a validated provider name). The frontend Model dropdown shows only when ≥2 providers are USABLE (`geminiUsable`/`anthropicUsable` via `getEffectiveKey` = config OR .env) and re-inits on Chat-tab entry (idempotent — `wireDropdown` guards double-binding; one module-level outside-click/Escape handler). The composer is one unified `.chat-composer` with Length + Model as upward-opening dropdowns.
- **Theme has no `--text-dim` var (v3.0.12 note)** — the CSS custom properties for text are `--text` (#cdd6f4, primary), `--text-2` (#a6adc8, secondary/dim), `--text-muted` (#6c7086, faint), `--text-faint`. `var(--text-dim)` is INVALID and silently falls back to the element's inherited/UA colour (black on a `<button>`). Use `--text-2` for "dim but readable" text. When styling a menu/option built from `<button>`s, set an explicit `color` on the button so children don't inherit the UA default black.
- **The Compile-to-Wiki outcome is a thread item, never a fixed panel (v3.0.14+)** — it renders as `.chat-compile-card` appended into `#chat-thread` via the single guarded renderer `renderCompileOutcome()` in [app.js](src/public/app.js). Invariants: (1) the card must never carry its own `max-height`, `overflow`, or `flex-shrink` — `max-height` re-creates the "second window" squeeze directly, and ANY `overflow` re-creates it indirectly (a flex item whose overflow isn't `visible` has its automatic `min-height` resolve to 0, so the thread can shrink it); horizontal containment belongs on the inner `.chat-compile-card .change-summary { overflow-x: auto }` block. (2) Scroll the card's TOP into view (`scrollCardIntoView`), never the thread's bottom — otherwise a 20+-page card's title and ✨/✏️ counts are scrolled off. (3) A compile runs 15–45s with the UI live, so the handler captures `activeConvId` + `chatDomain` at click time and the renderer refuses to append when either changed (misfiling the card into another transcript, or floating it over a freshly-emptied thread); `appendCompileCard` must NOT touch `chatEmptyEl`/`chatThreadEl` visibility. Guarded by [test-chat-compile-card.js](scripts/test-chat-compile-card.js). The **ingest** tab keeps its own `#ingest-result.result` panel; `renderChangeRecords(container, {title, changes})` stays the shared contract.
- **Chat conversations are intentionally synced (not a bug)** — `domains/.gitignore` is generated from `DOMAINS_GITIGNORE_RULES` in [src/brain/sync.js](src/brain/sync.js) (read the array, don't trust a count in prose — it has grown four times). It currently excludes machine-local and junk paths only: `*/raw/`, `*/.mcp-write-log.jsonl`, `*/.write-lock` (v3.0.15), `.DS_Store` (v3.0.16, deliberately un-prefixed so it matches at any depth), `.ingest-queue/` (v3.3.0, purely defensive), and `**/.obsidian/workspace.json`, `*.base`, `Untitled.md`, `Untitled 1.md` (v3.5.1 — the `**/` on the workspace rule is load-bearing per `gitignore(5)`). `conversations/*.json` is on none of those lists, so it IS tracked by the knowledge repo and travels with the wiki (documented in [sync.md](docs/sync.md): "so you can continue threads on any machine"). Consequence: sending one chat message adds exactly one pending change, so the Sync badge legitimately ticks to 1 after a chat with no ingest or compile. Community-reported as suspicious in v3.0.14 and deliberately left as-is. (CLAUDE.md's directory tree labels `conversations/` "gitignored" — that is true of the APP repo only.)
- **All user-data paths resolve through `src/brain/paths.js`; bundle detection is POSITIVE and fail-safe (v3.1.0+)** — an installed `.app` is read-only, so writing inside it breaks the code signature. `isBundleInstall()` requires an EXPLICIT signal (marker file or `<X>.app/Contents/` path shape); **everything else is repo mode — an unrecognised layout must ⇒ repo ⇒ data stays put.** Never invert this to "infer repo from markers, default to bundle": wrongly deciding "bundle" is catastrophic AND silent (empty wiki, data invisible), while wrongly deciding "repo" fails loudly on first write with nothing lost. Two earlier attempts were both wrong — `existsSync('.git')` alone (a ZIP download relocated everything) and data markers including `domains` (which `domains/.gitkeep` puts in EVERY shipped tree, making bundle mode unreachable). Any new user-data path MUST route through `paths.js` — there is exactly one root derivation in `src/`+`mcp/` and a source guard enforces per-call resolution (no top-level `const X = <getter>()`, which made an override silently import-order dependent). `scripts/build-app.sh` must NOT write the bundle marker while the `.app` remains an AppleScript wrapper around the checkout.
- **If a test spawns a server, isolate with `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR`, not `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR` (v3.1.0+)** — the latter isolates ONLY `domains/`, so a test server holds the developer's real `.sync-config.json` and GitHub PAT, and one stray Sync click pushes to their real repo. The new env is read before repo/bundle detection and re-read per call, so it crosses process boundaries; it isolates all four credential locations unconditionally, and `domains/` too UNLESS `DOMAINS_PATH`/`--domains-path` is set. (`scripts/test-sharedbrain-routes.js` still needs migrating.)
- **Give a clever test an independent dumb cross-check (v3.1.0+)** — `test-frontend-null-safety.js` was silently blind TWICE while reporting every assertion green: its lexer desynced on a nested template literal and on a regex containing backticks (seeing 78 of 90 declarations), and the rewrite then shipped a dead regex-vs-division heuristic that reopened the class. A parity tripwire could not catch it (two desyncs cancel). What works is a second, deliberately-dumb measurement compared for exact SET equality — it fires even on shapes the sophisticated path still misses. Same lesson as v3.0.15's CSS-token baseline and v3.0.17's source-regex assertion: a passing test that measures the wrong thing is worse than no test.
- **Assert behaviour, not the presence of a line of source (v3.0.17+)** — `test-ingest-prompt-slimming.js` asserted the CALL SITE of the Phase 1 usage report with a source regex and labelled it "the pages-vs-tokens datapoint sizing needs". The value it reported was ALWAYS wrong on the retry path (a dead `if (outline)` conditional inside `if (!outline)`), and the source assertion gave positive assurance the whole time. A test that proves a line exists proves nothing about what it does. `ingestMultiPhase` now takes a trailing defaulted `llm` param — a TEST-ONLY seam (same pattern and rationale as `compile.js`'s `opts.generateText`, null in production) that drives the real Phase 1 retry ladder and Phase 2 fallback ladder against a fake LLM, offline and free. Building that seam is what surfaced the lenient-`parseJSON` bug below; prefer it over source regexes for anything orchestration-shaped.
- **Apply a fix to the path that runs FIRST, not just the path you were looking at (v3.0.17+)** — v3.0.16's `extractPageArray` closed a silent-drop hole on the single-page FALLBACK while the batch path, which runs first for every batch of every multi-phase ingest, kept the original bug: `{"pages": []}` is truthy, so a whole batch's pages vanished with no stub, no warning, and a green result panel; `{"pages": {}}` threw `TypeError` and killed the ingest. When fixing a response-shape bug, enumerate EVERY call site that consumes that shape before declaring it closed.
- **`parseJSON` is lenient and its output must be validated, not merely truth-tested (v3.0.17+)** — it falls back to jsonrepair, which turns the bare text `not json at all` into the truthy STRING `"not json at all"`. An `if (!parsed)` check accepted that as a successful outline, skipped the retry, and `validateOutline` degraded it to a summary-only plan — an 80,000-char source could produce a ONE-PAGE wiki. Any `parseJSON` result that is supposed to carry a page list must go through `usablePageArray`/`extractPageArray`, never a truthiness check.
- **Repeated warnings aggregate at emission, and two classes deliberately do not (v3.0.17+)** — `aggregateWarnings()` collapses same-class entries at ONE chokepoint before the log entry is built, so `log.md`, the SSE payload and the result panel cannot disagree. Threshold is 3 (1–2 occurrences keep the specific path, which is the useful thing; 3 is the same "pattern, not incident" line the trunk-page detector uses), examples capped at 3 with "…and N more". A real run went 75 → 11. **Semantic near-duplicates and trunk-page injections are excluded on purpose** — each names a specific pair or cluster the user must decide about; collapsing them trades signal for tidiness. When adding a warning that can fire per-page or per-batch, add it to the aggregator; when adding one that names a decision, do not.
- **The batch prompt's stable prefix is load-bearing — never let it vary per call (v3.0.16+)** — `buildBatchPromptParts` returns `{prefix, suffix}` and the ENTIRE cost win of v3.0.16 depends on `prefix` being byte-identical across every batch of one ingest. Anything per-call in it (a timestamp, a counter, Set/Map iteration order, or moving the page list back out of `suffix`) silently turns every cache READ into a cache WRITE at 1.25×, making ingest **more expensive** — with no error, no warning, and no offline test able to see it. The only proof is a live multi-batch run showing non-zero `cachedReadTokens` (Anthropic) — verified at write=41,822 / read=250,932 = 56.6% saving. Anthropic caching is gated BOTH by a size floor (`ANTHROPIC_CACHE_MIN_PREFIX_CHARS`, because Haiku 4.5 silently declines to cache under ~4,096 tokens) and by `totalBatches >= 2` at the caller (break-even is two calls); single-pass must never get a breakpoint.
- **The broken-wikilink rate is not a valid single-run gate (v3.0.16+)** — measured on UNMODIFIED v3.0.15 against an md5-frozen source and a fixed articles-scale domain, it ranges **Gemini 0.0–3.8%** and **Anthropic 2.4–6.1%**. That noise is wider than any effect a prompt change is likely to have, so an A/B at n=1–3 will confidently report an improvement or a regression that does not exist (this happened twice in the v3.0.16 work, in both directions). Use paired runs with several replicates per arm, compare against the paired baseline rather than the fixed 1–5% band, and — where the question allows — prefer **byte-identity of the generated prompt** over a statistical argument: it is deterministic and settles in seconds what 14 live ingests could not.
- **Ingest warnings are the contract for anything the pipeline silently changed (v3.0.16+)** — `writePage`'s `opts.onWarn` and `reconcileGeneratedPages` exist because two failure modes were losing user-paid-for content with only a `console.warn`: a page path missing `.md` was discarded outright, and a Phase 2 response returning a non-canonical `summaries/` path created a SECOND summary, permanently breaking that source's re-ingest idempotency. Rule: if the pipeline drops, redirects, renames, or flattens something the model produced, it MUST push a user-visible entry into `warnings[]` — a stderr line is not a user surface. `validateOutline`'s guarantees bind the OUTLINE only; anything Phase 2 returns must be re-validated against the paths that batch actually requested.
- **The Gemini fallback chain must escalate FORWARD in time, and every rung must be priced (v3.0.15+)** — `FALLBACK_CHAINS.gemini` exists to keep users working when the pinned default is retired, so it must list *newer* models, never older ones. The pre-v3.0.15 chain pointed at `gemini-1.5-flash*`, both of which were already **404** — the safety net had one working rung. When adding a rung: probe it live with the app's real call shape (JSON mode + `maxOutputTokens: 65536`), add its price to `MODEL_PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` (an offline invariant in `test-chat-model.js` fails `npm test` otherwise), and never describe a rung as "same cost tier" without checking — a family-name heuristic is structurally wrong because the family word is stable across generations while the price is not (`2.5-flash-lite` → `3.1-flash-lite` is 2.5× in / 3.75× out; `haiku-4-5` → `3-5-haiku` is genuinely *cheaper*). `DEFAULTS.gemini` stays on the cheapest working model; the chain is insurance, not a migration.
- **The boot guard lives in `index.html`, not `app.js` (v3.0.15+)** — a handler inside `app.js` cannot catch `app.js` failing to evaluate, which is the exact case (~79 top-level `getElementById` consts; one missing element blanks the page for every user via the auto-updater). Only the `window.__curatorBooted` sentinel, set on `app.js`'s last line and checked at `DOMContentLoaded`, is authoritative — do NOT add filename-based resource-error heuristics, because the SPA fallback serves `index.html` with a **200** for any missing path, so a missing script throws a `SyntaxError` rather than a resource error. A missing `markdown.js` is NOT fatal (`app.js` has a designed escaped-text fallback) and must never trigger the panel.
- **CSS custom properties fail silently — `npm test` guards them (v3.0.15+)** — an undefined `var(--x)` makes the declaration invalid at computed-value time (inherited properties fall back to `inherit`, so it often looks *almost* right). [test-css-tokens.js](scripts/test-css-tokens.js) fails on any new undefined reference and carries named regression guards for `--font-mono` and `--text-1`. Three fallback-carrying names (`--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--surface-1`) are deliberately baselined — re-pointing them at real tokens would change rendered colours, which belongs with the design-system work. Never add a variable to the baseline to make the suite pass.
- **`push()`, `pull()` and `setup()` must all guard their commit (v3.0.15+)** — any `git commit` that can be reached with a clean index must be wrapped in `if (!err.message.includes('nothing to commit')) throw err`. `friendlyError` maps that message to `null`, so an unguarded throw surfaces as an **empty** error the user cannot act on or recover from. `untrackStaleWriteLocks()` made this reachable in `push()` by staging a deletion that `git add -A` then cancels.
- **A prior version is content or `null` — never `''` (v3.1.1+).** `loadPriorContent`'s pathspec is **work-tree-relative** (`<domain>/wiki/<path>`) because Personal Sync passes `--work-tree=getDomainsDir()`; a `domains/` prefix matches 0 of ~5,200 tracked files and silently disables every delta. Beyond that, `isNew` is a `=== null` identity check while `buildDeltaPrompt` branches on **truthiness**, so an empty-string prior makes the prompt self-contradictory ("IS NEW PAGE: false" + the new-page block) and the model can route real content into `stable_facts`, **which nothing reads** — permanent loss. 0-byte pages are ordinary input (Obsidian creates them on "new note" and the vault root IS `domains/<d>/wiki/`), so `if (!content) return null` is load-bearing, not defensive. Whitespace-only is deliberately NOT caught — it is truthy, so both consumers already agree. Do not over-credit `--literal-pathspecs`: it has been over-claimed twice and now guards no observable output difference.
- **Pages that never reached the collective must never be diffed (v3.1.1+).** A page in `pending_retry`/`permanent_skip` failed its last push, so none of its body was contributed; diffing it against the pre-failure git version drops everything that was never sent. Freeze the membership set off `connection` BEFORE the un-skip loop mutates the working copies. Redundant full contributions are absorbed by exact-string dedup; a gap is unrecoverable — always prefer the redundancy.
- **`getProviderInfo` FALLS THROUGH — never infer the provider from the label (v3.1.1+).** With no key for the requested provider it silently returns whichever provider still has one. Any test that redirects user-data paths also redirects `.curator-config.json` (where the Gemini key lives; it is not in `.env`), so a "per-provider" suite can run one model twice and pass. Seed the isolated config AND assert the resolved provider. Byte-identical output from two different models is the tell.
- **`npm test` must never touch a real credential file (v3.1.1+).** Suites needing a different domains dir use `__setDomainsDirOverride()` (in-process, domains-only) or `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR` (anything spawning a server) — never by deleting the real config so `DOMAINS_PATH` can win. Fingerprint guards compare **sha256 + size + existence only**: `mtime` makes them fail when the maintainer's live app rewrites config during an ordinary Settings action, producing a false "isolation is broken" — the misattribution shape that cost two investigations in v3.0.16.
- **Batch ingest queue invariants (v3.3.0+)** — the queue directory resolves through `getIngestQueueDir()` in `paths.js` and **must never live inside `getDomainsDir()`** (that is Personal Sync's git work-tree; staged sources would be committed and pushed). Execution is **strictly sequential and process-wide**: the worker/create claims are taken **synchronously**, with no `await` between check and set, and a second independent in-flight counter asserts "at most one `ingestFile` ever" around the call itself — do not "fix" a concurrency report by adding another check after an `await`, that narrows the window rather than closing it. **Duplicates are decided once, at create time, and the worker performs no duplicate check** — `ingestFile` writes `raw/<name>` first, so a runtime check would mark a crash-resumed item `skipped` and silently drop it. A **transient** provider error (structured `err.curatorTransient` tag from `llm.js`, never a bare `/\b429\b/` — that also matches ingest's own `"yielded only 429 characters"`) pauses the whole batch; anything else fails one item; and a **consecutive-failure circuit breaker** bounds the harm regardless of classification. A recovered job comes back **`paused`, never running** — nothing may auto-start spend. `toWire()` is an explicit **allow-list** with length caps and path scrubbing; never reintroduce a `...rest` spread. A `budgetUsd` that cannot be priced is **refused (400)**, never silently accepted — and the cap guarantees spend is *tracked and bounded*, not that it is never exceeded. The estimate quotes a multiple **computed for the batch in front of it**; the fresh-vs-mature ratio is size-dependent (**39.4× at 2 KB → 2.1× at 80 KB**, with a step at ~15,000 chars where a source crosses to multi-phase) so **never hard-code a single multiplier** — a test asserts the multiple must move with document size.
- **Memory-layer (working state) invariants (v3.17.0+, hardened v3.17.1–.2)** — **state SUPERSEDES; knowledge ACCUMULATES**, and that is why there are two stores rather than one. The wiki's merge unions bullets, so a blocker resolved on Tuesday would be resurrected by Wednesday's write; a union merge cannot express *no longer true*. **`state/` therefore never goes through `writePage`** — and could not, even if wanted: `normalizePath` redirects every non-canonical folder and FLATTENS to the basename, so `sessions/projA/main/state.md` and `sessions/projB/feature-x/state.md` BOTH land on `entities/state.md`, the second silently overwriting the first. `(project, scope)` is inexpressible through that path. **The `<machine>` segment is load-bearing and must not be collapsed or 'simplified':** Personal Sync resolves with `git pull --no-rebase -X theirs`, which on a conflicting hunk silently discards the local side and reports success, so per-machine paths mean no hunk ever conflicts. It is `<hostname-slug>-<install-id>`, never a bare hostname — two clones sharing a default macOS name destroyed a handoff and its journal. **That argument covers tiers 2 and 3 ONLY:** `state/project.md` has no machine segment, so two machines editing the standing brief DO produce the conflicting hunk — the one place the guarantee does not hold, and `docs/sync.md` carves it out. **Single writer:** agents write over MCP; the app is READ-ONLY over state, deliberately — a browser write path would make the app a SECOND writer and break the property the whole sync-safety argument rests on, and would stamp a human edit with the last agent's provenance. **The standing brief is human-authored** and there is deliberately no brief-writing MCP tool. **Capture is skill-instructed and ADVISORY** (`claude-skills/curator-continuity/`): nothing forces a save, and a missed one yields the PREVIOUS state, never a corrupted one — the fail-safe direction, and the reason no enforcement was added. Saving OVERWRITES and is therefore idempotent, so the instruction is **save early and often**, and **every save must be COMPLETE, not a delta**, or the third save silently drops the firm decisions recorded in the first. **An over-budget save is TRIMMED and disclosed in `notes`, never refused** — an agent near its context limit whose handoff is rejected loses it entirely. **Counts are taken BEFORE the index slice:** `distinctScopeCount` counts distinct scopes over the UNCAPPED pair list, because deriving it from the capped array reports a CAP as a measurement. **`scopeCount` is a LEGACY name meaning two different quantities** — distinct scopes on `GET /api/memory`, the (scope, machine) pair total on `GET /api/memory/:project` — both pinned by different suites, so neither was redefined; read `distinctScopeCount` and `savedCopies`, which mean one thing each everywhere. **`resolveInsideState` is the sole path chokepoint.** Sanitisation runs on READ as well as write, and defang **never deletes** — a legitimate handoff routinely carries URLs and commands. **The dominant defect class here is a consumer silently dropping a field the store honestly computed** (`unlistedEntries`/`unlistedReason`, `requestedMachine`, `machineIsThisHost`, `installIdAvailable`): guarded BEHAVIOURALLY by `scripts/test-working-state-disclosure.js`, never by a name scan — `res.json({ ...state })` and `out.current = state.current` forward fields wholesale, so a text search reports drops that are not real and misses real ones.
- **Version:** 3.24.0
## Active workstream — Shared Brain production hardening (2026-07-02 →)

A four-track evaluation (backend, routes/security, UI/UX, test coverage) of the
Shared Brain feature found 1 critical + ~10 high-severity issues and major
production-test gaps. **The full findings register + 5-phase upgrade plan live
in [SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md](SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md)** — a gitignored PRIVATE
working doc (never commit/push it). Any session continuing this work MUST read
that file first; it carries per-item status markers and all decisions
(including the §5 decision to move the Shared Brain enable toggle to Settings).
Phase mapping (versioning policy: plain semver, no -beta suffixes): Phase 1
(surgical bug fixes) → v3.0.2 ✅, Phase 2 (data-integrity + trust boundary) →
v3.0.3 ✅, Phase 3 (UI/UX) → v3.0.4 ✅, Phase 4 (admin features) → v3.0.5 ✅,
Phase 5 (production test program + beta pilot) gates GA (v3.1.0). Working agreements:
never break existing features (the app is in production; all offline suites
green before every commit), and technical + user docs are updated in the same
release as every behaviour change.

## Roadmap — Chat UI Modes 3 & 4 (planned, not yet implemented)

> This section is the **persistent design context** for a future session that
> will implement Modes 3 (Dictate) and 4 (Curate) of the Chat tab. It captures
> what we've decided so the implementing session can start coding without
> re-deriving the architecture. Update this section as decisions firm up.

### The four-mode model

The Chat tab is evolving from a single-purpose Q&A surface into a four-mode
authoring environment that mirrors the natural lifecycle of working with a
second brain:

| Mode | Status | What it does | Direction |
|------|--------|--------------|-----------|
| 1. **Discover** | ✅ shipped (v1.0) | Multi-turn Q&A against the wiki — answers cite specific pages. | read |
| 2. **Compile** | ✅ shipped (v2.5.0) | After a Discover conversation, click "Compile to Wiki" to turn it into a permanent summary + new entity/concept pages. | read → write |
| 3. **Dictate** | 🔮 planned | Stream-of-thought capture. User types or speaks raw thoughts; the chat UI structures them and writes to the wiki immediately without requiring a multi-turn conversation. | write-first |
| 4. **Curate** | 🔮 planned | Review-and-refine an existing wiki page or batch of pages in the chat UI. Conversational editorial dialogue (different from Health, which is rule-based). | rewrite |

The progression reads coherently: **Discover (read) → Compile (write a summary
of a thought process) → Dictate (capture raw thought directly) → Curate (refine
what's already in the wiki).**

### Mode 3 — Dictate

**Use case.** *"I just had an idea I want to capture quickly. I don't want a
conversation, I just want to dump what I'm thinking and have it land in the
wiki properly structured."*

Compile (Mode 2) requires a multi-turn conversation first. Dictate is for the
single-shot case — paste a paragraph, click Save, done.

**Proposed UI.**
- Mode-switcher in the chat-tab header (segmented control: `Discover · Compile · Dictate · Curate`).
- In Dictate mode, the chat input becomes a "Dictate" box with a **Save to wiki** button instead of Send.
- Optional **target page hint** (entity / concept / summary auto-detected from content, user can override).
- After save: the existing v2.5.0 change-record panel renders the result.

**Backend reuse.**
- `compileConversation()` is too multi-turn-shaped — Dictate gets its own thin wrapper:
  `dictateNote(domain, { text, kind?, hint? })` → builds a one-shot LLM prompt that asks for *one* page (entity / concept / summary) with proper structure, then runs through `writePage` + `syncSummaryEntities` + index merge — same chokepoint.
